When today's weekday is disallowed by `allowed_days`, the default becomes the **first allowed future day within the horizon**; when today is allowed, it stays today (byte-identical); when **no** allowed day fits the horizon (e.g. window=2, next allowed is +3d), it falls back to today via `??`. Load-bearing detail: **ordering** — `resetMonthCursor()` **must run first**, because `availableDates()`' windowed branch reads the typed-no-default `public int $currentYear; public int $currentMonth;` (`new Jalalian($this->currentYear, $this->currentMonth, 1)`). The old order (date-first) accessed `availableDates` before the cursor was set → `Error: Typed property must not be accessed before initialization`, swallowed by `availableDates`' `catch(Throwable)` → returned `[]` → fallback today → the feature was silently **inert** for windowed tabs. Correct order: `resetMonthCursor() → smart date → syncDefaultTimes()`. `availableDates` never reads `$this->date` (no circular dependency); `#[Computed]` access in `mount()` is fine because Livewire hydrates `#[Url] activeTab` **before** `mount()` runs; `switchTab`→`resetFilters` unsets `availableDates`/`dateWindow`/`allowedDays`. `goToday()` is intentionally **not** changed — its `now()->toDateString()` is correct because "today" is by definition allowed on that path.
- **Dedupe A — `allowedHoursBounds()`** — `availableTimeSlots()` and `syncDefaultTimes()` both parsed `allowed_hours['start'??'08:00']` / `['end'??'20:00']` with the same `if ($end <= $start)` guard. Extracted to `private function allowedHoursBounds(?array $allowed): array` returning `[$startCarbon, $endCarbon]`; both callers do `[$start, $end] = $this->allowedHoursBounds($policies['allowed_hours'] ?? null)`. Verified byte-identical across 6 `allowed_hours` shapes (null, normal, wrapped, missing start, missing end, equal). Zero logic change.
- **Dedupe B — `forUser` scope alignment** — `activeLimitUsage()` and `cancelLimitUsage()` both wrote `Reservation::where('user_id', auth()->id())`. Switched both to `Reservation::forUser(auth()->id())` (the existing `scopeForUser` is exactly `where('user_id', $userId)` with an `int` typehint). Identical query.
- **Dedupe C — `resetMonthCursor()`** — `mount()`, `goToday()`, and `resetFilters()` each inlined `Jalalian::now()` → `getYear()`/`getMonth()`. Extracted to `private function resetMonthCursor(): void`; called in each, preserving the exact original order. `Jalalian::now()` reads the real clock each call, so extracting changes nothing about timing.

A standalone diff harness (replicated `availableDates` exactly, ran old-vs-new `mount` across 7 scenarios) confirmed: today-allowed → identical (A/C/E); today-disallowed + next-in-horizon → NEW jumps to that future day (B/D/F); today-disallowed + none-in-horizon → NEW falls back to today == OLD (G). 3 changed, 4 identical.

## Resource-type kill switch — reuse `window_days`/`window_hours`, faded tab, `skip_admin: false`

No new field/enum/section to turn a reservation resource type (seat/spot/car/meeting) off. Admin sets both **`window_days = 0` AND `window_hours = 0`** on that type's policy → `ValidationService::isTypeActive(string $resourceType): bool` (`getPolicies()`-cached) returns `false`. Rides the EAV `ReservationPolicy` plumbing for free — `EditPolicy::fillForm/handleRecordUpdate` already flatten/unflatten arbitrary keys, so zero admin-page code changed beyond the field's `minValue(0)` (was `1`) and a hint update.

- **Validator pattern — pure static predicate, reused by the service (not the reverse)**: `app/Services/Reservation/Validators/TypeActive.php` stays a stateless `BookingRule` but exposes `public static function isTypeActive(array $policies): bool` (`(int)(window_days ?? 1) !== 0 || (int)(window_hours ?? 1) !== 0`); `validate()` calls its own static and throws `ReservationError::TypeInactive` (`ERR-022`) on `false`. `ValidationService::isTypeActive(string $resourceType)` delegates to that static (`TypeActive::isTypeActive($this->getPolicies($resourceType))`) — one source of truth, dependency direction service → validator, never validator → service via injection. Registered `TypeActive::class => ['skip_admin' => false]` — `skip_admin: false` makes this block **everywhere including admin edits/creates/series-generation**, unlike most rules admins can bypass.
- **Tab bar stays visible but faded — never hidden**: `Main::render()` keeps the existing `userCanBook()` permission filter (zero-permission types still fully removed) but no longer filters on type-activation; instead maps `'disabled' => !isTypeActive($tab['id'])` onto every permitted tab. The shared `<x-ui.buttons.tab-selector>` (9 consumers) grew an optional `disabled`/`disabledReason` key (default `false` — every other consumer unaffected): when true, the button gets native `disabled` (kills `wire:click` server-side, no JS), `opacity-40 grayscale-[35%] cursor-not-allowed hover:bg-transparent`, a lock glyph, and a `title` tooltip — matching the existing disabled-state vocabulary. `ensurePermittedTab()`/`switchTab()` also gate on `isTypeActive()` server-side, so a disabled type can never become `activeTab` via URL tampering.
- **Same fade/lock ported to the hamburger quick-menu (client-side)**: reservation quick-links in `resources/js/components/alpine/data/menu.js` carry a `resourceType` tag; `init()` maps `disabled: disabledTypes.includes(item.resourceType)` onto each item (options from `disabledReservationTypes()`, cached via `ValidationService::disabledTypes()` — 1 DB-cache read instead of 4 `getPolicies()` calls) rather than filtering out. `resources/views/components/dashboard/modal/menu.blade.php` mirrors the tab-selector's vocabulary and `handleItemClick()` no-ops on disabled. **Pitfall already hit once**: `@json()` only escapes `'` (`JSON_HEX_APOS`), never the literal `"` a JSON array-of-strings needs as its own delimiter (`["car"]`), so `@json(array)` inside a **double-quoted** HTML attribute silently truncates at the first `"` and corrupts the tag. `x-data="menu({ ..., disabledReservationTypes: @json(...) })"` broke this way and killed the entire hamburger menu (Alpine never parsed `x-data`) with no server-side error. Fix: the attribute is **single-quoted** (`x-data='menu({ ... })'`) — safe because `@json()` guarantees no literal `'`. Any future `@json()` whose value can be a string/array must go inside a single-quoted attribute; regression-covered by `NavbarTest::test_hamburger_menu_x_data_attribute_is_well_formed_html`.
- **`disabledReservationTypes()` — cache the derived list itself, don't refetch per type**: originally looped `ResourceType::cases()` calling `isTypeActive()` per type (4 `getPolicies()`/`Cache::remember()` calls every navbar render). `ValidationService::disabledTypes()` now caches the derived array directly (`Cache::remember('reservation_disabled_types', 3600, ...)`, same TTL as `getPolicies()`) — 1 query per call. `flushPolicyCache()` forgets both the per-type key and this aggregate key. Regression-covered by `ReservationTest::test_disabled_types_helper_issues_single_cache_lookup_when_warm` and `test_disabled_types_helper_reflects_policy_changes_via_cache_flush`.

## Card meta-row pattern (report + gallery)

The report **grid card** (`tab/reports/cards.blade.php`) is the canonical split: a `flex flex-col justify-between` info panel holding a title+description block, then a meta row separated by a divider. Meta row = `flex justify-between items-center pt-3 border-t border-[var(--md-sys-color-outline-variant)]/20 mt-3` — the `border-t` **is the divider** — with a **plain** date `<span dir="rtl" class="text-xs text-[var(--md-sys-color-on-surface-variant)] opacity-70">` (no chip) on the start side and the action button (download) on the end. Description = `text-[var(--md-sys-color-on-surface-variant)] text-xs line-clamp-2 leading-relaxed font-light opacity-80` with `Str::limit(strip_tags(...), 100)`. The report **list card** (`tab/reports/list.blade.php`) is the horizontal variant: `[thumb] | [title → description → meta(chip + date)] | [download]`, meta row `flex items-center gap-3 text-xs text-[var(--md-sys-color-outline)]` with a chip (`bg-[var(--md-sys-color-surface-container-high)] px-2 py-0.5 rounded`) + `dir="rtl"` date, no divider. The gallery card (`tab/gallery/item.blade.php`) **adapts** the grid card: the date stays in the **header** under the title (`text-[10px] font-medium text-[var(--md-sys-color-on-surface-variant)]`), and only the description moves below a divider at the bottom — `pt-3 border-t border-[var(--md-sys-color-outline-variant)]/20` then the same description classes. The description+divider block is conditional on `!empty($photo->description)` (no dangling divider); there is no meta-row date and no download button (gallery opens in Fancybox). Reuse the grid-card divider + description classes; only the date placement differs.

The gallery card header icon is **not** decorative — `GalleryPresenter::scopeMeta(Photo)` (`app/Livewire/Dashboard/Tab/Presentation`) classifies the photo's department scope and returns `['icon','label']`, mirroring admin `GalleryTablePresenter::department()` three-way rule: `count($photo->all_departments) > 1` → multi (`groups`, `resources/gallery/strings.filters.multiple_departments`), `=== 1` → single (`lock`, `...filters.single_department`), `=== 0` → public (`public`, `...fields.public_gallery`). Uses the `all_departments` accessor (department_id + departments JSON merged/deduped) rather than the literal "department_id filled / departments > 1" rule (which leaves gaps). `Gallery::render()` passes `['presenter' => new GalleryPresenter()]`; gallery → timeline → item are `@include`s, so `$presenter` reaches `item.blade.php` with no plumbing, and the icon box reads `$scope = $presenter?->scopeMeta($photo)` (null-safe with a `photo_library` fallback). The icon box carries `cursor-help` + `title="{{ $scope['label'] }}"`. Rule: when a user-panel card icon encodes a classification the admin already computes, put the logic in a tab Presenter that mirrors the admin presenter.

## Gallery mixed-media render — image/video conditional, no schema change

The `photos.path` JSON column holds an **array of stored file paths** (cast `array`); NOT image-only by shape, only by the admin form's old validation. Video support was added with **zero migration, zero model change, zero new component** — the single lever is a per-path extension branch in `tab/gallery/item.blade.php`. A `path` array may freely mix image and video strings; classification is by extension at render time, never a separate column.

**The conditional is a pure `if/else` inside the existing `@foreach`, image branch kept byte-for-byte identical to the pre-video markup** — same `<a data-fancybox="gallery-{{ $id }}">` wrapper, same `<img class="w-full h-full object-cover" loading="lazy">`, same collage transforms (`z-20 rotate-6 group-hover:*`). Images render EXACTLY as before; the video branch fires `in_array(strtolower(pathinfo(parse_url($url, PHP_URL_PATH), PATHINFO_EXTENSION)), ['mp4','webm','mov'], true)` and emits its own scaffold. No shared wrapper around both branches — image-only galleries are visually unchanged.

**Video tile scaffold** (inside the same collage `<a>` so transforms/stacking apply): a `bg-black` relative container holding `<video preload="metadata" muted playsinline src="{{ $url }}#t=0.1" class="w-full h-full object-cover">` as the cover frame, then overlays — a `from-black/35 via-transparent to-black/55` gradient (so a black/fade first frame reads as cinematic letterboxing), a centered primary play-circle badge (`group-hover:scale-110`), a top-start `VIDEO` chip, and a bottom-end duration badge bound `x-text="duration ? formatDuration(duration) : ''"` from `@loadedmetadata="duration = $el.duration"` (free with `preload="metadata"`, no extra network). The `#t=0.1` fragment forces the browser to decode the frame at 0.1s instead of frame 0 — avoids the black/fade-in first frame that makes plain `preload="metadata"` look broken.

**Fancybox v6 integration is unchanged JS**: `gallery.js` binds `[data-fancybox]` globally and doesn't care what's inside the anchor. Image anchors stay `data-fancybox="gallery-{{ $id }}" href="{{ url }}"`. Video anchors add `data-type="html5video"` + `href` to the `.mp4` → Fancybox v6's video plugin (`@fancyapps/ui` carousel.video) ONLY accepts the literal type `"html5video"` — NOT `"video"` — and renders an HTML5 `<video>` player (verified in the bundled dist: `if(!l||"html5video"===l){ ... match /\.(mp4|mov|ogv|webm)/ ... }`). A `data-type="video"` value is truthy-but-unrecognized, skips the extension auto-detect, and produces a slide no plugin handles (broken lightbox) — the value MUST be exactly `html5video`. The hidden-tile loop gets the same `data-type="html5video"` on video paths. Grouping still works — clicking any tile opens the whole row intermixed. **No `gallery.js` change required for playback.**

**Hover living-preview** is the only additive JS — two isolated methods on the `gallery()` Alpine data: `galleryPreviewPlay(el)` (guarded `if (!el || el.tagName !== 'VIDEO') return`; `el.currentTime = 0; el.play().catch(()=>{})`; a 1500ms `setTimeout` auto-pause) and `galleryPreviewStop(el)` (clears the timer, `el.pause(); el.currentTime = 0.1`). Wired from the video container `@mouseenter="galleryPreviewPlay($refs.vid)" / @mouseleave="galleryPreviewStop($refs.vid)"`. Also `formatDuration(seconds)` → `m:ss`. All three are additive and gated to `<video>` only — cannot affect image tiles, scroll/observer logic, or Fancybox. Do NOT wire hover-preview to image tiles.

**Admin form** (`GalleryFormPresenter::path()`): the gate was `->image()` (forces image-only) + a 4-mime `acceptedFileTypes` + `maxSize(5120)`. Video support = drop `->image()`, add `video/mp4,video/webm,video/quicktime` to `acceptedFileTypes`, bump `maxSize(51200)`. Keep `multiple/maxFiles(50)/directory('gallery')/panelLayout('grid')/reorderable/previewable/downloadable/openable` and the `Str::random(12).'-'.time().'.'.extension` storage rename. Filament's FileUpload grid doesn't render video thumbnails natively — accepted trade-off; the user-panel collage still shows the first-frame cover.

**Admin table/infolist video fallback**: `ImageColumn`/`ImageEntry` render nothing for a video path. `GalleryTablePresenter::preview()` `getStateUsing` returns `null` when the first path is a video. `GalleryInfolistPresenter::photos()` `getStateUsing` filters the path array to images only, and a sibling `GalleryInfolistPresenter::videos()` `TextEntry` renders the video paths as playable HTML5 `<video controls preload="metadata" src="…#t=0.1">` tags via `->html()` + `->getStateUsing` (returns the imploded `<video>` markup), `->visible()`-gated so the entry hides on image-only records. The `->html()` is safe because `path` is admin-stored, not user input, and the URL is `e()`-escaped into `src`. `photosCount()` still counts all items. Rule: when an infolist must render a media type `ImageEntry` cannot (video/audio), use a `TextEntry->html()` sibling that builds the tag from the same `path` array, `->visible()`-gated — do NOT overload `ImageEntry` or invent a view file.

**Module naming — admin/config vs user-panel**: admin panel label and modules config use the explicit «گالری تصاویر/ویدیو» (admin `label`/`plural_label` in `lang/fa/resources/gallery/strings.php`, `config/modules.php` `title`). User-panel page title stays the short «گالری» (`gallery.blade.php` `<x-ui.title title="گالری" countLabel="مورد">`), with media-agnostic empty states ("گالری هنوز خالی است" / "موردی در این ماه یافت نشد") and form `section_photos` («محتوای گالری»). The `photos` field label is «محتوا» (not «تصاویر») since the array mixes image + video. Code identifiers stay `Photo` model / `photos` table / `GalleryResource` — migration-bearing renames, NOT the minimal lever; only user-facing strings change. The only legitimate residual «تصاویر» in gallery strings is `hints.path` («تصاویر (JPG…) و ویدیو (MP4…)»), which names file formats, not the module.

## Reports dual load-more pattern (one canonical infinite-scroll idiom)

**Superseded 2026-08-24** — Reports now uses one canonical load-more shape everywhere, per `resources/views/components/ui/loaders/loadingPattern.md` category 1: both the list view and the card/timeline view use an explicit `<x-ui.buttons.load-more action="loadMore">` button. The card view's old `IntersectionObserver` scroll-trigger sentinel (`report.js` `setupIntersectionObserver`) and its `<x-ui.loaders.spinner/>` marker were removed — same click-based mechanism as every other paginated list (Feeds and Gallery's timeline views got the identical conversion). `spinner.blade.php` itself is now fully retired app-wide.

Because the sentinel's `wire:key` changes each load (count-based), the observer MUST be re-bound via `Livewire.hook('morph', ({component, el}) => { if (component.id === this.$wire.__instance.id) this.$nextTick(() => { if (this.$refs.loadTriggerCard) this.setupIntersectionObserver(); }); })` — `setupIntersectionObserver` disconnects the prior observer before re-creating. Guard `if (!this.$refs.loadTriggerCard) return` so the list view (no sentinel) does nothing. This matches `gallery.js` verbatim. Pattern: one canonical infinite-scroll idiom (sentinel + IntersectionObserver + morph re-bind), reused per module — do not invent a second one.

## Presenter extraction — additions + do-not-over-split rule

This session added four Presenter methods, all following the Step-4 "Data-bag Presenter" shape (bundling multiple derived values a Blade region needs in one call):
- `FaqPresenter::badge()` + `questionText()` — badge derivation + question display text.
- `GalleryPresenter::months(Collection):Collection` — `filter → map → unique('key') → sortByDesc('sort') → values()`. The filter→map→unique pipeline belongs in the Presenter, not the blade.
- `FeedPresenter::categoryLabels():array` — static map of category → label.

Rule: only extract presentation logic that bundles **multiple derived values** a Blade region needs. Do NOT create a Presenter for raw model attributes or single computed properties — those stay as model accessors or inline `@php`. The Data-bag Presenter replaces several per-row `@php` blocks with one call; if a candidate method only returns one scalar, it does not justify a Presenter.

**By-reference loop-state counters.** When a per-item `@php` block inside a `@foreach` mutates a counter that survives across iterations (e.g. Links `$hk` hotkey index, Feeds `$imgIndex` image-index), the relocated data-bag method takes that counter **by reference** (`launchLinkData(Link $l, string $kind, string $base, int &$hk)` / `mediaCellData(string $url, int &$imgIndex)`) and both reads and increments it — so the Blade keeps `$hk = 0;`/`$imgIndex = 0;` init before the loop and the method call replaces the inline `$hk++`/`$imgIndex++`. The unpack stays `@php $d = $presenter->launchLinkData($link, 'external', '', $hk); $hotkey = $d['hotkey']; ... @endphp`. **Test gotcha:** you cannot pass an inline assignment expression by reference — `$p->launchLinkData($link, 'external', '', $hk = 0)` throws `cannot be passed by reference`; assign `$hk = 0;` on its own line first, then pass `$hk`.

**Enum-badge + conditional sub-element in one method.** When a per-row `@php` derives an enum-backed badge (`ReleaseRequestType::from`/`ReleaseRequestStatus::from` → icon/color/label) AND a separately-gated sub-element (the response box, rendered under `@if(filled($item->response))`) whose color/bg/icon depend on the same status, fold both into one `statusMeta(string $status): array` that returns the badge keys PLUS `responseColor`/`responseBg`/`responseIcon` for **every** status — not only the "rejected" branch. The blade's `@if(filled($item->response))` guard fires for any status, so omitting the response keys on a non-rejected status yields an undefined-index warning inside the guard. Lock it with a test that iterates `ReleaseRequestStatus::cases()` asserting `responseColor`/`responseBg`/`responseIcon` exist on each, plus explicit rejected→error / resolved→primary value checks.

**Bake derived view-model into the existing shaped-array method.** When the view-model to fold reads source keys the presenter's existing shape method (`ContactPresenter::contact()`) doesn't yet expose, prefer baking the derived values INTO that method (`'occasion_tone' => $c['occasion'] ? Profile::occasionTone($c['occasion']) : null`, `'org_title' => collect([$c['unit'] ?? null, $c['section'] ?? null])->filter()->implode(' › ')`) over a new standalone method — the blade already iterates the shaped array, so it reads `$contact['occasion_tone']['chip']` / `$contact['org_title']` with no extra call. **Latent-drop watch:** `Main::contacts()` was fetching `unit`/`section` but `contact()` was dropping them, so the blade's `$contact['unit']` was undefined and the position-row tooltip was always empty — folding surfaced this. When folding reveals a presenter silently drops a source key the blade reads, surface it to the user (fix-while-folding vs. preserve-empty) rather than silently locking either behavior.

**Per-row card data-bag, unpacked into flat locals (TaskBoard).** `taskboard/card.blade.php` is rendered per task via `@include` from both `taskboard/column.blade.php` (`isPersonalBoard => true`) and `tasking/board-column.blade.php` (`isPersonalBoard => false`), so its whole top `@php` block folds into one `TaskBoardPresenter::cardMeta(array $task, bool $isPersonalBoard, array $departmentOptions): array` call bundling ~19 derived values (urgency, checklist total/done, attachments count, state chip, deadline title, `hasDeadline`, `isPending`/`isUrgent`, the Alpine `isFavoriteExpression`/`showTaskExpression` strings, `canCyclePriority`, `labelFilterMethod`, `departmentLabel`, `collaboratorUsers`, `colState`, `responsibleUser`). The body already reads these as flat local names across 100+ references, so the blade keeps those names — `$card = $presenter->cardMeta($task, $isPersonalBoard, $departmentOptions); $urgency = $card['urgency']; …` (unpack into locals, don't `$card['…']` inline — the churn isn't worth it). Trivial bare-accessor aliases the body reads many times (`$taskId`, `$isArchived`, `$canChangeStatus`, `$ticketId`) stay inline as one-liners per the Step-4 exception; only multi-step derivation (the `count(array_filter(...))` checklist-done counter, the `if/elseif` `deadlineTitle`, the string-built Alpine expressions, the `canCyclePriority`/`labelFilterMethod` ternaries) moves into the bag. Internals the body never reads (`$status`, `$isPersonal`, `$deadlineOverdue`) are NOT exposed — they live only inside `cardMeta` to compute the exposed keys, so they were dropped from the blade top block when the derivation moved (avoid unpacking a bag key no `@if`/`{{ }}` reads). Lock with pure tests (no DB) asserting the bag shape + the personal-vs-kanban expression/method switch + the overdue/done `deadlineTitle` branches.

## Dead state rule — remove unread public Livewire properties

Do not leave public Livewire properties that no view reads. `Gallery::$assetsLoaded` was written in `mount()` but never read in any blade — it only bloated every Livewire snapshot (public properties are serialized into every request). Remove the property AND all its assignment sites. Before removing, grep `resources/views` for readers to confirm it is truly dead. A public property with no Blade reader is dead state, not a hidden feature.

## Universal aggregate fold (UnreadNotifications)

`UnreadNotifications extends Filament\Notifications\Livewire\DatabaseNotifications` and folds the bell modal so >4 unread rows sharing a `data.menu_key` collapse to one or two **synthetic** `DatabaseNotification` rows rendered natively by Filament's modal — a UI-side anti-flood cap, zero engine change, applies to **every** nudge key (DMS, posts, tasks, feeds, …), not only DMS. Admin is untouched: `isPaginated()` returns true only for `admin`, so `getNotifications()` and `getUnreadNotificationsCount()` early-return the parent result; the non-admin `getNotificationsQuery()` adds `->unread()`.

- **Fold rule** (`getNotifications`) — `countBy` the parent collection by `data.menu_key`; iterate (parent relation `morphMany->latest()`, so first-encountered = most-recent). Per group: `count <= AGGREGATE_THRESHOLD(4)` ⇒ keep individual; `>4` ⇒ emit one aggregate and skip the rest (`$emitted[$key]` dedupe). **DMS** is special-cased to **two** aggregates — `agg:dms-controller:nudge:sign` (count = `DMS::needsSignCount`) and `agg:dms-controller:nudge:read` (count = `DMS::needsReadCount`). **Every other** over-threshold group ⇒ one generic aggregate titled «{count} اعلان جدید», body `«آخرین: {most-recent row title}»` (the "آخرین:" prefix is load-bearing — without it the body reads as "5 of that one item"). **DMS both-zero guard**: if `sign===0 && read===0` but stale rows exist (the `afterCommit` reconcile window before `ReconcileNudge` clears them), do **not** fold — keep individuals and skip the count adjustment, so `list == count == bell-dot` (the dot is a separate raw `unreadNotifications()->count()` and would otherwise stay lit while the modal went empty).
- **Header count** (`getUnreadNotificationsCount`) — start from `parent::getUnreadNotificationsCount()`, then a grouped SQL (`->reorder()->selectRaw("JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(data,'$.menu_key')) as menu_key, COUNT(*) as cnt")->groupBy('menu_key')`) adjusts each over-threshold group: DMS ⇒ `+= (sign>0?1:0)+(read>0?1:0) - cnt` (skipping on both-zero guard); generic ⇒ `+= 1 - cnt`. `->reorder()` is mandatory — the `notifications()` relation is `morphMany->latest()`, so an inherited `ORDER BY created_at` collides with `groupBy` under `sql_mode=only_full_group_by` (1055). `max(0, …)` floor.
- **Synthetic row** (`makeAggregateRow`) — `new DatabaseNotification(['id' => 'agg:{key}[:{mode}]', 'type' => FilamentDatabaseNotification::class, 'data' => Notification::make()->title()->body()->warning()->persistent()->getDatabaseMessage(), 'read_at'=>null, 'created_at'=>now()])`. The **deterministic `agg:` id** is load-bearing: `Notification::fromDatabase` sets `getId()` from `DatabaseNotification::getKey()`, and the inline view builds `wire:key = "{id}.notifications.{id}"` — a `Str::uuid()` id regenerates every render ⇒ `wire:key` churns ⇒ Alpine re-mounts + replays the enter transition every 30s poll; the stable `agg:` id stops the flicker. No `actions` key ⇒ no per-row action buttons, but the **close X is unconditional** (`x-on:click="close"` → `notificationClosed` with `notification.id`), so dismissing a synthetic via the base `removeNotification` would delete-by-id (0 rows) and reappear next render (vanish-then-return).
- **Honest dismiss** (`removeNotification` override, `#[On('notificationClosed')]`) — `str_starts_with($id,'agg:')` ⇒ parse `menu_key` out of the id (strip `agg:` prefix, then strip a trailing `:sign`/`:read` mode suffix — only DMS uses a mode and no nudge key ends in `:sign`/`:read`, so `preg_match('/:(?:sign|read)$/')` is unambiguous) and `$this->getNotificationsQuery()->where('data->menu_key',$key)->update(['read_at'=>now()])` — **mark-read, never delete**, so the aggregate stays dismissed. Real rows fall through to `parent::removeNotification`. DMS dismiss marks the whole DMS group read (sign+read rows share one `menu_key`, the sign/read split is computed at render from the `reads` table) — acceptable because the **DMS badge (Signal 1, pull-based `hasPendingFor`)** persists independently of the bell rows.
- **`dmsCounts` instance memo** — `DMS::needsSignCount`/`needsReadCount` are plain statics called in **both** `getNotifications()` and `getUnreadNotificationsCount()` on the same render; `once()` won't share across two call sites (distinct closure objects). `protected ?array $dmsCountsCache = null` + `dmsCounts(int $userId): array` (`??= [needsSignCount, needsReadCount]`) memoizes per-render — Livewire does not persist protected properties across requests, so it resets each request and the second call site is free.

## `@island` rules — memoized islands, reduced scope, and the tinker mask

The Channels module (`app/Livewire/Dashboard/Channel`) **uses `@island(name:'sidebar')` + `@island(name:'messages')`** per `app/Livewire/Dashboard/Channel/channelPattern.md`. The traps below are **general guidance for any `@island` use** — reviewers MUST reason about island behavior statically; tinker PASS does not acquit.

- **Island auto-scoping keys off `action.origin`, which only `wire:click`/`wire:model` directives set — `x-on:click` (Alpine) → `this.$wire.method()` does NOT.** Livewire's `interceptAction` (`dist/livewire.esm.js:13778`) auto-scopes an action to its enclosing island ONLY if `action.origin` is set; `wire:click`/`wire:model` call `setNextActionOrigin({el, directive})`, but an Alpine `x-on:click` that calls `this.$wire.method()` reaches `fireAction` with `outstandingActionOrigin = null` → `interceptAction` returns early → no island metadata → the server does a FULL render → every `@island` whose `always:` is false emits a `mode:'skip'` empty fragment (`HandlesIslands::renderSkippedIsland`) → the island DOM is left untouched. This is why `loadOlder`/`setFilter`/`search`/`removeAttachment` (all `wire:click`/`wire:model`) worked while `send`/`saveEdit`/`deleteMessage`/`undoDelete`/`selectChannel`/navigations (all `x-on:click → $wire.method()`) did not — the DB wrote and `#[Computed]` was unset, but the messages island showed nothing. **Rule: every Alpine-triggered action that must update an island is explicitly scoped with `$wire.$island('name').method()` — scope the action ITSELF so the method runs and `renderIsland('name')` fires in one request (server `call` hook sees `metadata.island` → `skipRender()` + `renderIsland()`), then chain a refresh of the other island only if its UI also changed.** The Channels pattern: `selectChannel` is `$wire.$island('messages').selectChannel(id).then(() => $wire.$island('sidebar').refreshUnread())`; `send`/`saveEdit`/`deleteMessage` are `$wire.$island('messages').<method>()`; navigations (`toggleBrowse`/`openCreate`/`closeCreate`/`backToList`/`leaveChannel`/`createChannel`/`joinChannel`) are `$wire.$island('messages').<method>().then(() => $wire.$island('sidebar').refreshUnread())`. `undoDelete` is scoped in the blade (`x-on:click="$wire.$island('messages').undoDelete()"`). Do NOT use `$wire.$island('name').$refresh()` — `$refresh()` is a magic action, not a real method; scope a real method instead. `wire:click`/`wire:model` triggers need NO explicit scoping — they auto-scope; only Alpine `x-on:click → $wire.method()` triggers need `$island()`.
- **Island `$refresh()` runs in REDUCED scope.** On `$island('messages').$refresh()` the island re-renders with: ✅ public properties as bare vars, ✅ `$this`, ✅ `$__livewire`. ❌ bare `#[Computed]` variables are NOT injected — `$activeChannel`, `$messages`, `$groupedMessages`, `$joinableChannels`, `$presenter` are undefined on refresh. **Rule: inside any `@island` template, access computeds via `$this->` — `$this->activeChannel`, `$this->messages`, `$this->presenter` — never the bare name.** The header/info/browse/messages islands had to be converted from `$activeChannel` → `$this->activeChannel` after a real browser island-refresh threw "Undefined variable $activeChannel".
    - **The same reduced scope silently swallows a plain `view()`-extra-data variable too — not just `#[Computed]` ones — and this variant produces NO error at all, just missing content.** Traced to `HandlesIslands::renderIsland()` (`vendor/livewire/livewire/src/Features/SupportIslands/HandlesIslands.php`): an island-scoped re-render rebuilds its Blade data purely from the component's own public properties/`#[Computed]` methods plus an explicit `$with` array (empty by default for an ordinary auto-scoped `wire:click`) — it does **not** carry forward whatever extra array `Main::render()` passed as `view($name, [...])`'s second argument. `Project\Main::render()` passed `'tabs' => $presenter->tabs(...)` this way, and `project.blade.php`'s `@if(isset($tabs[$activeTab]))` block — used to swap in the Report/Analytics `dynamic-component` — sits inside the `workspace` island. Every `switchTab()` call (a plain auto-scoped `wire:click`) re-rendered that island with `$tabs` silently undefined; `isset()` on an undefined variable just returns `false` — no exception, no console error, the `<livewire:dynamic-component>` tag simply never emitted, no matter how many times the tab was clicked. Server-side unit tests (`Livewire::test()->call(...)->assertSet('activeTab', ...)`) never caught it because they only asserted the property, never that the nested component actually mounted. Fixed by converting `tabs()` into a `#[Computed] Main::tabs(): array` method and referencing `$this->tabs[$activeTab]` in the blade — same fix shape as the bullet above, just proving the rule extends to **any** view-data key, not only ones that happen to carry a `#[Computed]` attribute already. **Rule, generalized: nothing reaches an `@island` template except `$this`, public properties, and `#[Computed]` methods (via `$this->`) — if a value is only ever passed through `render()`'s `view($name, $extraData)` second argument, it does not exist inside any `@island`, full stop.** When adding a new `dynamic-component`/lazy-swap map or any other lookup an island-scoped Blade region will read, either promote it to `#[Computed]` on the component or (like `calendar.blade.php` already does for `$presenter`) reconstruct it locally via `@php` inside that same template — never rely on `render()`'s extra `view()` data being present. **Recurrence (2026-08-30):** `project/header.blade.php` hit this with a different failure mode — `$presenter->headerRiskChips(...)` is a direct dereference, not an `isset()` guard, so the island-scoped re-render threw a hard `Undefined variable $presenter` 500 (whole project page dead) while the first full render kept working, making it look load-then-break. Same remedy as `calendar.blade.php`: `$presenter = new ProjectPresenter()` in the partial's own `@php` block. `project/form.blade.php`'s bare `$presenter` is safe only because its `@include` sits outside `@endisland` — verify an include's island position before assuming render-data reaches it.
- **A shared `@include` that dereferences `$presenter` breaks the moment a second module includes it.** `taskboard/form.blade.php` calls `$presenter->taskFormTabs()` — correct under `TaskBoard\Main` (render passes TaskBoardPresenter as `presenter`), fatal (`Call to undefined method ProjectPresenter::taskFormTabs()`) the day `project.blade.php` added `@include('livewire.dashboard.taskboard.form')`, because `@include` forwards the includer's own `$presenter` (ProjectPresenter) under that name. The whole form is presenter-bound to ONE class (taskFormTabs/defaultTaskFormTab/tabBadgeCounts), so it must never inherit the includer's presenter shape. **Rule: a cross-module include that calls presenter methods binds its presenter explicitly at the call site — `@include('livewire.dashboard.taskboard.form', ['presenter' => $taskBoardPresenter])` — don't rely on the local `$presenter` meaning the same class in the new context.** The sibling include of the same blade from `taskboard.blade.php` needs no array; the naming asymmetry is the fix, not a wart.
- **`@php(...)` parenthesized form breaks inside islands.** `@php($a = 1; $b = 2)` compiles differently in Livewire's `ExtendedCompilerEngine` island compile path vs a full render, producing "syntax error, unexpected token ;". The block form `@php ... @endphp` compiles identically in both paths. **Rule: inside `@island` templates, always use the block form `@php ... @endphp`, never `@php(...)`.** The parenthesized form is fine outside islands.
- **Correction, confirmed 2026-08-24: the parenthesized form is NOT reliably safe outside islands either.** `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/taskboard/column.blade.php` (no `@island` anywhere in its render path) broke with the identical "unexpected token endif" via `ExtendedCompilerEngine` from a single-statement `@php($countTone = $column === 'pending' ? 'error' : $config['color'])` — no semicolon, no island. Switching to block form (`@php $countTone = ...; @endphp`) fixed it immediately (verified: `TaskBoardTest` went from 81 failing to 88 passing). The exact trigger condition is still unclear (plenty of pre-existing parenthesized `@php($x = ...)` calls in `card.blade.php` compile fine in the same render tree), so **treat parenthesized `@php(...)` as fragile everywhere in this codebase, not just inside islands — prefer the block form by default.**
- **`@entangle` and nested anonymous Blade components (`<x-...>`) do NOT work inside islands.** Nested anonymous components don't inherit `$__livewire`, so `@entangle($model).live` (used by `<x-ui.forms.search>`) fatals inside an island. **Rule: inside `@island` templates, use plain `wire:model` / `wire:model.live` + Alpine `x-model` for inputs, never `<x-ui.forms.search>` or any component that relies on `@entangle`.** The sidebar search is a plain `wire:model.live.debounce.200ms` input.
- **`Livewire::test()->html()` and tinker full-render MASK every island-only bug.** A full render shares scope globally (computeds injected as bare vars, `@php(...)` compiles the full-render way, the whole component renders as one tree) — so `Livewire::test(...)->call('send')->html()` shows the new message, the edited body, the restored row, all correct; the island `$refresh()` path is never exercised. **Rule: a tinker/test PASS is not evidence the island works.** Reviewers MUST reason statically — `grep` island templates for bare computed names, `@php(`, `@entangle`/`<x-ui.forms.search` inside `@island`, and mutations for a missing `$island(...).$refresh()` or `messages-updated`-style dispatch — because the runtime test that would catch these is a real browser island-refresh, which the gate does not perform. This is why 59 reviewers green-lit a module that errored on first browser use.
- **`x-ref` inside `@island` throws on the first island re-render and aborts Alpine's whole init pass — "works after refresh, dies after first change."** Registering `x-ref` walks up `findClosest(el, i => i._x_refs …)` to the nearest Alpine root to write `_x_refs[name]`. During an island morph, Alpine's `cloneNode`/`initTree` clones the new subtree while its ancestor chain is mid-detach, the walk returns `undefined`, and it throws `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading '_x_refs')` (Alpine `module.esm.js:3436`, `$refs` magic → `getArrayOfRefObject`). The throw aborts `initTree` partway, so every directive AFTER the ref element (toolbar buttons, `x-on:click` handlers, `x-if` clones further down) never initializes — the island looks frozen even though the morph completed. First cold load survives; the first `send`/`saveEdit`/`deleteMessage` that re-renders dies. **Rule: never use `x-ref` inside an `@island` template.** Reach the element by `document.getElementById(...)` (the Channels module's `msg-viewport`/`msg-ta` access pattern in `channel.js`) or, for reactive sibling state, a scoped `<div x-data="{ len: 0 }" x-effect="len = ($wire.composer.body || '').length">` wrapper feeding `x-on:input="len = $el.value.length"` — no ref walk, morph-safe. `grep -rn 'x-ref=' resources/views/livewire/dashboard/channel/` MUST return empty.
- **`<template x-if>` inside `@island` renders dead buttons when the condition is true at morph time.** Alpine's morph plugin clones `<template x-if>` content into the DOM during the island morph but does NOT reliably re-run `x-on:click` handler init on the cloned content — buttons render but clicks do nothing. The trap is specific to x-if conditions **TRUE at morph time** (content cloned during the morph): the Channels message bubble used `<template x-if="!(isEditing && editingMsgId === {{ $msg['id'] }})">` (true while not editing → toolbox cloned at morph → dead). An x-if **FALSE at morph time** (e.g. the composer's `<template x-if="replyingTo">`, false until `startReply`) is fine — its content is cloned later by Alpine's reactive system, which inits correctly. **Rule: inside `@island`, prefer `x-show` over `<template x-if>` for any toggle whose visible branch contains interactive elements.** `x-show` keeps both branches in the DOM (one `style="display: none;"`), Alpine inits them once, handlers survive every morph. With `x-show`, replace `x-init` with `x-effect` for behavior that must re-run each time the toggle becomes active — e.g. `x-effect="if (isEditing && editingMsgId === {{ $msg['id'] }}) $nextTick(() => { $el.focus(); $el.selectionStart = $el.selectionEnd = $el.value.length })"`.
- **`ChannelMessage::lastIdForChannel(int): ?int` returns null for empty channels — null-guard every caller.** `MarkChannelReadAction` did `orWhere('last_read_message_id', '<', $lastId)` with `$lastId === null`, which Laravel rejects with "Illegal operator and value combination". **Rule: any caller of `lastIdForChannel` (or any nullable-id lower-bound filter) must `if ($lastId === null) return;` before the query.** Empty-channel `markRead` is a no-op, not a crash.
- **`WithFileUploads` inside `@island` skips the island on upload-finish, so the staged-attachment preview never paints — bridge it with a `->self()` dispatch the island's own Alpine `$wire.on` catches, then re-render via a real island-scoped method (NOT `$refresh()`).** `startUpload`/`finishUpload` are Livewire-internal calls that don't carry island origin metadata, so the upload-finish response is a full render where the island's DOM diff is skipped. The composer property (`composer.attachments`) IS set server-side and the client model IS updated, but the staged-preview `@if(count($composer->attachments))` lives inside the island template — its HTML never morphs in, so the user picks a file, sees nothing, and only sees it after `send`. The fix is a two-call dance: (1) a Livewire `updated(string $name)` hook on the root fires when `composer.attachments` changes and dispatches `attachments-updated` `->self()` — `->self()` events are delivered at component level even when the island DOM was skipped, so Alpine `$wire.on('attachments-updated', ...)` always catches it; (2) that listener calls `$wire.$island('messages').syncAttachments()`, a real non-empty island-scoped method (e.g. `unset($this->groupedMessages)` — non-empty, non-looping, no extra query, matches the `unset($this->X)` computed-invalidation idiom; do NOT touch `composer.attachments` inside it or `updated()` re-fires into an infinite loop), which issues a fresh island-scoped request and the preview paints. **Never use `$wire.$island('name').$refresh()` here — it's the forbidden magic action; scope a real method instead.** Same trap hits any `wire:model` on a file input inside an `@island`.
- **A shared modal that uses `@entangle` (via `x-ui.modals.base`'s `x-data="{ show: @entangle($attributes->wire('model')) }"`) fatals with `Undefined variable $__livewire` the instant it renders inside an `@island` — this is the SAME `@entangle`-in-islands restriction already documented above for `<x-ui.forms.search>`, but it also applies transitively through any nested anonymous component the island renders, not just ones written directly inside the island's own template.** Discovered wrapping the shared TaskBoard/Project task-edit modal (`taskboard/form.blade.php`, `@include`d by both `taskboard.blade.php` and `project.blade.php`'s kanban tab) in a new `@island('taskModal')` to fix a *different* bug (below) — the modal's own wrapper chain (`x-ui.modals.action` → `x-ui.modals.base`) uses `@entangle`, so island-wrapping it broke every render immediately. **Rule: never island-wrap a component that (transitively) renders `x-ui.modals.base` or any other `@entangle`-based wrapper** — if the thing you need to keep in sync lives inside one of these shared modals, the fix belongs on the *triggering* action, not on wrapping the modal.
- **A `wire:click` fired from inside one `@island` auto-scopes its response to that island only, silently skipping a DIFFERENT (or non-island) region the same action also needs to update — the fix is to drop `wire:click` in favor of Alpine `x-on:click="$wire.method(...)"`, which carries no island-origin metadata at all and forces an ordinary full render.** Real bug: `Project\Main::openProjectTask()`/`openCreateModal()` are called via `wire:click` from inside the `kanban` and `projectCalendar` islands (`tasking/board-card.blade.php`, `tasking/board-column.blade.php`, `project/calendar.blade.php`) — each server-side state change (`isEditMode`, `isReadOnly`, the modal title) was applied correctly, but the shared task modal (`@include`d outside every island, see above) never got the updated HTML back, so the modal's title stayed frozen at whatever it showed on the last full page load (typically "ایجاد وظیفه جدید"/Create) no matter which task you opened or how you opened it. The `$wire.$island('name').method()` scoping pattern documented earlier in this section (Channels) is for when you WANT to target a specific island; here the actual fix is the opposite — you want NO island scoping, i.e. a plain, unscoped `$wire.method()` call, which per the very first bullet of this `@island` section ("Island auto-scoping keys off `action.origin`, which only `wire:click`/`wire:model` set — `x-on:click` → `this.$wire.method()` does NOT") simply never enters the island-scoping path and falls through to a full render instead. **Rule: any action that must refresh a region living OUTSIDE every island (a modal, a toast, a sibling non-island section) should be triggered via Alpine `x-on:click="$wire.method(...)"`, never `wire:click` — this is the reverse case of "scope an Alpine-triggered action to an island with `$wire.$island()`," and picking the wrong one is easy to miss because the server-side state change still silently succeeds; only the HTML the client actually receives is wrong.**

## Passing PHP values into Alpine handlers — `data-*` + `$el.dataset`, not `@js()` or Blade directives inside the attribute

When a Blade-rendered list row needs to hand a server value (DB id, slug, key) to an Alpine `x-on:click` handler, **serialize it through an HTML attribute and read it back from the DOM** — never inline `@js($value)` and never embed Blade control directives inside the Alpine attribute.

- **The pattern:** put the value on the element with `data-id="{{ $value }}"`, then call `x-on:click="handler($el.dataset.id)"`. `{{ }}` HTML-escapes and stringifies, the DOM `dataset` API returns a plain string, and the Alpine handler receives it as a normal arg. Concrete Channel use: in-chat message-search result buttons (`search.blade.php`) — `data-id="{{ $r['id'] }}"` + `x-on:click="focusSearchResult($el.dataset.id)"` → `focusSearchResult(id)` → `$wire.$island('messages').focusMessage(id)`.
- **Why not `@js($value)`:** `@js()` emits a raw JS literal *directly into the attribute string*. For an int that's `123` (fine), but for any string value (UUID, slug, anything carrying quotes) `@js` emits `"abc-123"` — the embedded double-quotes **break the surrounding `x-on:click="..."` attribute**, silently corrupting the handler. The `data-*` route is immune to quoting regardless of value type, because the value lives in its own attribute (HTML-escaped by `{{ }}`), not inside a JS expression embedded in another attribute.
- **Type round-trip:** `dataset.id` is always a string. For a Livewire method typed `int $id`, Livewire JSON-wires the string and PHP's type-hint coerces it back to int — the int round-trips correctly. One micro-note: a JS `if (!id)` guard treats string `"0"` as truthy (unlike `@js(0)` which yields falsy `0`), so a `0`/missing id makes one extra round-trip before the server guard returns null; in practice list rows never carry `0`, and the server guard handles it safely, so no change needed. If you ever want to close that gap, `focusSearchResult(Number($el.dataset.id))`.
- **Same principle for the island-safe `search-field` partial:** inside Alpine attributes (`x-on:keydown.escape`, `x-on:click`) interpolate **only safe scalar identifiers** (`$model`) or a **precomputed plain string** built in `@php` (`$closeExpr`) — never Blade control directives. The earlier broken form embedded `@if($refreshSidebarOnClose)...@endif` directly inside `x-on:keydown.escape="..."`, which made the Blade compiler emit `<?php ... endif; ?>` inside the attribute and produced `syntax error, unexpected token "endif", expecting end of file` (a hard fatal, not a quoting edge case). The fix builds the close expression once in `@php` — `$closeExpr = "{$model}Fullscreen = false; \$wire.{$model} = {$model}Value" . ($refreshSidebarOnClose ? "; \$wire.\$island('sidebar').refreshUnread().catch(() => {})" : '')` — and echoes `{{ $closeExpr }}` into the attribute. One source of truth, no Blade directives inside the JS string.
- **Rule:** inside any Alpine attribute (`x-on:*`, `x-bind:*`, `x-show`, `:class`), (a) pass server values via `data-*` + `$el.dataset` rather than `@js()`, and (b) keep all conditional/loop logic in `@php` and interpolate only the resulting plain string — never put `@if`/`@foreach`/`@php(...)`/`@js(...)` inside the attribute value. This is the island-safe generalization of the `@entangle`/`<x-ui.forms.search>` ban (see the `@island` rules above): the island-safe `search-field` partial exists precisely because the shared `<x-ui.forms.search>` relies on `@entangle`, and it must stay self-contained — parameterize only by `$model` (a safe identifier) plus precomputed strings, with a unique `wire:key="search-overlay-{{ $model }}"` on the teleported overlay.

## Alpine `:class`/expression bindings on a Livewire property must read `$wire.propertyName`, never a bare variable (DMS filter buttons)

DMS's filter buttons (`dms/filters.blade.php`) had correct server-side reactivity — `updatedActiveFilter()` reset `open` and reloaded docs, `getBaseQuery()`'s JSON `whereJsonContains` matching was correct — yet users reported "filters not working, clicking does nothing." The bug was purely visual: `:class="activeFilter === 'all' ? ... : ..."` referenced a **bare** `activeFilter`, but the page root is `x-data="dms()"` (`resources/js/components/alpine/data/dms.js`), whose factory defines no `activeFilter` property at all. Alpine evaluates `:class` expressions in `try/catch` and **swallows the `ReferenceError` silently** — no console error, no visible failure — so the binding just never updated and the active-state highlight never moved, reading to the user as "the click did nothing."

Every other filter partial in the codebase (`ads/filters.blade.php`, `tab/status/filters.blade.php`, `authority/filters.blade.php`, `tab/feeds/filters.blade.php`, `tab/faqs/filters.blade.php`) already used `$wire.propertyName` correctly — DMS was the sole outlier, introduced by copy-pasting a bare-variable pattern that only works when that exact name is ALSO defined in the same page's local `x-data()` factory. The shared `<x-ui.forms.filters>` wrapper (`resources/views/components/ui/forms/filters.blade.php`) and its `filters()` Alpine factory (`resources/js/components/alpine/data/filters.js`) confirm the convention: `activeCondition`/`clearAction` props default to `this.$wire.get("activeFilter") !== "all"` / `this.$wire.set(...)`.

**Rule:** inside any Alpine attribute (`:class`, `:style`, `x-show`, `x-on:*`) that needs to read or react to a **Livewire public property**, always use `$wire.propertyName` (or `$wire.get('propertyName')`) — never a bare identifier — unless that identical name is genuinely declared inside that page's own local `x-data({...})` object or `.js` Alpine factory. Because Alpine swallows expression errors silently, this class of bug produces zero error output anywhere (browser console, Livewire, logs) and must be caught by code review/grep (`:class="\w+ ===` bare-variable patterns), not by watching for exceptions. See regression tests in `tests/Feature/Livewire/Dashboard/DmsTest.php` (`test_filter_buttons_bind_to_livewire_scope_not_bare_alpine_variable` and the filter-narrowing tests).

**Update:** the "همه" (All) button this section originally referenced was removed (`filters.blade.php`) — it was fully redundant with the shared `<x-ui.forms.filters>` wrapper's own "حذف فیلترها" (Clear Filters) button, whose default `clearAction` already does `$wire.set('activeFilter','all'); $wire.set('search','')` (a superset), shown exactly when `activeFilter !== 'all'` — i.e. exactly when the All button would have mattered. The regression test above now asserts the same `$wire.`-scoping invariant against a still-existing per-value filter button instead of the removed one.

## A leading `:attr=` on a Blade `<x-component>` tag is ALWAYS Blade's PHP-eval shorthand, never Alpine's — fatal on every page load, not silent

`taskboard/tools.blade.php`'s density toggle wrote `<x-ui.buttons.form :class="$store.density.compact ? '...' : '...'" :title="..." :aria-label="...">`, intending an Alpine `$store` binding. Blade component tags reserve the bare `:attr=` prefix for its own PHP-evaluation shorthand (`:class="$expr"` compiles to `<?php echo $expr ?>` against Blade's PHP scope, not Alpine's JS scope) — this collision exists **only on `<x-component>` tags**, never on plain HTML elements, where `:attr` is unambiguously Alpine's `x-bind:attr` shorthand. Blade tried to evaluate `$store.density.compact` as PHP, where `.` means string concatenation, and `density`/`compact` are undefined-constant references — a hard fatal (`Error: Undefined constant "density"`) on every single page load, unlike the DMS bare-variable bug above (§"Alpine `:class`/expression bindings...") which fails silently. **Rule: on any `<x-component>` tag, an Alpine binding must use the explicit `x-bind:attr=` (or `x-bind:class=`) prefix — never the bare `:attr=` shorthand, which Blade always claims first.** `grep` for `<x-[a-z.]+[^>]*\s:(class|style|title|aria-[a-z-]+)=` to catch this on component tags; the same bare `:attr` on a plain `<div>`/`<button>` is fine and is the correct Alpine shorthand there.

## `getBaseQuery()` free-text search — `INSTR()`, not `LIKE`, for every free-text OR-clause

`Dms/Main.php`'s `getBaseQuery()` searches `title`/`code`/`version`/`revision` plus the `extra`/`tags` JSON columns (via `CAST(... AS CHAR)`) against one user-typed `$this->search` string. Two independent bugs, both traced to MySQL's `LIKE` operator specifically (not a query-builder or PHP-side issue), both fixed by switching every free-text clause from `LIKE '%...%'` to `INSTR(column, ?) > 0`:

1. **A literal `%`/`_` in the search term silently over-matches.** `LIKE` treats `%`/`_` as wildcards even when they arrive via a bound parameter (parameter binding prevents SQL injection, it does **not** disable `LIKE`'s own pattern-metacharacter semantics) — a search for `50%_report` would match far more than the literal string. `INSTR()` is a plain substring-position function with **no** metacharacter interpretation at all, so this class of over-matching is structurally impossible, not just guarded against.
2. **A `\uXXXX`-escaped needle silently under-matches (false negative, the more dangerous direction).** `extra`/`tags` are Eloquent `array`-cast columns, saved via `json_encode()` with no `JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE` flag — every non-ASCII character (all Persian content) is stored on disk as literal `\uXXXX` ASCII escape sequences, e.g. `{"notes":"رضا..."}`. Searching for the raw UTF-8 term (`رضایی`) against `CAST(extra AS CHAR)` never matches that escaped text, so the natural fix is to *also* search for the JSON-escaped form of the needle: `trim(json_encode($needle), '"')` turns `رضایی` into the literal string `رضا...` — the exact bytes present in the stored column. But `LIKE`'s **own** default escape character is `\`, so a pattern containing `ر...` gets its backslashes stripped/reinterpreted by `LIKE` itself before matching, silently corrupting the search — confirmed via direct `DB::select` with **no** query builder, **no** `CAST`, just plain `extra LIKE ?` against the raw column: still zero matches with the correctly-escaped needle. `INSTR()` has no escape-character concept whatsoever, so the escaped needle matches byte-for-byte once `LIKE` is out of the picture. This is the **same root MariaDB JSON/unicode-escaping problem family** documented in `app/Filament/filamentPattern.md`'s "MariaDB fails to match a JSON object key…" note (which covers `JSON_EXTRACT` path-matching specifically) — a *different* mechanism (LIKE-escape stripping, not JSON path encoding), same underlying cause (PHP's `json_encode` ASCII-escaping non-ASCII content), same fix direction (stop relying on MySQL to interpret the escaped bytes correctly, work with them explicitly in PHP instead).

**Current shape** (abridged — real method also repeats the `extra->Category`/`extra->type`/`extra->Type` `orWhereJsonContains` variants and the same `INSTR` pair for `tags`, omitted here for brevity; read `getBaseQuery()` directly before copying this pattern):
```php
$needle = $this->search;
$escapedNeedle = trim(json_encode($needle), '"');
$q->whereRaw('INSTR(title, ?) > 0', [$needle])
    ->orWhereRaw('INSTR(code, ?) > 0', [$needle])
    ->orWhereRaw('INSTR(version, ?) > 0', [$needle])
    ->orWhereRaw('INSTR(revision, ?) > 0', [$needle])
    ->orWhereJsonContains('extra->category', $needle) // exact-match specific keys, untouched — JsonContains has no LIKE-escape exposure
    ->orWhereRaw('INSTR(CAST(extra AS CHAR), ?) > 0', [$needle])       // ASCII/mixed substrings
    ->orWhereRaw('INSTR(CAST(extra AS CHAR), ?) > 0', [$escapedNeedle]); // Persian/non-ASCII substrings
    // ...same two INSTR lines repeated for `tags`, same four orWhereJsonContains repeated for extra->Category/type/Type
```
**Rule:** any future free-text `LIKE`-based search added to this codebase should default to `INSTR(column, ?) > 0` instead of `LIKE '%?%'` unless genuine wildcard matching (`%`/`_` as user-facing pattern syntax) is actually wanted — `INSTR` is strictly safer for "does this substring appear anywhere" searches and sidesteps this entire bug family by construction. Regression tests: `tests/Feature/Livewire/Dashboard/DmsTest.php` — `test_search_filters_by_persian_extra_value`, `test_search_with_literal_percent_and_underscore_does_not_wildcard_match`.

**Scale note:** `dms` sits at ~150 rows in practice, expected to plateau around 500–2000 — a 12-condition `OR` full scan at that size is sub-millisecond regardless of indexing. A generated-column + FULLTEXT-ngram rewrite was evaluated and explicitly rejected: the ngram parser is MySQL/Oracle-only and **not available on MariaDB** (this project's production engine — confirmed MariaDB 10.6.20 elsewhere in this doc set, vs. the plain MySQL 5.7.24 this was developed against locally), so it would work in dev and break in prod. Revisit only if `dms` genuinely grows into the tens-of-thousands range; if so, generated `STORED` columns for the *exact-match* JSON fields (`extra_type`, `extra_category`) are MariaDB-safe and worth it — the FULLTEXT/ngram half of that idea specifically is not.

## `getBaseQuery()` sort — read-state priority (unreceived → received-unread → read), then recency

The DMS workflow is two-stage per user, per document: **receive** (acknowledge existence, `ConfirmReadAction::execute($id)` → upserts a `reads` row with `read=true`) then **read** (`ConfirmReadAction::execute($id, increment: true)` → increments `read_count`). Three states follow directly from that, with no state column of their own — derived from whether a `reads` row exists and its `read_count`:

| State | Condition | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 0 — unreceived | no `reads` row for this user+doc | brand new, not even acknowledged |
| 1 — received, unread | `reads` row exists, `read_count = 0` | acknowledged, not yet reviewed |
| 2 — read | `reads` row exists, `read_count > 0` | fully handled |

`getBaseQuery()`'s order is this 3-tier state ascending (0 first — most-needs-action surfaces at the top) as the **primary** key, then a user-selectable column as the secondary tie-breaker *within* a tier, then `id desc` as the final deterministic tie-breaker. Read-state priority always wins over the column sort, by design (a just-updated-but-already-read doc must not outrank an older untouched one). The secondary sort is the in-tab `sortBy()` feature (title/code/updated), defaulting to `updated desc`. Implemented as a correlated subquery in `orderByRaw`, not a `JOIN`, specifically to avoid introducing a second `id` column into the query (`matchingDocIds()`'s `pluck('id')`/`select(['id','tags'])` calls right after `getBaseQuery()` would become ambiguous under a join):
```php
private function readPriorityExpression(): string
{
    return "COALESCE(
        (SELECT CASE WHEN read_count = 0 THEN 1 ELSE 2 END
            FROM `reads`
            WHERE `reads`.document_id = dms.id AND `reads`.user_id = ?
            LIMIT 1),
        0
    ) ASC";
}

private const SORT_COLUMNS = [
    'updated' => 'updated_at',
    'title' => 'title',
    'code' => 'code',
];

// ->orderByRaw($this->readPriorityExpression(), [auth()->id()])
//   ->orderBy($sortCol, $sortDir)   // from validSort(): whitelisted column + asc/desc
//   ->orderBy('id', 'desc');
```
**Security — `orderBy()` does not parameterize column names.** `$this->sort`/`$this->sortDir` are public Livewire properties (client-settable via the snapshot), so a raw `->orderBy($this->sort, $this->sortDir)` is an injection vector. `validSort()` resolves the column through the `SORT_COLUMNS` whitelist (unknown key → `updated_at`) and the direction through a strict lower-case `asc`/`desc` check (anything else → `desc`). `sortBy($column)` is itself a no-op for keys outside `SORT_COLUMNS`; calling it on the active column toggles direction, on a new column starts at `asc`. `sort` survives `switchTab` (a view preference); `pendingFilter` does not (reset per tab). `resetSort()` restores the `updated`/`desc` default and is a no-op when already there — wired to a `restart_alt` button beside the table's maximize toggle, dimmed + `disabled` at the default so it never fires a no-op round-trip.

**Gotcha — the inline `@php($expr)` Blade directive breaks `@forelse`/`@empty` compilation in this Livewire/Blade stack** when the expression contains `&&`/`===` (the compiled view throws `syntax error, unexpected token "endforeach", expecting end of file` at the later `@forelse`). Use the block form `@php … @endphp` for any multi-operator expression; reserve `@php($single = value)` for trivial single assignments only.
**Gotcha — `READS` is a reserved MySQL/MariaDB keyword** (used in stored-routine `READS SQL DATA` characteristics), so the bare, unquoted table name in a raw SQL fragment throws `SQLSTATE[42000]: syntax error ... near 'reads'` — the error points at the token *after* the real problem, not at `reads` itself, which makes this easy to misdiagnose as a paren-matching bug. Any future raw SQL referencing the `reads` table must backtick-quote it (`` `reads` ``); Eloquent's query builder backticks it automatically via `->table('reads')`/relationships, so this only bites hand-written `whereRaw`/`orderByRaw`/`DB::select` strings. Confirmed via direct `DB::select` reproduction with zero query-builder involvement.
**Gotcha — a bare `{{ $expr ? 'x' : '' }}` echo (or `@disabled($expr)`) used as a standalone pseudo-attribute on a Blade `<x-component ... />` tag silently breaks `ComponentTagCompiler`.** No exception is thrown — the entire tag is emitted as literal, uncompiled text in the final HTML (e.g. `<x-ui.buttons.submit ...>` shows up verbatim in the page, so the component simply never renders). Reproduced directly via `Blade::render()`: the same bare echo/`@disabled()` breaks compilation anywhere in the attribute list, while `:disabled="$expr"` (a proper dynamic prop) compiles cleanly and correctly omits/includes the attribute via `ComponentAttributeBag`'s standard `true`/`false`/`null` handling — no `@props` declaration needed on the receiving component for it to fall through to `$attributes`. Always pass conditional boolean-ish attributes to `<x-...>` tags as `:name="$expr"`, never as a bare `{{ }}`/`@directive()` pseudo-attribute; that form is safe only on plain HTML tags, which `ComponentTagCompiler` never touches.

Regression tests: `tests/Feature/Livewire/Dashboard/DmsTest.php` — `test_sort_prioritizes_unreceived_then_received_unread_then_read` (3-tier order via `search` to isolate from unrelated seeded rows), `test_sort_within_same_read_state_falls_back_to_most_recently_updated`, `test_sort_by_title_orders_docs_alphabetically_within_same_read_state`, `test_sort_by_same_column_toggles_direction`, `test_sort_by_new_column_resets_direction_to_asc`, `test_sort_by_unwhitelisted_column_is_silent_noop` (injection guard), `test_reset_sort_restores_default_and_reloads`, `test_reset_sort_is_noop_when_already_default`.

## DMS action-required banner — clickable pending counts → in-place pending filter (no new filter UI)

The action-required banner (`livewire/dashboard/dms/legend.blade.php`) shows two pending counts per tab: `receivePendingCount` (unconfirmed docs) and `readPendingCount` (confirmed-but-unread docs), both already `#[Computed]` off `visibleTabQuery()` + the `confirmedDocs`/`readDocs` memoized lists. Making those two numbers clickable filters the table to exactly that pending cohort — **no new segmented toggle, no extra filter row**: the count itself is the filter affordance.

- **`togglePendingFilter(string $which)`** accepts only `'receive'`/`'read'` (anything else is a silent no-op — the same whitelist-guard discipline as `SORT_COLUMNS`). `'receive'` → `whereNotIn('id', $this->confirmedDocs)`; `'read'` → `whereIn('id', $this->confirmedDocs)->whereNotIn('id', $this->readDocs)`. Both reuse the already-queried `#[Computed]` lists — **zero new queries** (the filter is appended to the single existing `getBaseQuery()` re-query that search/filter already trigger). Calling the active value again toggles it off (`pendingFilter = null`); `clearPendingFilter()` is the explicit reset (no-op when already clear). `switchTab` resets `pendingFilter` (per-tab cohort, not a cross-tab view pref like `sort`).
- **Empty state is actionable, not generic.** When the pending filter yields zero rows, the `@empty` branch of the table `@forelse` swaps the generic `folder_off` empty for a `task_alt` "هیچ سند معوقی باقی نمانده" empty plus a centered `clearPendingFilter` button — the user can dismiss the filter from the empty state itself, not only from the banner.
- **Rule:** a count that already represents a meaningful cohort is the filter affordance — wire `wire:click="togglePendingFilter(...)"` on the count `<span>`, reuse the `#[Computed]` lists for the clause, and give the resulting empty state a one-tap exit. Do NOT add a parallel segmented control or a new filter chip; the banner count and the filtered table are the same data, expressed two ways.

Regression tests: `test_toggle_pending_filter_receive_isolates_unconfirmed_docs`, `test_toggle_pending_filter_read_isolates_confirmed_but_unread_docs`, `test_toggle_pending_filter_same_value_clears_it`, `test_toggle_pending_filter_rejects_unknown_value`, `test_clear_pending_filter_resets_and_reloads_all`, `test_clear_pending_filter_is_noop_when_already_clear`, `test_switch_tab_resets_pending_filter`, `test_pending_filter_composes_with_search`, `test_pending_filter_receive_empty_state_yields_zero_doc_ids`.

## DMS version-cell popover — created/updated Jalali dates on the version chip

The version column (`code - version` chip) in `livewire/dashboard/dms/table.blade.php` is a click-toggle popover revealing `created_at` («ایجاد», `add_circle`, primary) and `updated_at` («بروزرسانی», `update`, tertiary) as Jalali `j F Y` via the `toJalali()` helper, each `@if`-null-guarded.

- **Reuse decision: mirror the in-table renditions dropdown, NOT `<x-ui.modals.popover>`.** The existing `<x-ui.modals.popover>` and `<x-ui.modals.tooltip>` components both use **absolute** positioning (`z-50`/`z-[9999]`). Inside this table's `overflow-x-auto` wrapper — which per CSS spec forces `overflow-y: auto` and clips out-of-flow descendants — an absolute popover clips at the wrapper's bottom edge for the last row. The renditions dropdown in the **same file** already solved this by using **normal-flow `mt-1`** (no `absolute`, in-flow) so opening it expands the cell instead of overlaying/clipping. The version popover follows that same in-table convention (`relative flex flex-col items-center` + in-flow `mt-1 w-max` panel) for consistency and clip-safety. **Rule: in any table wrapped by `overflow-x-auto`, prefer the normal-flow `mt-1` popover pattern over the absolute `<x-ui.modals.popover>` component; reserve the absolute component for non-clipped contexts.**
- **Empty-state guard.** `$versionPopover = !empty($doc->created_at) || !empty($doc->updated_at)` (block `@php … @endphp`, NOT inline `@php(...)` — see gotcha below). When false (both timestamps null), the cell renders a plain non-interactive chip with no trigger, so a click never opens an empty dropdown. The `docs` `#[Computed]` re-queries fresh from DB, so a raw null-timestamp row is honored.
- **Gotcha — inline `@php(...)` with `||`/`&&` also breaks `@if`/`@endif` compilation** (same family as the sort section's `@forelse` gotcha above — `syntax error, unexpected token "endif"` pointing at an unrelated `@endif` far from the `@php`); same fix: block form `@php … @endphp`.

Regression tests: `test_version_cell_popover_renders_created_and_updated_labels_when_dates_set`, `test_version_cell_renders_plain_chip_without_popover_when_both_dates_null` (the null case uses `DB::table('dms')->update(['created_at'=>null,'updated_at'=>null])` after factory create, since Eloquent's `updateTimestamps()` re-stamps a null `created_at` on insert).

## DMS details + description popovers — collapse verbose cells, one item per row

Two more DMS table columns collapse behind the same normal-flow `mt-1` popover idiom (above) so wide cells stay compact and row heights uniform:

- **Details column — popover whenever there's at least one item.** `$extraDetails = collect($doc->extra ?? [])->except(['category','Category','type','Type','users'])` (category/type/users are consumed elsewhere, not "details"). When `count() > 0`, render a «جزییات» chip with a count badge and a click-toggle popover listing each item on its own row (`@foreach($extraDetails as $key => $value)` → key badge + value) — this applies uniformly whether there's one item or many, so the trigger/chip/badge treatment never varies by count; `@else` (zero items) shows the «بدون جزییات» empty state, unchanged. A prior `> 1` threshold (single item rendered inline, no popover) was dropped for consistency — every non-empty row now behaves identically.
- **Description column — revision popover below the renditions block.** The existing file-types/renditions popover («نسخه‌های سند») stays untouched at the top of the cell. Below it, `@if(!empty($doc->revision))` renders a «توضیحات ▾» click-toggle popover with `{{ $doc->revision }}` on its own row (`max-w-xs`); `@else` renders a muted italic «بدون توضیح». This removes the old inline `line-clamp-2` block so the row height no longer grows with revision length.
- **Test-marker convention: `title="…"` is the unique selector, NOT the column header.** The details and description columns both share header text («جزییات»/«توضیحات») that also appears in body chips, so asserting on header text is ambiguous. Each popover trigger carries a unique `title` attribute (`title="نمایش جزییات"`, `title="نمایش توضیحات"`) used as the raw-HTML assertion marker (`assertSee('title="نمایش جزییات"', false)` / `assertDontSee(…, false)`). Same convention the version-cell popover uses for its trigger.

Regression tests: `test_details_cell_renders_popover_when_multiple_extra_details`, `test_details_cell_renders_popover_when_single_extra_detail`, `test_details_cell_renders_empty_state_when_no_extra_details` (the empty case uses `extra => ['type' => 'دستورالعمل']` since `type` is excluded, yielding zero details), `test_description_cell_renders_revision_popover_when_revision_set`, `test_description_cell_renders_no_popover_when_revision_empty`. All use `DMS::factory()->create([... 'code'=>'DET-00X' ...])` + `->set('search', $code)` isolation and `uniqid()`-suffixed values for collision-free assertions.

## DMS table popover trigger chips — one unified chip style across all four popovers

All four click-toggle popovers in `dms/table.blade.php` (version-cell created/updated, details, renditions «نسخه‌های سند», description-revision) now share **one trigger-button style** so the column reads as a consistent ERP-grade set, not a mix of chips and links:

```
inline-flex w-max items-center gap-1.5 rounded-md border border-[--outline-variant]/30 bg-[--surface-container] px-2 py-1 text-[11px] font-semibold text-[--on-surface-variant] transition-colors hover:bg-[--surface-container-highest]
```

plus a colored `material-symbols-rounded` glyph (version has none; details `list`/primary; renditions `file_present`/primary; revision `comment`/tertiary), the label, an optional count badge (`rounded-full bg-[--primary-container] …`), and the `expand_more` caret with `:class="{ 'rotate-180': open }"`. The renditions button was the hold-out — it was a borderless link (`text-primary … hover:opacity-70`) — and was brought into the chip style. Each trigger also carries a unique `title="…"` marker (`نمایش جزییات` / `نمایش نسخه‌ها` / `نمایش توضیحات` / `تاریخ ایجاد و بروزرسانی`) used as the raw-HTML test selector (the `title`-as-test-marker convention above). The popover **panels** themselves were already consistent (`mt-1 w-max rounded-xl border … bg-[--surface] p-1.5/2/2.5 shadow-xl`); only the triggers were divergent.

**Tag radius softening.** The category + free-tag pills under the title (the `rounded-full` spans wired to `wire:click="$set('search'|'activeFilter', …)"`) were softened from `rounded-full` to `rounded-md` so they read identically beside the popover chip items (which are `rounded-md`) instead of clashing as harder full pills. The count badges inside popovers stay `rounded-full` — they are small numeric pips, a different visual role from text pills.

Regression tests: `test_renditions_cell_renders_popover_chip_when_multiple_renditions`, `test_renditions_cell_renders_inline_when_single_rendition` (renditions popover only renders when `$isConfirmed`; both tests `call('confirmRead', …)` first, then a fresh `test(Main::class)->set('search', …)` to assert the marker).

## DMS column-visibility mask — `$store.colVisibility` + FOUC `<style>`, morph-safe by construction

A per-user show/hide mask on the DMS doc table's 7 columns (`title`, `code`, `dept`, `status`, `details`, `desc`, `action`), **fully client-side** and chosen specifically because it is **morph-safe by construction** — unlike column reorder. Hiding is a pure-CSS `display:none` driven by an injected `<style id="dms-col-visibility-style">` (one rule per hidden key: `.dms-doc-table [data-col="KEY"]{display:none!important}`), NOT a per-cell Alpine binding and NOT a DOM move. Every `<th>` + `<td>` carries a static `data-col="…"`; the table always renders the server's default column order, so Livewire morph matches cells by position cleanly — no corruption, no keyed-move revert, no Alpine-popover state loss. The `<style>` sits in `<head>` outside the Livewire component, so morph never touches it: a masked column stays hidden across every sort/filter/loadMore/confirmRead with **zero re-apply logic**. State in `localStorage['dms-col-hidden']` (JSON array). Full architecture in `resources/js/scriptPattern.md` §13.

**Two pieces + the blade:** `Alpine.store('colVisibility', { hidden, isHidden, toggle, reset, render })` in `resources/js/components/alpine/stores/colVisibility.js` (registered in `main.js` like density) + the paired FOUC head-script in `public/js/prefs-manager.js` (loaded in `<head>` before `@vite`, injects the same `<style>` pre-paint so masked columns never flash on load — merged into one file alongside the density IIFE below, since both are user-panel-dashboard-only and always loaded together; `mode-manager.js` stays its own file because the admin panel needs dark/light mode standalone, without this DMS/density-specific logic). `render()` rewrites the style's `textContent` (no `innerHTML` → no XSS); `init()` guards the parsed value with `try/catch` + `Array.isArray` so a tampered non-array can't make `.map` throw.

**Two-icon header + reveal-on-demand (mirrors `ths` search toggle):** the action column `<th>` (`data-col="action"`) holds only TWO always-visible icon buttons — maximize (`open_in_full`/`close_fullscreen`) + settings (`tune`) — in a compact `inline-flex items-center justify-center gap-1` group, `@include`d from `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/dms/toolbar.blade.php`. The settings button toggles `openSettings` (on the `dms()` Alpine data, alongside `maximizeMixin`'s `max`/`toggleMaximize`), which reveals a top row `<tr x-show="openSettings" x-collapse>` as the FIRST row in `<thead>` — a single `<th colspan="7">` holding the THREE other controls (density → `view_compact`/`view_comfy`, reset-sort → `restart_alt`, columns → `view_column`+popover) right-aligned in an `inline-flex` box. This is the same shape as `ths/ticket-table.blade.php`'s search toggle (maximize + search in the action header; search click opens a top row) — DMS just swaps "search" for "settings" and the row content for the three view-adjustment buttons instead of a search input. **No search button/row on DMS** — search stays in the floating `x-ui.forms.filters` panel (`dms/filters.blade.php`, included by `dms.blade.php`), untouched. The action `<th>` no longer carries its text label (self-evident from its tbody action buttons), same as ticketing's action header showing only its button group. This replaced the prior spread of independently absolutely-positioned `right-2/11/20/[7.25rem]` 36px buttons floating at the card's top-right.

The col-visibility button + popover share one `x-data="{ open: false }" @click.away="open = false"` wrapper (button inside, so the toggle click never trips `@click.away`); popover is `absolute left-0 top-11` off the wrapper (opens rightward into the table, since the button sits at the row's left end — `right-0` would overflow off-table), with a «نمایش همه» reset + a red count badge for `hidden.length`. The seven column-toggle rows are generated by a `@foreach` over a `[key => label]` array (`title`→عنوان سند … `action`→مشاهده و تایید, defined in the settings-row `@php` block) so any class/behavior change applies uniformly — one block, not seven. `$sortIsDefault` (for the reset-sort disabled state) is also computed in that `@php` block from the parent Livewire `$sort`/`$sortDir` inherited via the view scope.

**Why mask, not reorder:** reorder on this Livewire-morphed, Alpine-popover-bearing table has no minimal+safe path — moving `<td>`s needs per-cell `wire:key` + a post-morph re-apply (else content corruption / every popover snapping shut on each update), and a CSS-`order` flex refactor fights the tuned layout (widths, `first:`/`last:` corners, status `inset` shadow, `overflow-x-auto`). Visibility sidesteps all of it.
