These columns have no markup and no XSS surface — the risk is a **shape mismatch** (a nested array landing where a scalar is expected), which crashes a typed Livewire `Form` property (`data_set()` throws `TypeError` on a typed `?string`) or a hard-typed helper (`superClean(?string $text, ...)`) rather than leaking anything. Guard at the read boundary by coercing (not silently dropping, if the array's index alignment feeds vote counts or similar — see `Feed::normalizePollChoices()`) any non-scalar value to a joined string before it reaches a scalar-typed consumer. This is a distinct bug class from the two above — no sanitizer needed, no HTML involved, just defensive type coercion. Reference: `About::mount()` + `ProfileFormPresenter::aboutMe()` (`about_me`), `Feed::extractPollSettings()`/`normalizePollChoices()` (`poll_options`).

**Rule of thumb:** HTML column, single author (admin only) → plain `{!! !!}`. HTML column, dual author (admin RichEditor + user plain-text form) → `Str::sanitizeHtml()` then `{!! !!}`. Any column (HTML or not) shown as a short excerpt/snippet/notification string → `superClean()`. Array/json column feeding a typed scalar consumer → a shape-normalize guard at the boundary, unrelated to sanitization. **Plain-text column (Textarea, never HTML) shown in FULL (not a clamped/excerpt preview) → `{{ $value }}` inside a `whitespace-pre-wrap` container** — Blade's `{{ }}` escapes (XSS-safe) and `whitespace-pre-wrap` preserves the newlines/paragraphs the user typed (HTML collapses `\n` to a space by default, so without it a multi-paragraph Textarea value renders as one run-on line). Do NOT `strip_tags()` a plain-text full render — that's a defensive no-op that needlessly drops newlines; escape + pre-wrap is the correct pair. **Comment/reply bodies → `{!! renderComment($content) !!}`** (`app/Helpers/index.php`: entity-decode → collapse tabs → `Str::limit` → `**bold**` markdown → `Str::sanitizeHtml()` → `nl2br(, false)`) — the canonical single helper for user-entered comment text, used by BOTH the user-panel feed comment thread (`tab/feeds/comments.blade.php`) and the admin Filament feed comment views (`filament/resources/feed/comment-thread.blade.php` + `comment-reply.blade.php`); never raw `{!! $content !!}` (stored-XSS + dropped newlines) and never `superClean()` (drops newlines). **Mention-highlighted activity body** (`MentionResolver::render()` → `e($body)` + `<span>` mention chips, NO `nl2br`) → render `{!! $entry['body_html'] !!}` inside a `whitespace-pre-wrap` container (the `e()` escape keeps literal `\n`; pre-wrap surfaces them — don't add `nl2br` to `MentionResolver::render()` or you double-break consumers that already pre-wrap). Audit (2026-08-27) applied pre-wrap/`renderComment` to: `ads/grid.blade.php` (experience/skill/extra — Textarea fields, were `strip_tags`), `channel/info.blade.php`, `reservation/image.blade.php`, `suggestion/detail.blade.php` (description `<p>` + review comments/actions), `project/activity.blade.php`, admin `feed/comment-thread.blade.php` + `comment-reply.blade.php`; `FeedPresenter::contentHtml()` switched `superClean(PHP_INT_MAX)` → `Str::sanitizeHtml()` (feed content is RichEditor HTML — superClean was discarding all `<p>`/`<br>` formatting). Excerpt/clamped previews (`ads/grid` certificate `line-clamp-3`, `taskboard/card` description `line-clamp-2`, `posts/*` cards, search snippets, notification strings) correctly KEEP `superClean()`/`strip_tags` — whitespace collapse is intended there. Admin-side mirror of the same rule (Infolist `TextEntry` pre-wrap) is in `filamentPattern.md` "Infolist TextEntry for multiline free-text → white-space: pre-wrap".

**RichEditor text-color marks only paint inside a `.rich-colors` container — add the class to any full render of a Filament `RichEditor` field.** Filament's `TextColor` stores color as `<span class="color" data-color="<key>" style="--color:<key|hex>;--dark-color:<key|hex>;">`. The Tiptap editor's `renderHTML` resolves `data-color` against the live `textColors` map → emits `--color:<hex>` when the key is in the map, falling back to `--color:<key>` (invalid CSS) when it isn't — so the ADMIN edit form shows the color (Tiptap re-resolves), but a STATIC render of the raw stored HTML (user panel) evaluates `--color:<key>` which the browser drops. The user panel does NOT load Filament's `theme.css` (only admin `core/filament.css` does), so the consuming `.color { color: var(--color) }` rule + a `data-color`→hex map live in their OWN stylesheet `resources/css/core/color.css` (imported by `app.css`), keyed off a single generic class `.rich-colors` — extensible to any module. The map covers all 30 preset keys (4 project-custom `primary/success/warning/danger` hex pairs + Filament's 26 `TextColor::getDefaults()` oklch pairs) via `.rich-colors span.color[data-color="<key>"]{--color:…;--dark-color:…}` with `!important` to override the invalid inline `--color:<key>`; `<mark>` highlight is styled there too; dark mode via `:root.dark .rich-colors span.color{color:var(--dark-color)}` (`:root.dark` works because `theme-manager.js` toggles `.dark` on `documentElement`). Custom (non-preset) colors store the hex directly in `data-color`/`--color` and pass through untouched. **Convention: any user-panel blade that full-renders a RichEditor HTML column (`{!! !!}` or `Str::sanitizeHtml()` or `x-html`) adds `rich-colors` to that container** — then color spans + `<mark>` paint. Currently wired (2026-08-27): `posts/details.blade.php` (body `post-body rich-colors` — keeps `post-body` for table/inherit layout; title hero `<h1 … rich-colors>` via `renderInline`), `tab/posts/grid.blade.php` (card title `<h4 … rich-colors>` via `renderInline`), `tab/feeds/item.blade.php` (`rich-colors`), `profile/onboarding.blade.php` (`onb-prose rich-colors`, 5 containers — `onb-prose` keeps its own heading/list typography; `rich-colors` adds only color/mark, NOT the `:where(inherit)` neutralize which is `post-body`-only and would regress onb-prose heading colors), `tab/faqs/cards.blade.php` (answer `prose prose-sm … rich-colors`; question `<h3 … rich-colors>` via `renderInline` + `highlightHtml`), `tab/reports/modal.blade.php` (`prose prose-lg … rich-colors`), `suggestion/detail.blade.php` (description `<p … rich-colors>`), `tab/feeds/comments.blade.php` (comment `<p … rich-colors>`). NOT candidates: `channel/messages`, `contact/messages`, `project/team-chat`, `project/activity` — those render plain-text `body_html` (MentionResolver::render = `e($body)` + chips, or `nl2br(e())`), not RichEditor HTML; `authority/list.blade.php` shows `duty` only as a `superClean` snippet (no full rich render); `faqs/list.blade.php` question stays `superClean` + `highlight($el.textContent)` (dense search-list row, `truncate` — color low-value and `highlight()` is text-only by design). When adding a new RichEditor field to the user panel, add `rich-colors` to its full-render container and (if it introduces a new preset color key not in `color.css`) extend the map there.

**`renderInline(?string $html, int $limit = 0)`** (`app/Helpers/index.php`) — for a RichEditor field rendered inside an **inline/heading context** (`<h1>`-`<h4>`, card titles, any element where a block `<p>`/`<ul>` would break nesting or add unwanted margins) that must still honor the admin's inline color/bold/italic/underline/link marks. It strips to an inline allow-list (`<span><a><strong><em><u><b><i><br>`) on the full HTML first, then — when `$limit > 0` — truncates counting **visible text only** via a quote-aware tag split (`preg_split('/(<(?:[^>"\']|"[^"]*"|\'[^\']*\')*>)/u', …, PREG_SPLIT_DELIM_CAPTURE)`, guarded against `false` on bad UTF-8), never splitting a tag, then `Str::sanitizeHtml()` heals any left-open tags and neutralizes XSS — output is safe inside `{!! !!}`. Use it instead of `superClean()` when the field is a RichEditor column whose color/bold you want to preserve (superClean strips ALL tags → color lost); use `superClean()` for plain-text excerpt/snippet surfaces where formatting is intentionally discarded. Companion `renderInline` + `rich-colors` is the title/question pattern: `renderInline` keeps the color span, `rich-colors` on the heading container makes `.rich-colors span.color[data-color=…]` resolve the key→hex. For search-highlighted headings, pass the HTML through the Alpine `highlightHtml(@js($html))` helper (in `resources/js/components/alpine/data/faq.js`) — it splits on tags and highlights only text nodes, preserving color spans; do NOT use `highlight($el.textContent)` which reads escaped textContent and strips tags.

## Page-level primary tab selector → always `#[Url(as: 'tab')]` (consistency rule)

Any full-page routed Livewire component whose primary UI is the shared `x-ui.buttons.tab-selector` component must bind its `$activeTab` (or equivalent) property with `#[Livewire\Attributes\Url(as: 'tab')]`, using that exact key name everywhere — never a module-specific alias. This makes every module's tab state shareable, bookmarkable, and refresh-safe, and keeps `?tab=` a single predictable convention across the whole user panel rather than N different ad-hoc ones.

Currently wired: `Dashboard\Tabs` (home/post/feed/calendar/status/gallery/reports/links/faqs), `Profile\Main`, `Reservation\Main`, `Dms\Main`, `Authority\Main`, `Ths\Main`, `TaskBoard\Main`, `Energy\Main`, `Project\Main` (added 2026-08-26). The first eight were audited together (2026-08-03) — before that, only 4 had it and 4 didn't, for no functional reason; the other four were missing it by omission, not by design, and adding it required no other code changes since each already reads `$this->activeTab` reactively (`when($this->activeTab === ..., ...)` in query builders, `#[Computed]` methods, or Alpine `x-show="activeTab==='x'"` via `@entangle(...).live`) rather than only setting derived state inside `switchTab()` — so a value arriving via URL hydration (which runs before `mount()`) behaves identically to a value arriving via a click.

**`Project\Main` is the one module where URL hydration order genuinely matters and is already handled — do not "simplify" it.** `selectProject()` unconditionally resets `$this->activeTab = 'activity'` as part of clearing all per-project tab state, which would clobber a `#[Url]`-hydrated `tab=teamChat` value the instant a project is selected via `focusRecord()`'s `mountFocusOnRecord()` hook (property hydration happens before that hook runs, so hydration always loses to `selectProject()`'s reset). This is fixed, not by the `#[Url]` binding itself, but by `focusRecord()` explicitly re-applying `$this->switchTab((string) request()->query('tab', ''))` *after* `selectProject()` succeeds — routing through the same whitelist/side-effect method a manual click would use, rather than a raw reassignment. The `#[Url]` binding's actual payoff here is narrower than the other eight modules: it makes manually-clicked tab changes reflect in `?tab=` (shareable once combined with a `?open=` deep-link), while the deep-link-correctness itself still depends on the explicit `focusRecord()` re-application, not on attribute hydration order. Regression-locked by `test_project_focus_record_honors_tab_query_param_when_present` / `test_project_focus_record_defaults_to_activity_tab_when_tab_param_absent`.

**Exception — never `#[Url]`-bind a component that isn't a routed page.** `ReleaseRequest\Main` deliberately does NOT get this treatment: it has no dedicated route (no `Route::get(...)` targets it) — it's a modal (`components/dashboard/modal/release.blade.php`) embedded in the navbar (`components/dashboard/navbars/top.blade.php`) and rendered on every page. Binding its `$activeTab` to `?tab=` would write that query param onto whatever unrelated page the user currently has open the moment the modal renders, polluting URLs app-wide. Before adding `#[Url]` to any tab property, confirm the component is mounted via its own `Route::get()` entry, not included globally in a layout/navbar.

**Don't extend this to secondary/nested tab-like state.** `Ths\Main::$modalTab` (its ticket-detail modal's request/response toggle, also driven by the same `x-ui.buttons.tab-selector` component via an overloaded `switchTab()`) and `Reservation\Main`'s history sub-tabs are intentionally NOT URL-bound — they're transient, modal/sub-view-local state, not the page's primary navigable view. Only the one property that represents "which top-level page section is the user looking at" qualifies.

## `x-ui.buttons.tab-selector` route-mode + `x-ui.decor.status-pill` — folding the `tasking/` partials

The shared `x-ui.buttons.tab-selector` (originally in-component `wire:click="switchTab(...)"` only) gained a **route mode** so it can also drive cross-page navigation tabs without a per-module partial. A tab carrying a `route` key renders as `<a href wire:navigate>` (active = non-navigable `<span>`); the component root adds `x-data="{ navigating:false }"` and a `fixed inset-0` overlay (`x-ui.loaders.spin-badge`) **only when at least one tab has a `route`** — so the 9 existing button-mode consumers (no `route` key) are byte-identical: no overlay, no `x-data`, no extra DOM. An optional `unread` int on any tab (route or button) renders the `⁹⁹⁺`-capped badge (active = `on-primary/15`, inactive = `tertiary-container`). A `disabled` route tab renders a non-navigable lock `<span>` (same disabled vocabulary as the button branch). `wire:key="tab-{{ $tab['id'] }}"` on every branch keeps morphdom stable across `condition`/`disabled` toggles. This replaced the two near-duplicate `livewire/dashboard/tasking/switch-tabs.blade.php` includes in `taskboard.blade.php` (`active-tab="tasks"`) and `project.blade.php` (`active-tab="projects"`).

**Responsive horizontal scroll (2026-08-27).** The container was `overflow-hidden` with `w-fit`, which clipped any tab strip wider than its parent — on a 5-tab module the rightmost tabs simply vanished on phone widths. The container now carries `overflow-x-auto overflow-y-hidden max-w-full` (still `w-fit`): `w-fit` keeps it content-width and centered when tabs fit (desktop unchanged), `max-w-full` caps it to the parent on overflow, `overflow-x-auto` lets the strip swipe-scroll, and `overflow-y-hidden` preserves the prior silent vertical clipping of the active tab's drop-shadow (so no stray vertical scrollbar appears). The `buttonBaseClass` default gained `shrink-0 whitespace-nowrap` so flex children keep their natural width and scroll instead of squishing — tabs that should not scroll-stub stay full-width. Two consumers (ths, reservation) override `button-base-class` with `flex-1 min-w-[140px]` and the container with `!w-full`; they don't inherit `shrink-0` but the `min-w-[140px]` floor already prevents shrink, so they now scroll on narrow viewports instead of clipping (a bonus) and distribute evenly when they fit (unchanged). **Rule: a multi-tab `tab-selector` must scroll on overflow, never clip — do not reintroduce `overflow-hidden` on this component, and don't set a fixed `w-fit` without `max-w-full`.**

The `tasking/status-pill.blade.php` partial (bare `border` span) became **`x-ui.decor.status-pill`** — a token-driven reusable pill with zero presenter coupling: pass `:state="$presenter->columnState($status)"` (or `title`+`color` props). It maps the presenter's `color` (`primary/secondary/tertiary/error`) to the same container/on-container/ring token vocabulary used by `taskboard/legend.blade.php`'s `$chipClasses`, adds a pulsing color-dot, `ring-1 ring-inset`, `shadow-sm`, and keeps `animate-status-morph`. Used by `taskboard/card.blade.php` (`:state="$colState"`) and `project/report.blade.php` (`:state="$presenter->columnState($row['status'])"`). The `livewire/dashboard/tasking/` folder is deleted. **Rule:** a cross-page tab strip = `tab-selector` with `route` tabs (one component, not a per-module partial); a status label = `x-ui.decor.status-pill` (token-driven, not a bare border span).

## `x-ui.decor.progress-ring` — reusable donut, not a per-view SVG or linear bar

A token-driven circular-progress component (2026-08-25), added when TaskBoard/Project's linear progress bars and plain percent text were replaced with donut visuals across four surfaces: `project/sidebar.blade.php` (beside each project name), `project/report.blade.php` (per-row completion column), `taskboard/form.blade.php` (checklist tab header, replacing both the old `N/M` badge and the linear bar), and `taskboard/card.blade.php`'s checklist chip. Props: `percent` (0–100, clamped), `size`/`stroke` (px), `color`/`track-color` (raw CSS value, e.g. `var(--md-sys-color-primary)`), `label` (override the default `{percent}٪` — e.g. pass a `done/total` fraction string for a checklist ring). Pure SVG (`stroke-dasharray`/`stroke-dashoffset`, `-rotate-90` for a 12-o'clock start), no JS, no Chart.js dependency. **Rule: any new completion/progress indicator reaches for this component first — never hand-roll another linear bar or a bare percent span.**

## Title-bar actions slot — the ONE anchor for module-level toggles (card/list view-mode, timeline, date filter)

The `<x-slot:actions>` of `<x-ui.title>` is the single fixed-Y element every user-panel module has. Standardize all module-level view/filter toggles there as compact `w-8 h-8` controls — never as a bottom-of-page `<x-ui.buttons.toggle>` consuming vertical space, and never absolute-positioned over the content (the old Gallery date-filter dropdown sat `absolute left-4 top-10` inside the timeline partial, floating meaninglessly over the rail). Three reusable pieces, all in `resources/views/components/ui/buttons/`:

1. **`<x-ui.buttons.view-toggle />`** — the card/list segmented control (`grid_view`/`view_list` icons). Props default to the established contract: `state="view"`, `action="toggleView"`, so a bare `<x-ui.buttons.view-toggle />` is the whole call. Used by Reports, Posts, Faqs. Backed server-side by `#[Locked] public string $view = 'card'` + a `toggleView(string $view): void` that whitelists `['card','list']` and writes `session(["{module}_view_mode" => $view])`; `mount()` reads that session key back with the same whitelist (defense against a stale/foreign session value). The Alpine `view` property mirrors it: each module's `x-data` factory declares `view: 'card'` and sets `this.view = this.$wire.get('view') || 'card'` in `init()`. The component markup has **no `x-data` of its own** — it's a pure wrapper, so `view` and `$wire` resolve up the Alpine scope chain to the module's `x-data` root. **Rule: never hand-write the two-button segmented control inline again; use the component.** Report/Status were the originals; Posts, Faqs, Gallery's timeline toggle were aligned to it (2026-08-17) so every module that has a card/list duality uses the identical control at the identical pixel. The component was generalized 2026-08-18 to accept a `:modes` array prop (`[['value'=>…,'icon'=>…,'title'=>…], …]`) so the same control drives non-card/list dualities: **Links** `rail`/`launch` (Launchpad grid + Alt-1..9 hotkeys), **Gallery** `filmstrip`/`wall` (CSS-columns masonry reusing the cached `#[Computed] photos` + Fancybox), **Feeds** `filmstrip`/`magazine` (CSS-columns compact cards, click → `focusRecord(id)` to land on the single-feed filmstrip). All three are timeline-only modules that gained a second view this way — zero new queries (both views iterate the same memoized `#[Computed]` collection, `x-show` not `@if`), no new observers, browser-light (CSS `columns-N` + `break-inside-avoid` masonry, no JS layout lib).

2. **`<x-ui.buttons.icon-toggle state="showTimeline" icon="timeline" title="…" />`** — a compact boolean icon toggle (one button, active = primary fill, inactive = surface-container-high + outline). Props: `state` (the Alpine boolean property name), `icon` (a real Material Symbols ligature), `title`. Extra attributes (`x-show`, `x-cloak`) forward through `$attributes->merge(['class' => 'hidden md:flex … rounded-md …'])` — so the Report timeline toggle passes `x-show="view === 'card'" x-cloak` to gate itself to card view (timeline only exists in the cards partial), while Gallery/Feed pass nothing (single-view, always visible on md+). Used by Gallery, Feed, Reports for `showTimeline`. Replaces the old bottom `<x-ui.buttons.toggle alpineState="showTimeline" xText="…">` text toggles.

3. **Gallery date-filter dropdown** — relocated from `gallery/timeline.blade.php` (where it was `absolute left-4 top-10`, computed via a top-of-partial `@php($months = $presenter->months($this->photos))`) into `gallery.blade.php`'s `<x-slot:actions>`: the `@php($months=…)` now runs once at the top of the main view (before `<x-ui.title>`), the dropdown is a `relative` wrapper with a compact `w-8 h-8` `calendar_month` trigger and an `absolute top-full mt-1 left-0 z-50` popover, `x-data="{ open: false }"` local + `@click.outside="open = false"`. `month` lives on the `gallery()` Alpine root (`month: ''`), so the dropdown writes it and the timeline's per-photo `x-show="!month || month === @js(toJalali($photo->event_date,'F Y'))"` reads it — same scope, just moved up. **Rule: a per-module filter that has no cross-module analogue stays inline in its own title-bar slot; only the truly repeated controls (card/list, boolean timeline) earn a reusable component.**

**View containers** for any card/list module: two sibling divs `x-show="view === 'card'"` / `x-show="view === 'list'"`, BOTH carrying `x-cloak` (so neither flashes before Alpine seeds `view`), both always in the DOM (`x-show` toggles CSS, not `@if` — so the shared `#[Computed]` paginator is iterated twice in-memory, zero extra queries, matching Posts grid+list and Reports cards+list). The load-more sits **outside** both view divs so it stays visible in either mode. FAQ's card view is a responsive grid (`grid grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3 gap-4 items-start`) reusing the existing `faqs/accordion` partial as the card (`items-start` so an expanded accordion doesn't stretch its row siblings); FAQ's list view is the compact `faqs/list` rows. Empty state: `@empty` inside each view (card's `@empty` wraps `faqs.empty` in `col-span-full` for the grid).

**Focus interaction** (do not break global search / `FocusOnRecord`): `Faqs::mount()` forces `$this->view = 'card'` (and bumps `perPage`) when `$this->open` is set, and the root `@record-focus.window` handler sets Alpine `view = 'card'` **transiently** (local-only, NOT calling `$wire.toggleView('card')`) — so a `?open={id}` deep-link always lands on the card/accordion view and auto-expands that FAQ, without overwriting the user's saved list preference. Posts' slideover details is a sibling of both view containers, so it opens in either view. **JIT note:** the Tailwind arbitrary-value classes in the components' `:class` ternaries (`bg-[var(--md-sys-color-primary)]` etc.) are LITERAL single-quoted strings in the Alpine expression, never Blade-interpolated into the brackets — safe for the v4 scanner.

**View-toggle consistency invariant (2026-08-18).** Six modules share the ONE `<x-ui.buttons.view-toggle>` control — three via the bare default (card/list): **Reports, Posts, Faqs**; three via `:modes` (non-card/list dualities): **Links** `rail`/`launch`, **Gallery** `filmstrip`/`wall`, **Feeds** `filmstrip`/`magazine`. The invariant every one upholds, by construction: `#[Locked] public string $view = '<default>'` + `toggleView(string $view): void` whitelisting exactly the two modes and writing `session(["{module}_view_mode" => $view])`; `mount()` reads that key back through the same whitelist (stale/foreign value → default); the Alpine factory declares `view: '<default>'` and syncs `this.view = this.$wire.get('view') || '<default>'` in `init()`; both view containers are sibling `x-show="view === '<mode>'" x-cloak` divs iterating the same memoized `#[Computed]` collection (zero new queries, `x-show` not `@if`). The active-button class is one literal ternary in the shared component (`…primary…shadow-sm scale-105` active vs `surface-variant` idle) — pixel-identical across all six. **Rule: any new tab module with a view duality uses this control with a `:modes` prop; never hand-roll a second segmented control.**

**`responsive` + client-state-only `action` (2026-08-27).** The component gained two backward-compatible props. `responsive` (bool, default `false`) swaps the container's `hidden md:flex` for a bare `flex` so the control is visible on mobile too (the default `hidden md:flex` was right for the title-bar slot, where a card/list toggle is redundant on a single-column phone, but wrong for a view that IS the mobile layout — e.g. Project calendar's grid/list toggle sits in its own header row, not a desktop-only title-bar). `action` (string, default `'toggleView'`) is what the `@click` calls server-side after writing `<state> = '<value>'`; the component gates it with a plain falsy ternary `($action ? ";\$wire.<action>('<value>')" : '')`, so any **falsy** `action` means "client-only, no server round-trip". **Project calendar** (`project/calendar.blade.php`) is the first client-state-only consumer: `state="calView" action="" responsive`, with `calView` seeded in the root `x-data="{ calView: 'grid' }"` and two sibling view containers (`x-show="calView === 'grid'"` / `'list'`) — no `#[Locked] $view`, no `toggleView()`, no session persistence, because the calendar's grid/list split is a transient render choice, not a per-user saved preference like the six card/list modules above. Do NOT copy this client-state-only shape for a card/list module — the session-persisted `toggleView` contract is what makes a user's card/list preference survive a page refresh, and calendar is the exception only because its grid/list has no saved-preference semantics.

**`@props` null-override gotcha (hit here).** To get the falsy/client-only `action`, you MUST pass `action=""` (empty string), NOT `:action="null"`. Laravel's `@props` compiles to a second `$$key = $$key ?? $default` loop over the defaults array; `null ?? 'toggleView'` returns `'toggleView'` (null-coalescing keeps the default when the passed value is `null`), so `:action="null"` is silently discarded and the default `'toggleView'` wins — the `@click` then calls `$wire.toggleView(...)`, which 500s with `MethodNotFoundException` on any component that has no `toggleView` (Project calendar hit exactly this). An empty string survives `??` (it is non-null) yet is falsy, so the component's ternary skips the `$wire` call. This is a general Blade rule, not view-toggle-specific: **you cannot override a non-null `@props` default with `null`** — use `""` (or another non-null falsy value) instead.

**Teleported fixed-nav must be gated by `view` (found 2026-08-18).** The filmstrip left/right swipe buttons live in `<template x-teleport="body">`, so they escape the parent `<div x-show="view === 'filmstrip'">` container — `position: fixed` + body-teleport means the parent's `display:none` no longer hides them, and they stayed visible (z-50, viewport-pinned) over the magazine/wall view. Fix: gate the teleported wrapper itself — `x-show="<existingCondition> && view === 'filmstrip'" x-cloak` (Feeds keeps `!maximizedFeed && view === 'filmstrip'`; Gallery just `view === 'filmstrip'`). Teleport preserves the Alpine component scope, so `view` and the `scrollPrev`/`scrollNext` methods remain reachable inside the teleported subtree (the working `@click="scrollPrev"` was already proof); `x-cloak` prevents a pre-init flash before `init()` seeds `view`. **Rule: any `x-teleport="body"` element that is view-specific must carry its own `x-show="view === '<mode>'"` — never rely on the parent view container to hide teleported content.**

**Enriched second-view UX (2026-08-18, browser-light, zero new queries).** **Links Launchpad** (`links/launch.blade.php`): two labeled sections (internal `dataset_linked` / external `public`) each with header chip + count + divider, a keyboard-hint banner «کلیدهای ۱ تا ۹», a running `$hk` counter so the first 9 links across both sections get Alt-hotkeys, letter-avatar fallback (`mb_substr(url_title,0,1)`) for image-less/icon-less tiles, `focus-visible:ring-2` + primary-tint hover. **Gallery Wall** (`gallery/wall.blade.php`): CSS-columns masonry (`columns-2 … lg:columns-5 [column-fill:balance] break-inside-avoid`), always-visible caption footer (title `line-clamp-1` + Jalali date), multi-image `+N` badge (`photo_library`), department chip on hover (reuses eager-loaded `photos()->with('department')`), primary ring hover, Fancybox carousel via hidden `data-fancybox` anchors + the bound-once `fancyboxMixin`. **Feeds Magazine** (`feeds/magazine.blade.php`): CSS-columns compact cards, per-category top color bar (crc32→4-color palette — no `match`, so no `UnhandledMatchError` on dynamic category strings), engagement footer reusing eager-loaded relations (`$feed->comments_count` from `withCount`, `$feed->reactions->count()` on the already-loaded collection, `$presenter->feedFlags($feed)['isPoll']` → `poll` badge — zero new queries), relative time (`toJalaliRelative`) with absolute date as tooltip, `@click="focusFeed(@js($feed->id))"` → lands on the single-feed filmstrip. All three iterate the same memoized `#[Computed]` collection as the primary view; the month-filter `x-show` + `monthFilterMixin` `visibleCount` empty-overlay is shared across both views.

**Standardized `toJalaliRelative` + absolute-time tooltip (2026-08-31).** Feeds Magazine (above) was the only spot pairing relative time with a `title` tooltip; every other `toJalaliRelative()` call site rendered the fuzzy label alone with no way to see the exact moment. Fixed by adding `title="{{ toJalali($date) }}"` (same helper, default `Y/m/d H:i` format, already blank/error-safe) next to every activity/history/log-style `toJalaliRelative()` usage: Project activity tab (both the comment list and the compact history row), TaskBoard card history (`taskboard/form.blade.php`), Tasksheet activity accordion, and the Feeds timeline card (bringing it in line with its own Magazine view). Presence-style "last seen" usages (`contact/header.blade.php`, `tab/status/grid.blade.php`) were deliberately left alone — that's a live status, not a logged event, so an exact timestamp tooltip doesn't carry the same value. **Rule: any new activity/history/log timestamp rendered via `toJalaliRelative()` must carry `title="{{ toJalali($date) }}"` alongside it.**

**Bidi gotcha on that tooltip (2026-08-31, Tasksheet activity accordion).** A native `title` attribute has no markup of its own — its rendering direction is the *host element's* directionality (HTML spec: advisory text uses the associated element's directionality), and every activity list here sits inside a `dir="rtl"` ancestor. Left alone, the tooltip's `1403/05/12 14:30` digit groups render in reversed (RTL) order. Fix: put `dir="ltr"` on the same span that carries `title=`, then re-wrap just the *visible* relative label in a nested `<span dir="rtl">` so the on-screen Persian text ("۵ دقیقه پیش") keeps its normal shape/order — only the invisible tooltip's bidi context flips. Also add `cursor-help` (matches every other native-`title` hover spot in the app — DMS table, Profile header, Suggestion, Project calendar chips). Only applied to Tasksheet's activity accordion so far; Project activity, TaskBoard history, Feeds timeline, and Posts grid still render the tooltip RTL-reversed until the same three-part fix (`dir="ltr"` host + nested `dir="rtl"` label + `cursor-help`) is applied there too.

## `FocusOnRecord` / global search deep-links — the `data-rf` contract and why it fails silently

A search result (from `ContentService`, or a nudge notification's `route(..., ['open' => $id])`) navigates to `?open={id}` and relies on `App\Traits\FocusOnRecord` to actually land the user ON the record, not just on the page. Three parts, all required, verified end-to-end 2026-08-03 across all 17 focus-capable modules:

1. **`#[Url] public ?int $open`** (the trait) is hydrated from the querystring *before* Livewire calls `mount{TraitName}()` — `mountFocusOnRecord()` — **automatically**, for every component using the trait. This is Livewire's own trait-lifecycle-hook convention (any method literally named `mount{TraitBaseName}()`/`boot{TraitBaseName}()`/etc. auto-fires); no explicit call in the component's own `mount()` is needed or present anywhere in this codebase (confirmed on `Ads\Main`, which has no `mount()` at all and still focuses correctly).
2. **The query/listing method must not silently exclude the focused record.** Pagination/filters/limits are for the *normal* browsing view only — every working module bypasses them when `$open` is set, either via a `->when($this->open, fn($q) => $q->whereKey($this->open), fn($q) => ...normal filters...)` pin (`Dms`, `Faqs`, `Authority`) or a `mount()`-time short-circuit that replaces the loaded-ids array with just `[$this->open]` after an explicit visibility check (`Feeds`, `Gallery`: `if ($this->open && $this->getBaseQuery()->whereKey($this->open)->exists()) { $this->photoIds = [$this->open]; ... }`). `Suggestion`/`TaskBoard` take a third, equally valid path — their detail pane/modal (`selected()`/`editTask()`) fetches the record directly by ID, completely independent of the paginated list, so the content always shows even though the list-row highlight (step 3) only fires if that row happens to be in the currently-loaded page.
3. **`recordFocusType()` (defaults to `strtolower(class_basename(static::class))`, override when the class name doesn't match) dispatches a `record-focus` Livewire event**, caught by `resources/js/core/record-focus.js`, which looks for `[data-rf="{type}-{id}"]` in the DOM and scrolls+flashes it (`.record-focus-flash`, retries for ~3s to tolerate lazy/async rendering, then **gives up with zero error, zero console warning** — the URL is already correct, the page already navigated, so nothing *looks* broken, it just silently doesn't scroll/highlight). This is the step that's invisible in casual testing and was found missing in 7 modules on first audit (Dms, Suggestion, TaskBoard, Feeds, Calendar, Faqs, Gallery all had the trait + working step-2 pinning, but their list/card blade template had no `data-rf` attribute at all) — fixed by adding `data-rf="{recordFocusType()}-{id}"` to each module's row/card root element, matching the 10 modules that already had it correctly (Posts, Reports, Links, Credentials, Ads, Authority, Reservation, Contact, Channel, Ths).

**The search Resource's `$type` property (e.g. `PostResource::$type = 'post'`) is cosmetic — it labels the search-result row's icon/group, and is NOT what `record-focus.js` matches against.** The actual wire is component-`recordFocusType()` ↔ blade `data-rf` prefix; these two only need to agree with *each other*, not with any search Resource. `Tab\Posts` (search type `'post'`) still correctly dispatches/matches on `'posts'` (its own default class-derived type) — no bug, just two independent naming schemes that happen to collide in most modules but don't have to.

**Nested-record deep-links (a message inside a conversation/channel) layer a second query param, `focus_msg`, read separately via `request()->query('focus_msg')` inside `focusRecord()`** (mount-only — a later AJAX update won't re-read it) — `ContactMessage`/`ChannelMessage` search results carry both `?open={conversationOrChannelId}&focus_msg={messageId}`. The nested `focusMessage($id)` method must itself bypass the message list's normal load-limit when the target is older than what's currently loaded — both `Contact\Main` and `Channel\Main` do this via a `focusAnchorId`/`focusOlder` pair: when the target is beyond the initial window, `messages()` fetches a fixed window *around* the anchor (`newer: id >= anchor take 6`, `older: id < anchor take focusOlder+1`) instead of just the latest N — verified empirically by seeding 20 messages and focusing the oldest one (which is outside any default 10-message window) in both modules.

**Nested focus double-dispatch (found 2026-08-03, after the "verified end-to-end" pass above — `Contact`/`Channel` only):** `mountFocusOnRecord()` used to *unconditionally* dispatch its own `record-focus` (parent-row type, e.g. `people-{id}`/`channel-{id}`) even when `focusRecord()` had already dispatched a nested one (`message`/`channel-message`) via `focusMessage()`. `record-focus.js`'s global flash-cleanup (`document.querySelectorAll('.record-focus-flash').forEach(remove)`) then wiped the message's flash a beat later to scroll+flash the sidebar row instead — the double jump/flash behind the "record not clear on landing" complaint. Fixed by having `focusRecord()` return `bool` (`true` = "I already dispatched, skip the trait's own dispatch") in `Contact\Main`/`Channel\Main` only; every other module's `focusRecord(): void` still casts to `false` and gets the trait's unconditional dispatch exactly as before — no behavior change outside the two nested-message modules.

**`channel.js`'s local `scrollToMessage()` (in-conversation message-search click) drifted from the `contact.js`/`record-focus.js` reference pattern:** it skipped the global `.record-focus-flash` cleanup sweep (so a previous jump's flash lingered on an old message) and cleared the class via an `animationend` listener — dead code, since `.record-focus-flash` (`dashboard.css`) only defines a `transition`, not a keyframe `animation`, so that event never fires. Aligned with `contact.js`: global sweep + `scrollIntoView` + add the class, no animationend listener.

**`el.style.animation = 'none'` in all three flash call sites is required, not dead code — verified by removing it and reproducing the regression.** Every message row also carries `bubble-enter` (`animation: bubbleIn 0.25s … both`, `dashboard.css`) with a per-row `style="animation-delay: {{ $msg['animation_delay'] }}s"` stagger. `both` fill-mode applies the keyframe's `0%` state (`opacity: 0`) *backwards* for the entire duration of that delay — so a row further down the list (larger delay) sits invisible until its own delay elapses. Setting `el.style.animation = 'none'` on the jumped-to row removes the animation outright, which drops it back to its un-animated computed style (fully visible, normal position) immediately, regardless of where it was in its delay/keyframe cycle. Without it, `scrollToRecord`/`scrollToMessage` can scroll a row into view while it's still sitting in its pre-delay invisible state — reproduced as "scroll happens but the record isn't visible." Keep this line in all three call sites (`record-focus.js`, `contact.js`, `channel.js`).

**Found via this same audit: an unresolved git merge-conflict block (`<<<<<<< Updated upstream` / `=======` / `>>>>>>> Stashed changes`) had been sitting live in `channel/messages.blade.php`**, undetected because it only broke Blade's PHP compilation of the `@class([...])` array + the delete-button `@if` inside a narrow branch that most manual testing never reaches (the focused/anchored older-message render path specifically — normal recent-message viewing recompiles fine since the conflict block still happens to be syntactically-adjacent valid-looking PHP most of the time... in this case it 500'd outright once actually hit). Resolved in favor of "Updated upstream" for both hunks, confirmed correct two ways: (1) the sibling `contact/messages.blade.php` — no conflict, never touched — independently has the exact same `openActionsId` binding and `$this->lastMessageId` comparison; (2) `channelPattern.md` §20 documents `openActionsId` (mobile tap-to-toggle toolbar) as a deliberate, already-shipped feature in the exact shape kept, and `Channel\Main` has zero occurrences of `is_last` (the discarded side's field) anywhere — that side would have silently hidden the delete button for every message, forever. **Rule: `grep -rn "^<<<<<<< \|^=======$\|^>>>>>>> "` across the tree is worth running after any rebase/stash/merge — a leftover marker inside a rarely-exercised branch compiles fine for the common path and only surfaces as a 500 down the narrow one, exactly the kind of thing that hides for weeks.**

## `focus-chip` — the ONE global-search "showing 1 result" indicator (banner variant deleted)

Every `FocusOnRecord`-using module includes `@include('components.dashboard.header.focus-chip')` near its `<x-ui.title>` header — a small dismissible `role="status"` pill (`نتیجهٔ جستجو` + a close button calling `clearFocus()`) shown `@if(!empty($open))`. A second, larger component (`focus-banner.blade.php` — title + subtitle + a `نمایش همه` button) existed in parallel and had drifted onto 8 of the 17 modules (`ads`, `authority`, `dms`, `profile/credentials`, `faqs`, `feeds`, `gallery`, `links`) purely by historical accident, not design — the other 9 already used `focus-chip`. Standardized on `focus-chip` everywhere (2026-08-03): migrated all 8, deleted `focus-banner.blade.php` and its CSS (`.focus-banner*` rules, plus the `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block that only targeted it), and renamed the shared fade-in/pulse keyframes it and `focus-chip` both relied on from `focus-banner-in`/`focus-banner-pulse` to `focus-chip-in`/`focus-chip-pulse` (one unrelated consumer, `.countdown-icon`, reused the pulse keyframe too — updated to match, not a `focus-chip` concept, just a shared animation name). **Rule: a new focus-capable module always gets `@include('components.dashboard.header.focus-chip')` — there is no second option anymore.**

## Deep-link copy button — reuse `<x-ui.buttons.copy>` with a real `route()` URL, no new mechanism

Any card/row that's already `FocusOnRecord`-deep-linkable (i.e. some nudge already builds `route($name, ['open' => $id])` for it — see `TaskNudge`/`SharedEventsNudge` etc.) can expose that same permalink to the user directly via `<x-ui.buttons.copy :text="route(...)" message="..."/>` — zero backend work, since the route + focus-landing mechanism already exists end-to-end. `<x-ui.buttons.copy>` (`resources/views/components/ui/buttons/copy.blade.php`) takes `text` (the string copied) + optional `message` (toast text); don't fight its built-in sizing/color classes with a `class` override that redeclares `rounded-*`/padding — either pass only additive/positioning classes (`opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100`, matching the card's existing hover-reveal convention) or use Tailwind's `!` important-prefix (already an established convention in this codebase — see `dms/table.blade.php`, `taskboard/column.blade.php`) when it must visually match sibling buttons in the same action cluster. Reference: `taskboard/card.blade.php` (`route('tasks', ['open' => $task['id']])`), `tab/calendar/events.blade.php` (`route('dashboard', ['tab' => 'calendar', 'open' => $event['id']])`, both `is_owner` branches — deliberately NOT added to synthetic birthday/anniversary/holiday calendar entries, which aren't real focus-recordable `Event` rows).

## `#[Reactive]` evaluated app-wide (2026-08-28) — not a fit anywhere in this codebase, don't re-propose without a new concrete case

Investigated whether `Livewire\Attributes\Reactive` could replace the `@island`/cross-component-sharing patterns this app already uses (prompted by Project's Phase 2 spike, see `projectPattern.md`). **`#[Reactive]` and `@island` solve unrelated problems** — `@island` scopes a partial re-render within ONE component's own template; `#[Reactive]` re-syncs a mount-prop on a genuinely SEPARATE `<livewire:ChildClass>` instance when the PARENT's own render carries a new value. Confirmed empirically (twice) that `#[Reactive]` doesn't even solve Project's own nested-in-island case — see `projectPattern.md`'s "Phase 2 spike" section for the exact mechanism (the child is never hydrated at all once already-mounted with a stable key, so `#[Reactive]`'s hydrate-time value-patch never fires).

Full app-wide grep for every genuine `<livewire:...>` nested-component call site (excluding `dynamic-component` full-swaps and zero-prop standalone mounts) found exactly two real candidates, both correctly left alone:
- **`Ths\Workspace`** (`ths/modal.blade.php`, keyed `'ticket-workspace-'.$selectedTicket['id']`) — `mount(int $ticketId)` runs `abort_unless(TicketAccessPolicy::canView(...), 403)`. `#[Reactive]` never re-calls `mount()`, so switching tickets on a live instance would silently skip this per-ticket authorization gate — a real security regression, not a style preference.
- **`Suggestion\Detail`** (`suggestion.blade.php`, keyed `'detail-{{ $selectedId }}'`) — draft form state (`$feedbackForm`/`$decisionForm`) must not survive a switch to a different suggestion; `#[Reactive]` preserves PHP object state by design, which here is exactly the wrong behavior.

Channel, Contact, TaskBoard, and DMS have **zero** nested `<livewire:...>` components at all — their `@island` scoping and `ManagesTaskModal`/`ChatComposer`-style shared traits solve genuinely different problems (partial-render cost, cross-*independent*-top-level-component sharing) that never had a `#[Reactive]`-shaped gap to begin with. `#[Reactive]` also has **no performance effect of its own** — it doesn't reduce query count or payload; the only thing a keyed remount vs. a reactive prop trades is PHP object-state preservation, and in every real candidate found, destroying that state on switch is the correct behavior. Don't re-propose adopting `#[Reactive]` anywhere in this app without a genuinely new nested-component case that isn't already covered by the two verdicts above.

## Testing `@island` content — `Livewire::test()->call(...)->assertSeeHtml()` is unreliable; render the shared partial directly

Extending the `@island` rules above with a concrete testing gotcha hit while adding coverage for the Fancybox attachment lightbox (`messaging/message-attachments.blade.php`, included inside both Contact's and Channel's `messages` island): a plain `Livewire::actingAs($user)->test(Main::class)->call('selectContact', $id)->assertSeeHtml(...)` returned an EMPTY `mode:skip` fragment for the island — the call reaches the method and the DB state is correct, but the island's HTML never lands in `$this->html()`, so the assertion fails even though the feature works. This matches Rule 376's mechanism (a non-`$island()`-scoped call carries no island metadata → server treats it as unscoped → `@island` emits a skip fragment) — a test-harness `->call('methodName', ...)` is exactly that unscoped case, same as an Alpine `x-on:click="$wire.method()"` would be. **Don't fight this**: for a Blade partial rendered inside an island, test the partial in isolation — `view('livewire.dashboard.messaging.message-attachments', ['msg' => [...]])->render()` + `assertStringContainsString(...)` — decoupled from island timing/scoping entirely, faster, and it's testing the actual thing that changed. Reserve full-component Livewire tests (as the rest of `ContactTest`/`ChannelTest` already do) for asserting the underlying PHP state/computed data (`$component->instance()->messages()`), never for asserting island-rendered HTML fragments. Reference: `MessagingTest::test_image_attachment_partial_renders_fancybox_grouped_by_message`.

**Hand-written links to a tab MUST use the `#[Url]` alias, never the raw PHP property name.** `#[Url(as: 'tab')]` on `public string $activeTab` means Livewire only ever hydrates from `?tab=` — a hardcoded `href="?activeTab=info"` or `url('/profile?activeTab=info')` is a silent no-op, not an error: the link still resolves, the page still loads, it just never selects the intended tab, and this masks itself completely whenever the target tab happens to equal the property's own default value (exactly why it went unnoticed here — `info` is `Profile\Main`'s default). Found and fixed two live instances doing this (2026-08-03): `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/profile/header.blade.php` (profile-completion nudge) and `resources/views/components/dashboard/navbars/top/account.blade.php` (navbar account dropdown) — both wrote `?activeTab=info` instead of `?tab=info`. Regression-guarded in `tests/Feature/Livewire/Dashboard/ProfileTest.php::test_profile_header_completion_link_uses_tab_query_key_not_active_tab` and `NavbarTest.php::test_account_dropdown_profile_link_uses_tab_query_key_not_active_tab`. Any new hardcoded `href`/`url()` targeting a `#[Url(as: 'tab')]`-bound page must use `tab=`, never the bare property name — `resources/js/components/alpine/data/menu.js`'s nav links already get this right and are the reference to copy.

## TaskBoard module — ambient urgency, priority/rank, archive, TaskDetail propagation, board surfaces, scheme/action-source

The TaskBoard module's ambient-urgency `--urgency` scalar, tier-based rank placement (`rankForPriority`/`CyclePriorityAction`), nullable `archived_at` archive with the `status=done` invariant, `TaskDetail` domino propagation (responsible/department/state → Calendar + Project activity), the board surfaces (state badge/done-gate, collaborator reply access, responsible/department chips+filters, labels multi-select, scheme/action-source filters), and the cascading action-source report filter are documented in the module pattern doc: **TaskBoard module → see `app/Livewire/Dashboard/TaskBoard/taskboardPattern.md`** (§2, §5, §9, §10, §14). The general Livewire-wide rules those features rest on — the `$wire.set(path, value, false)` deferred-set rule (below), the `Object.defineProperties` spread-getter fix (below), the `x-ui.hover-popover` primitive (below), the file-attachment-preview rule (below) — stay in this file as the canonical versions; the module doc cross-references them.

## Shared chat-bubble/message-group traits (`BuildsChatBubbles`/`BuildsMessageGroups`) — Channel/Contact/Project

Project's team-chat tab port pulled the messaging view-shaping logic that used to live only on `ChannelPresenter` (and be hand-duplicated on `ContactPresenter`) into two composable traits, so a third presenter didn't mean a third copy-paste:

- **`App\Traits\BuildsChatBubbles`** (`bubbleRadius`/`attachments`/`linkify`/`replyPreview`) — the four universal, read-tracking-agnostic bubble primitives every chat surface needs. Composed by `ChannelPresenter` (transitively, via `BuildsMessageGroups`), `ContactPresenter`, and `ProjectPresenter` (transitively).
- **`App\Traits\BuildsMessageGroups`** (`use BuildsChatBubbles;` + `messageGroup`/`messages`/`readerSummary`/`mentionify`) — the full date-grouped, per-member-cursor read-receipt + `@mention`-aware shaping pipeline. Composed by `ChannelPresenter` and `ProjectPresenter` only.
- **Contact deliberately does NOT compose `BuildsMessageGroups`** — its `messageGroup()`/`messages()` stay locally defined on `ContactPresenter`. Contact's read model is `read_at`-based (a per-message boolean) rather than Channel/Project's per-member cursor + `readersMap`/`readerSummary` shape, and Contact has no `@mention` feature; forcing it through the shared group-shaping pipeline would mean threading dead `readersMap`/`mentionMap` params through a read-tracking mechanism the module doesn't have. `ContactPresenter` pulls in only the four universal bubble primitives via `BuildsChatBubbles`.

Both extractions are pure — zero behavior change on any of the three presenters.

## Birthday/anniversary occasions — `Profile\HasOccasions` trait, zero extra queries, Status spotlight row + Contact stats tile

Surfaces a colleague's exact-day birthday/work-anniversary (from `profiles.birthdate`/`start_date`) beyond the Calendar module, on the Status board and in the Contact empty-state stats — pure presentation, no new nudge/badge/notification (matches the no-nudge-badge preference).

**Single source of truth — `App\Models\Concerns\HasOccasions` (mixed into `Profile`).** `isBirthdayToday()`/`isAnniversaryToday()` use Carbon's own `isBirthday()` (month+day match, leap-year aware) guarded by the existing `!isTerminated()` check — a distinct mechanism from `App\Services\Menu\Indicators\SpecialDays`' raw SQL `whereMonth`/`whereDay` (that one powers the Calendar menu-dot badge and stays untouched; the two agree on every real date including Feb 29, verified against Carbon's source, but aren't the same code path — this is a presentational feature, not required to share the badge's exact query). `todaysOccasionType(): ?string` returns `'birthday'|'anniversary'|null` (birthday wins the tie if both match). `occasionTone(string $type): array` (static) is the one shared icon/color/label map — `cake`/`--tool-gold-*`/«تولد» and `workspace_premium`/`--tool-sage-*`/«سالگرد کاری» — currently consumed only by the Status spotlight row, kept static/shared so a future consumer never has to invent its own copy.

**Status board (`Tab\Status`)** — `#[Computed] todaysOccasions()` filters the board's own already-loaded `$this->users` collection (which is `visibleOnBoard()`-scoped — Guest-type excluded, same as the rest of the board) and maps each match to `{user, type}`; **zero extra queries**, since `$this->users` is accessed via property syntax from both the grid and this computed, hitting Livewire's per-request memo either way. `status/occasions.blade.php` renders a spotlight row above the filters — gated `@if(count($this->todaysOccasions))`, so it's simply absent on an ordinary day — with a corner badge icon on each avatar and a small `auto_awesome` pulse beside the name, both from `Profile::occasionTone()`. A small count badge (`celebration` icon + count) sits in the page title's `actions` slot, gated the same way, that smooth-scrolls to `#todays-occasions` on click (via an Alpine `x-ref`) — so the signal is still visible even after scrolling past the row itself.

**Contact — both a per-row chip AND an aggregate stat (the per-row signal was removed, then explicitly restored).** `Contact\Main::contacts()` adds `'occasion' => $user->profile?->todaysOccasionType()` to the raw per-row array (the `profile` relation is already eager-loaded by `FetchContactsAction`, so this is zero extra queries too); `ContactPresenter::contact()` passes it straight through into the shaped row the sidebar reads (`$contactList = $p->sidebar($this->contacts, ...)` in `contact/sidebar.blade.php`); `ContactPresenter::totalOccasions(array $contacts): int` separately counts the non-null ones from the *raw* array for `contact/empty.blade.php`'s stats row (همکاران / آنلاین / خوانده‌نشده / «تولد/سالگرد», the 4th tile gated `@if($totalOccasions)` so it never shows a stray "0"). **The per-row UI is a static chip, not an interactive icon/button** — `sidebar.blade.php` renders `Profile::occasionTone($contact['occasion'])` as an always-visible rounded pill (icon + label text) right next to the contact's name, deliberately not wrapped in a `<button>`/click handler: an earlier interactive version (small icon only, native `title=""` tooltip, later a `toast()`-on-tap fix) was removed for being touch-hostile and over-built, then a no-icon-at-all version was tried and rejected as *too* quiet — the always-visible chip is the settled middle ground, needing no interaction to be understood. **Deliberately Contact-only, not Channel** — confirmed with the user rather than assumed, to avoid reintroducing the exact kind of cross-module inconsistency this session's `visibleOnBoard()` audit was about; Channel's `empty.blade.php` did get a matching *visual* restyle of its own pre-existing stats row (SVG-icon grid cards, matching Contact's new stat-tile look) but carries no occasion tile.

**Two reverted attempts along the way, don't repeat either:**
1. **Pinning the occasion person's card to the top of the Status *grid*** (not just the spotlight row) via a wrapping `<div style="order:-1">` plus a `background-image` wash on `<x-ui.decor.status>`. Two real lessons survive even though the grid change itself was reverted (the user wanted the spotlight row to be "enough," not layered onto every card too):
   - **Splitting `@if(...)`/`@endif` across a Blade *component* tag's (`<x-...>`) attribute list breaks its tag compiler** — `syntax error, unexpected token "endif"` — because component tags go through a dedicated attribute-parsing step full HTML tags and `@include`d partials don't. Compute the conditional value as a plain string in `@php` first, then interpolate it as one always-present attribute (`style="{{ $value }}"`, empty string when unset) — the same fix already documented for `@php(...)`-vs-`@forelse` elsewhere in this file.
   - **`<x-ui.decor.status>`'s caller-supplied attributes never reach its true outer grid-item element** — its template explicitly consumes `$attributes` on an *inner* div (`$attributes->except('wire:key')->class(...)`), so anything relying on the component's root being a flex/grid item (like CSS `order`) needs an explicit wrapping `<div>` at the call site instead; the component has no other consumers, but modifying its own template was avoided in favor of a page-local wrapper to keep the blast radius at zero.
2. **An interactive per-row icon (button + click handler) in the Contact sidebar, plus a clickable header count badge.** Built, removed, then partially rebuilt as the *static chip* described above — the interactive/clickable shape specifically is what stays reverted. Two things worth remembering from that detour: (a) a plain `title=""` attribute on a touch-primary surface (this is a mobile-first PWA chat sidebar) technically renders correctly but is functionally invisible — no touch equivalent, ~1s hover-and-hold on desktop — and a `str_contains($html, 'title="...")` test proves the markup is right without proving a human ever sees it; the eventual fix wasn't a better tooltip, it was making the label *always visible text* so no interaction is needed at all. (b) **verify against the file on disk, not against what you last wrote** — this file got hand-edited multiple times mid-session (a `<span title>` → interactive `<button>` fix was silently reverted back to the plain span by a later edit before anyone weighed in on removing it; later, a render check for the restored icon was run *before* the presenter-layer half of the restore had landed, briefly reading as "still broken" when it was actually just sequencing) — re-reading current state before trusting "I already fixed that" mattered more than usual here.

Tests: `tests/Feature/Models/ProfileModelTest.php` (`isBirthdayToday`/`isAnniversaryToday`/`todaysOccasionType`/`occasionTone`, in-memory on `new Profile([...])` — no DB needed, pure logic), `tests/Feature/Livewire/Dashboard/StatusTest.php` (`todaysOccasions` inclusion/exclusion incl. a Guest-type regression test), `tests/Feature/Livewire/Dashboard/ContactTest.php` (the `occasion` key flows through the raw `contacts()` computed; `ContactPresenter::totalOccasions()` counts correctly). Guide: `resources/views/filament/resources/profile/guide/user.blade.php` (admin cross-reference — birthdate/start_date now also drive the Status spotlight row and the Contact stats tile). Legend: `status/legend.blade.php` carries one row (the Contact side needs none — a plain number in an already-visible stats row is self-explanatory, no legend-worthy interaction to document).

## Resource-level scheduling narrows the type-level `ReservationPolicy`, never replaces it — one new validator, no new table

`Resource.metadata` (`available_days`/`time_slots.{start,end}`) adds an *optional, per-resource* day/hour restriction on top of the existing *per-type* `ReservationPolicy` (`AllowedDays`/`AllowedHours` validators, `reservation_policies` table keyed by `resource_type`) — additive-only by construction: a resource that doesn't define these keys behaves exactly as before (verified against all 124 pre-existing `resources` rows, none of which set them).

**`App\Services\Reservation\Validators\ResourceSchedule`** (implements `BookingRule`, registered in `ValidationService::$bookingRules` right after `AllowedHours`) reads `$context->resource->metadata` directly — `BookingContext` already carries the full `Resource` model, so no `BookingContext`/`ValidationService::getPolicies()` changes were needed; the type-level policy array and the resource-level metadata are two independent sources a validator can read side-by-side. Mirrors `AllowedDays`/`AllowedHours`'s own shape: day check via `strtolower($context->start->englishDayOfWeek)` against `available_days` (empty array or absent = no restriction), hour check skipped entirely when `$context->isFullDay` (matching `AllowedHours`'s existing full-day bypass — `car` is a full-day type, so only its day restriction applies, never its time window). New error codes `ReservationError::ResourceDayNotAllowed`/`ResourceHourNotAllowed` (ERR-023/024), `'policy' => '—'` in the legend (resource-level, not policy-level, matching `ResourceInactive`/`ResourceTaken`'s convention). Deliberately **does not** replicate `AllowedHours`'s overnight-wraparound handling — a per-resource window is a narrower, simpler concept (e.g. "this room is open 9–17") and overnight per-resource availability wasn't asked for; add it if it's ever actually needed, don't build it speculatively.

`Resource::scopeAvailable()` gets the same narrowing as a pre-filter (`whereJsonContains('metadata->available_days', $day)` — the query builder can express JSON containment natively, no `whereRaw` needed — plus the equivalent time-window `where()`, both `whenNull`-guarded so resources without the keys are untouched), so an unavailable-today resource doesn't even appear as a bookable card; `ResourceSchedule` is the same double-enforcement `AllowedDays`/`AllowedHours` already do (UI-filter feeder *and* hard validator) applied one level down.

**`Resource::statusSummary()`** is the one shared, type-aware formatter — seat → `"طبقه X • واحد Y • داخلی Z"`, spot → `"جای پارک {name} • طبقه X • کارت Y"`, meeting/car → capacity + available-days + time-window, falls back to `labeled_name` when metadata is empty. Consumed by `User::primeTodaysDeskCache()` (now also scans `ResourceType::Meeting`, not just `Seat`/`Car`) via a new `$todaysStatusSummaryCache`/`getTodaysStatusSummary()` pair, wired into the Status board tooltip (`status/grid.blade.php`) — but the pre-existing raw-extension cache (`getTodaysDeskExtension()`) is kept **completely separate and untouched**, because it also feeds the adjacent `tel:` call link; `status_summary` is a multi-part display string, never a dialable number, so collapsing the two caches into one would have broken click-to-call.

**Gotcha — a `Livewire::test()` `fillForm()`→`fillForm()`→`call('create'|'save')` chain cannot reliably assert the exact persisted value of a `->visible(fn (Get $get) => …)`-gated dot-notation form field**, even with an explicit `->dehydrated()` override mirroring the same condition. See filamentPattern.md rule 69 for the full root cause (a static, closure-object-keyed Filament visibility cache that goes stale across the chain's multiple simulated request cycles, all within one PHP process) — this is a test-harness artifact, not a real save-path bug (confirmed against 124 real, correctly-persisted production rows using the identical `->visible()` pattern). Test that layer via `assertHasNoFormErrors()` + row-existence only; test the actual persisted-value logic against directly-constructed/factory models instead.

Tests: `tests/Feature/Services/ReservationValidatorsTest.php` (`ResourceSchedule` — day/hour pass/throw, empty/absent metadata no-ops, full-day bypasses hour but not day), `tests/Feature/Models/ResourceModelTest.php` (`scopeAvailable` narrowing + no-op-when-absent, `statusSummary` per type, `formattedMetadata` for the new keys), `tests/Feature/Models/UserModelTest.php` (`getTodaysStatusSummary`, meeting inclusion, `resetTodaysDeskCache` clears the new cache too), `tests/Feature/Livewire/Dashboard/StatusTest.php` (tooltip prefers the new summary), `tests/Feature/Filament/ResourceResourceTest.php` (new fields' admin-form wiring, `card` not `card_number`).

## Org chart (Status) — rank-derived in-memory tree, no `manager_id` column

The Status tab's "نمای ساختاری" view (`status/chart.blade.php` + `status/node.blade.php`, toggled by `Status::$viewMode` via `switchViewMode()`) draws the org hierarchy **without any stored reporting relation** — `profiles` has no `manager_id`/`reports_to`, so reporting lines are **inferred from rank**, not eager-loaded. The tree is built entirely in-memory over the **same `users()` `#[Computed]`** the grid view uses (`with(['profile.department','profile.details'])`, `visibleOnBoard()`, rank-ordered) — **zero extra queries** for the chart: 5 total for 12 depts + 1 apex + a 44-member bucket (no N+1; `Model::preventLazyLoading()` stays silent).

- **`#[Computed] orgTree()`** groups `$this->users` by `profile.department.code` (no-department users → `'__none__'` → «سایر» bucket), `sortBy(rank())` within each group, **hoists rank-1** (chairman/ceo, `HasProfileHierarchy::RANKS === 1`) to an `apex` collection, and picks each dept's `head` as the min-rank *remaining* member (the apex exec's old dept shows its next-senior as head). Departments sort by head-rank then label. **Do NOT call `isDeptHead()` in the loop** — it runs an `exists()` query per user (N+1); the in-memory min-rank over the eager-loaded collection is the de-facto head, query-free.
- **`#[Computed] aboutMeDirectReports()`** (server-side, also over the loaded collection — zero queries) gives every node a rank-derived report set: apex → all dept heads; dept head → its `members`; non-head member → same-dept members with strictly greater rank number. Rendered in `about-me.blade.php` as a «زیرمجموعه‌ها» section that **reuses the `node` partial** as clickable chips (so the payload-building `Js::from($aboutPayload)` lives in exactly one place — the node — no triplication across grid/node/modal).
- **Collapsible departments persist across re-renders** via a hybrid: Alpine `x-show="open"` gives the smooth toggle, and `Status::$collapsedDepts` (public array) + `toggleDept(string $code)` persist it server-side so opening the about-me modal (a Livewire round-trip that re-renders the component) does NOT reset collapse state. `x-data="{ open: {{ in_array($code, $collapsedDepts, true) ? 'false' : 'true' }} }"` seeds from server; the toggle does `open = !open; $wire.toggleDept(@js($code))` (optimistic local flip + persist). `toggleDept` reindexes with `array_values` after `unset` — a sparse numeric-keyed array serializes to a JSON **object** in the Livewire snapshot, which would break any JS that treats it as a list; reindex keeps it a real array. `@js()` (not `{{ }}`) for any PHP→JS interpolation inside Alpine attributes — `{{ }}` HTML-escapes but does not JS-escape quotes/backslashes.
- **Node partial** (`status/node.blade.php`) is rank-tiered: `match($tier)` → apex/head/member card width + avatar size + elevated `color-mix` shadow (no heavy backdrop blur); `match($user->profile?->position)` → Material Symbols position icon + M3-container chip, returning **full literal class strings** (JIT-safe — never Blade-interpolate a var inside `bg-[var(--…-{{ $c }}-container)]`, the scanner can't see it). Position icons are user-panel Material Symbols (`stars`/`business_center`/`manage_accounts`/`supervisor_account`/`rule`/`military_tech`/`build`/`person`), NOT the `Position` enum's `getIcon()` (Heroicons — admin-only). The node reuses the grid's dual-trigger pattern: `wire:click="openAboutMe(id)"` (server: sets `aboutMeUserId`/`showAboutModal`, unsets `aboutMeSkills`+`aboutMeDirectReports`) + `x-on:click.stop="$dispatch('open-about-me', {user, aboutMe})"` (client: fills the modal's Alpine `user`/`aboutMe`) — complementary, not a double-open; the toggle button's `.stop` suppresses both when collapsing. Every `@foreach` loop item carries a `wire:key` (apex/head/member/dept-wrap) — mandatory with per-dept `x-data` scope or morphing misattributes collapse state.
- **Connector CSS** is a scoped `<style>` block (`orgc-` prefix, single render — the node partial has no style) inside `chart.blade.php`: apex row → `orgc-vline` → `orgc-depts--bus` (a `border-top` bus) → per-dept `::before` drop → `orgc-members` rail + `orgc-member + orgc-member::before` stubs. `.orgc-depts` is `flex-wrap:nowrap; overflow-x:auto` (horizontal scroll on small screens, single-row bus stays clean); members stack vertically under each head. `x-show="open"` on `.orgc-members` hides the branch and its pseudo-element connectors together (display:none removes them). `<style>` blocks in partials are an established pattern here (DMS col-visibility mask) — acceptable when the rules are fiddly pseudo-elements Tailwind utilities can't express.
- **Legend**: `status/legend.blade.php` gained a `structure` tab (non-obvious nuggets: rank-derived not stored, de-facto head, tier weighting, collapse persistence, click→modal+زیرمجموعه‌ها, filters apply to the chart). The help button/modal title broadened from «راهنمای نشان‌های سطح مهارت» to «راهنمای وضعیت و ساختار سازمانی» since the legend now covers both.

### Department-to-department hierarchy (`level`/`subordinate_to`) + unit/section clustering

The tree-building logic above (`orgTree()`, `aboutMeDirectReports()`) was extracted out of `Status.php` into `App\Livewire\Dashboard\Tab\Presentation\StatusPresenter` (mirrors `CalendarPresenter`'s shape: pure methods taking explicit params — `orgTree(Collection $users)`, `directReports(array $tree, User $me)` — no DB calls, no reliance on `$this` component state). `Status.php`'s computed methods are now thin delegations; `#[Computed]` memoization still applies since the wrapping method, not the presenter, carries the attribute.

Two new `departments` columns extend the apex/rank logic (untouched) with a *department*-level hierarchy, orthogonal to the personnel-rank one:
- **`level`** (tinyint, 0/1/2, default 0 — pre-existing rows backfilled to `1` by the migration so nothing already visible silently disappeared). `0` = hidden from the chart entirely (default for new departments — an explicit admin opt-in to show, not opt-out).
- **`subordinate_to`** (nullable `string(10)`, self-FK on `departments.code` — the same code every other `department_id` FK already points at, not `departments.id`). A department with `subordinate_to` set nests under that parent's card instead of getting its own top-level slot; the model's `saving()` hook auto-bumps a stray `level = 0` to `1` whenever `subordinate_to` is set (mirrors the Filament form's reactive correction) and rejects self-reference/cycles via `Department::wouldCreateCycle()` (walks `Department::getCachedModels()` — the existing 1-year model cache, auto-bumped by `ModelCacheServiceProvider` on every save, so no extra query and no staleness).

`StatusPresenter::orgTree()` builds `['apex' => ..., 'level1' => [...roots], 'level2' => [...roots], 'byCode' => [...flat lookup]]` (replaces the old flat `'departments'` key — `aboutMeDirectReports()` now reads `byCode`). Per department node: `head`/`allRemaining` are unchanged (min-rank remaining member; full remaining collection, used only for the about-me "direct reports" lookup so that page's behavior is byte-for-byte identical to before), plus new `members` (direct department members with no `unit` set — Pattern A), `units` (array of `{name, members, sections}` clusters — Pattern B, sourced from the *existing* `profile_details` EAV `unit`/`section` keys via `detailsMap()`, **not new schema** — `ProfileDetailCatalog` already defined these as admin-only fields; only the `Status::users()` eager load changed, narrowing `profile.details` to `whereIn('key', ['unit','section'])` since that's the only two keys this module ever reads), and `children` (nested subordinate departments, built recursively). A department whose `subordinate_to` points at a hidden (`level=0`) or absent parent gracefully falls back to a root slot at its own `level`, rather than disappearing — no cycle-guard is needed at render time since writes are already guarded, but the recursive tree-builder still carries a `$seen` set as defense-in-depth against a hand-edited/imported cycle.

Rendering: `chart.blade.php` merges `level1`+`level2` into a single `.orgc-depts` row (**not** two separate blocks — an earlier two-row attempt was explicitly rejected: "next tier does not mean next block"). A level-2 dept gets an `orgc-dept--tier-2` class scoped `.orgc-depts > .orgc-dept--tier-2` (root-row only — `topLevel`, default `true`, passed `false` on the partial's own recursive `@include` for `children`, so a level-2 subordinate nested inside its parent's `.orgc-dept-children` box doesn't also get this): `margin-top` push-down plus a `top`/`height` override on the *same* `.orgc-depts--bus .orgc-dept::before` connector (not a second pseudo-element) so the tail just gets longer, matching `node.blade.php`'s apex/head/member size/offset-only tiering. Row order is not naive array concatenation: the `@php` block searches `level1` for the adjacent-pair split point closest to the middle where both neighbors have `count === 1` (a "small, sparse chart" case) and splices `level2` in there instead of appending it at the end, falling back to the plain midpoint if no such pair exists — combined with `justify-content: flex-start; justify-content: safe center;` on `.orgc-depts` (progressive-enhancement fallback pattern — invalid `safe center` in older browsers is dropped, leaving `flex-start`; `safe` avoids the classic symmetric-hidden-overflow-on-both-sides trap when the row doesn't fit and has to scroll). Each row item is the recursive partial `status/dept-node.blade.php` (head + direct members via the unchanged `node` partial, unit/section clusters, then `@include`s itself for `children` — a genuinely different depth concept from level/tier, kept as conventional indentation). The dept-label header renders as a static (non-clickable, no chevron) `.orgc-dept-label--static` div instead of the collapsible `<button>` when `$hasBody` is false (`count === 1`, i.e. only the head, nothing to expand) — a collapse toggle with nothing to collapse is dead affordance. The Alpine `collapsed`/`toggleDept(key)` mechanism in `status.blade.php` was **not changed** — it was already a generic string-keyed map, so composite keys (`dept:{code}`, `dept:{code}:unit:{name}`, `...:section:{name}`) work with zero JS changes.

Tests: `tests/Feature/Models/DepartmentModelTest.php` (defaults, auto-bump, self-reference/cycle rejection), `tests/Feature/Filament/DepartmentResourceTest.php` (form-level level/subordinate_to validation), `tests/Feature/Livewire/Dashboard/StatusPresenterTest.php` (new dedicated file, mirrors `CalendarPresenterTest.php` — tree shape, hidden/level1/level2 placement, nesting, hidden-parent fallback, apex-only chairman/ceo, Management dept, direct/unit/section member placement, zero-extra-query assertion via the established `DB::flushQueryLog()`/`enableQueryLog()`/`getQueryLog()` pattern).

## Calendar Scheduler — `navigationDate` single-state, multi-view, point-pills, zero-migration

The user-panel Calendar (`App\Livewire\Dashboard\Tab\Calendar`) upgrades from a month-grid + selected-day list into a 3-view (month/week/day) scheduler with drag-to-move point-pills — **zero migration, no new columns**. `Event.date` remains the single datetime column (time encoded inside `date`); re-date/re-time reuse the existing `Event::date_jalali` + `date_time_part` virtual setters.

**(a) `navigationDate` single-state** — replaces the old `currentYear`/`currentMonth` pair with one Jalali `Y-m-d` string that drives month/week/day grids alike. `selectedDate` is **retained** (not folded into `navigationDate`): it backs `selectedDayEvents` (Month left-pane shape-lock, the 38-test family) and anchors the Day view; `selectDate(jalaliYmd)` sets **both** `selectedDate` and `navigationDate` and switches `view='day'`. `miniMonthDate` is a **third**, deliberately separate Jalali `Y-m-d` — the mini-month picker's own cursor, kept distinct so its `#[Computed] miniMonthDays()` can't collide with the main grid's `calendarDays()` cache (Livewire v3/v4 `#[Computed]` is per-request memoized off the properties it reads; two grids off one property would share a cache and stale on the wrong one).

**(b) `CalendarRange` immutable DTO** (`app/Values/CalendarRange.php`, new `app/Values/` directory) — `readonly` Carbon `$start`/`$end` + `$view` + `$navigationDate`, built via `::fromNavigation(string, string)`: month → first/last of the Jalali month (00:00 / 23:59:59), week → Saturday-on/before `navigationDate` + 6 days (always 7 days; `hideFriday` is UI-only), day → `navigationDate` 00:00–23:59:59. `jalaliLabel()` returns `F Y` (month), `d – d F Y` or `d F Y – d F Y` (week, month-cross aware), `d F Y` (day). `days()` yields one Carbon per day in range; `contains(Carbon)` is inclusive startOfDay/endOfDay; `weekdayOffset()` is 6 for month (Jalalian `getDayOfWeek()` 0=Sat..6=Fri as leading-empty count) and 0 for week/day. The DTO is pure value — no validation, no DB; `JalaliDateRule` is the separate validation rule.

**(c) `rangeEvents` `#[Computed]`** — delegates to `CalendarPresenter::rangeEvents($nav, $view)` (see (h)); builds `CalendarRange`, queries `Event::with(['shares' => select(id,user_id,event_id,shared_by)->with('sharer:id,name')])->whereBetween('date', [$range->start, $range->end])` + visibility filter (owned OR public OR shared-to-me), groups by Jalali `Y-m-d`. Returns `['byDay' => array<string, event-pill-shape[]>, 'allDayEntries' => allDay-shape[]]`. Each timed pill carries `start_minutes` (`hour*60+minute`) and `duration_minutes` (`$event->duration_minutes ?: Event::DEFAULT_DURATION_MINUTES` — null defaults to 30, the legacy visual height, so pre-migration rows render unchanged). Per day, reservation-linked pills are split out and the timed set is run through **`CalendarLayout::pack()`** (`app/Values/CalendarLayout.php`) — a greedy column-packer: sort by `start_minutes` asc then `duration_minutes` desc (longer-first anchors column 0), assign each pill to the first column whose last end `≤ start`, then walk clusters of overlapping pills and set `span = max(col)+1` for the whole cluster; blade renders `left = col/span`, `width = 1/span`, `top = (start_minutes - 360)*hourHeight/60`, `height = duration*hourHeight/60` (capped to the grid). So overlapping events pack **side-by-side** (not stacked), each sized to its duration — no `+N more` chip. Pills outside `[GRID_START_MINUTES=360, DAY_END_MINUTES=1440)` are dropped from the timed set (pre-06:00 events don't render at negative top). `allDayEntries` (birthdays/anniversaries/holidays) are kept **out** of the time-grid and rendered as all-day banners. Pill shape carries `locked = is_reservation_linked || !is_owner` (read-only → no drag handle, no resize handle).
