**(d) `MoveEventAction` / `ResizeEventAction` guards** (`app/Livewire/Dashboard/Tab/Actions/`, one `execute(...)` each — receives primitives, never `$this`): global `Event::find($id)` (NOT `firstOrFail` — a missing id is `{ok:false, reason:'not_owner'}` not 404); owner check `$event->user_id !== Auth::id()` → `{ok:false, reason:'not_owner'}` (NO `revertTo` — a non-owner gets no cross-user existence oracle) + Persian toast «شما مالک این رویداد نیستید.» (NOT 403/abort — the frontend reverts the pill and toasts); reservation lock via `EventSyncService::isReservationEvent($event)` → `{ok:false, reason:'locked', revertTo}` + «این رویداد از طریق سیستم رزرو مدیریت می‌شود؛ برای تغییر آن به تب رزرو مراجعه کنید.»; `MoveEventAction` strict-validates `dateJalali` via `JalaliDateRule` (leap-aware `CalendarUtils::checkDate`) + `timePart` regex `H:i`; `ResizeEventAction` validates `durationMinutes` `min:15 max:min(480, 1440 - startMinutes)` (the day-end cap is computed from the event's `start_minutes` so a late-night event can't extend past midnight) → `{ok:false, reason:'invalid_input', revertTo}` on failure; `DB::transaction` + `lockForUpdate`; **optimistic-concurrency** compares `$event->updated_at->format('Y-m-d H:i:s')` vs `Carbon::parse($clientMtime)->format('Y-m-d H:i:s')` (Carbon parsed BEFORE the transaction; format-level compare **discards microseconds** — MySQL stores them, format() truncates them, avoiding false-stale on sub-second races) → `{ok:false, reason:'stale', revertTo}` of the current row. Rate-limit 30/min/user (`RateLimiter::attempt`, keys `calendar-move:{userId}` / `calendar-resize:{userId}`) → `{ok:false, reason:'rate_limited'}`. Happy path: `MoveEventAction` re-dates via `date_jalali` and/or re-times via `date_time_part`; `ResizeEventAction` persists `duration_minutes`. Both return `{ok:true, newMtime}`. The Livewire `moveEvent`/`resizeEvent` actions delegate here and share a single `handleGridActionResult(...)` helper that dispatches the success toast on `ok:true`, or the reason-mapped toast + `revert-event-{id}`/`revert-resize-{id}` (window CustomEvent) on `ok:false`.

**(e) Reservation-banner topological immutability** — reservation-linked all-day pills render via the shared `reservation-banner.blade.php` partial as a read-only continuous strip spanning all 7 week columns (`grid-column: 2 / span 7`) or the single day column (full-width, `gridSpan=null`), NOT a movable pill. CSS `grid-column` placement makes it topologically immutable (no drag handle, no `touch-action:none`); the data range always spans 7 days regardless of `hideFriday` (the Friday column is `display:none` UI-only, the grid still reserves the banner's full span). Multi-day reservations render as a **separate inline spanning bar** in `week.blade.php` (`grid-column: {2+col_start} / span {col_span}`) — see the "Week multi-day spanning + day-view full-day" bullet above. The standalone `reservation-lane.blade.php` and `time-rail.blade.php` partials (pre-scheduler leftovers, zero references) were removed; the time rail renders inline in the week/day blades.

**(f) `#[Computed]` invalidation rule** — every range-dependent computed (`rangeEvents`, `calendarDays`, `rangeLabel`, `currentMonthName`, `miniMonthDays`) reads `$this->navigationDate` and `$this->view` (or `$this->miniMonthDate` for the mini grid) as its **first statements** so Livewire's dependency tracker re-runs it when nav changes. The navigation actions (`prevPeriod`/`nextPeriod`/`toggleView`/`goToToday`/`selectDate`/`focusRecord`/`goToDate`) **defensively `unset()` the computeds** they invalidate — belt-and-suspenders against stale memoization across a single request. `goToDate` mirrors `goToToday` (sets `selectedDate`+`navigationDate`+`miniMonthDate`, unsets the nav computeds + `miniMonthDays`); `stepMiniMonth` is picker-only — it shifts `miniMonthDate` by ±N months (12 = a year jump, via Jalalian `addMonths`) and unsets **only** `miniMonthDays`, leaving the main grid untouched so browsing the popover never disturbs the open view. `view` is `#[Locked]` (client-tamper-proof); `toggleView` is the only mutator and whitelists `month|week|day`, persisting to `session('calendar_view_mode')` which `mount` reads back with the same whitelist.

**(g) `Event::date_jalali` accessor is misnamed** — its getter returns `Carbon::parse($attributes['date'])->format('Y-m-d')` (GREGORIAN `Y-m-d`) and its setter expects a Gregorian `Y-m-d`. `MoveEventAction` converts the incoming Jalali `dateJalali` (frontend) → Gregorian via `Jalalian::fromFormat('Y-m-d', $dateJalali)->toCarbon()->format('Y-m-d')` before writing `$event->date_jalali`; `revertTo` converts back via `Jalalian::fromCarbon($event->date)->format('Y-m-d')` for the frontend. Do NOT "fix" the accessor name — it's the existing contract and `SaveEventAction` depends on it.

**(h) `CalendarPresenter` extraction** (`app/Livewire/Dashboard/Tab/Presentation/CalendarPresenter.php`) — all read-side `#[Computed]` bodies (`activeDate`, `calendarDays`/`miniMonthDays` via `monthDays()`, `currentMonthName`, `rangeLabel`, `rangeEvents`, `selectedDayEvents`, plus the private `buildMonthDays`/`buildAllDayEntries`/`getProfilesWithDates`/`getAllHolidays` helpers and their caches) live in this plain POJO, NOT on the Livewire component. `Calendar` keeps a memoized `private ?CalendarPresenter $presenter` resolved via `presenter() { return $this->presenter ??= new CalendarPresenter(); }`, and each computed is a one-line delegate (`return $this->presenter()->rangeEvents($this->navigationDate, $this->view);`). The component retains ONLY Livewire state (`navigationDate`/`selectedDate`/`view`/`hideFriday`/modal flags) + mutation actions (`moveEvent`/`resizeEvent`/`saveEvent`/`deleteEvent`/`shareEvent`/`editEvent`/nav) — clean read/write separation. The presenter's instance caches (`monthDaysCache`, `profilesWithDatesCache`, `holidaysAllCache`) live for one request (one presenter per request via `??=`), matching the old per-request memoization. The presenter takes no constructor args (it reads `Auth::id()` lazily inside each method), so it stays construction-free.

**(i) `CalendarPresenter` owns all blade presentation — write-once, consume-everywhere** — the day/week/mobile/month/mini-month blades no longer triplicate the PHP setup (hour offsets, all-day grouping, per-day jalali/today/reservation derivation, icon map, pill geometry). Three presenter methods feed them: `weekLabels()` (the 7 Sat-first Persian labels `['ش','ی','د','س','چ','پ','ج']`, consumed by month + mini-month headers via `$this->weekLabels`), `gridData($nav, $view, $rangeEvents)` (day/week grids), and `agendaItems($nav, $view, $rangeEvents)` (mobile list). `Calendar` exposes `weekLabels` as a `#[Computed]` delegate and `gridData(string $scope)`/`agendaItems(string $scope)` as **regular public methods** (take an explicit `$scope`, read cached `$this->rangeEvents` + `$this->navigationDate` — NOT `#[Computed]`, so blades pass the scope and they recompute per call without memoization). `gridData` returns `{daysMeta[], hourOffsets[h=>px], hourLabels[h=>persian], gridHeight, hourHeight, startHour, endHour, iconByType, allReservations[], spanningReservations[]}`; each `daysMeta` entry carries `{jKey, weekLabel, dayNum, isFriday, isToday, dayAllDay[], dayPills[], startIso}`. **Per-pill geometry is precomputed** by private `withGeometry($pill, $gridHeight)` (mirrors the old blade math exactly: `top=(start_minutes-360)*60/60`, `height=min(max(duration*60/60,24), gridHeight-top)`, `left_pct=col/span*100`, `width_pct=1/span*100`; returns the pill unchanged when `col`/`span`/`start_minutes`/`duration_minutes` are missing or `span<1`, and reservation-linked pills skip enrichment entirely), so `event-block.blade.php` reads `top`/`height`/`left_pct`/`width_pct` straight off `$event` (cast `(int)`/`(float)` there) and the grid blades' pill guard reduces to `if (!empty($pill['is_reservation_linked']) || !isset($pill['top'],$pill['height'],$pill['left_pct'],$pill['width_pct'])) continue;`. `allReservations` iterates `byDay` in insertion order to preserve the reservation-banner ordering. The day-view empty-day fallback (`emptyDayMeta`) guarantees `daysMeta[0]` always exists so `day.blade.php` can dereference `$grid['daysMeta'][0]` unguarded. The Friday `hideFriday` toggle stays inline in the week blade as `x-show="@js(!$meta['isFriday']) || !$wire.hideFriday"` (the expression is week-only; day view never toggles) — `isFriday` comes from `Jalalian::getDayOfWeek() === 6` (0=Sat..6=Fri), equivalent to the old loop-index `$i === 6` because weeks are Sat-first. Two shared partials kill the markup duplication: `calendar/all-day-chip.blade.php` (holiday/birthday/anniversary chip, `keyPrefix`-parameterized `wire:key`) and `calendar/reservation-banner.blade.php` (`columnsStyle`/`gridSpan`/`keyPrefix`-parameterized; day passes `gridSpan=null`, week passes `grid-column: 2 / span 7`). `wire:key` prefixes are preserved exactly per blade (day: `day-allday`/`day-res`; week: `allday`/`week-res`) for morph-stable diffing. `agendaItems` pre-converts each day's `label` (`convertToPersian(format('l، d F Y'))`) so the mobile blade must NOT double-convert the label (it still converts `time`). No `_`-prefixed partials — both new partials drop the underscore.

Admin guide: `EventResource` already uses `FilamentAdminGuide` with a 5-tab guide (`overview/countdown/list-tabs/admin-ops/user`); the scheduler is user-panel-only, so **no admin-guide work** is needed — only this `livewirePattern.md` append + `calendar/legend.blade.php` schedule section + `scriptPattern.md` Alpine append.

**(j0) Header date-picker popover — click the range label to jump** — the static `<h2>` range label in `view-header.blade.php` is now a button opening a mini-month popover (the viewPattern §8.2 solid-button-dropdown shape: `x-data="{ pickerOpen: false }"` + `@click.outside`/`@keydown.escape.window` close, `x-show`+`x-cloak`+`x-transition` panel, `@include('livewire.dashboard.tab.calendar.mini-month')`). The popover reuses the existing `miniMonthDate` cursor + `miniMonthDays` grid but adds a two-row stepper header in `mini-month.blade.php`: a **year** row (`stepMiniMonth(-12)`/`stepMiniMonth(12)`, `keyboard_double_arrow_right`/`left`) and a **month** row (`stepMiniMonth(-1)`/`stepMiniMonth(1)`), labels from the enhanced `miniMonthData()` (`yearLabel`/`monthLabel`/`prevYear`/`nextYear` via Jalalian `subYears`/`addYears` — immutable, original untouched). Day-click is `wire:click="goToDate('{{ $day['date'] }}')"` (Livewire parses method+args — injection-safe, NOT a raw `@click` JS string that would `eval` a server-interpolated value) plus a separate Alpine `@click="pickerOpen = false"` to close: the wire call navigates, the Alpine call closes, both fire on the same click. Browsing the picker (`stepMiniMonth`) moves only `miniMonthDate`; picking a day (`goToDate`) syncs all three cursors and re-renders the main grid. The always-visible prev/today/next inline controls stay; the popover is the discoverability shortcut for month/day/year jumps — one standard Google/Outlook-pattern popover instead of 11 next-arrow clicks. The popover is `@include`d unconditionally (hidden via `x-show`), which is fine because `miniMonthData`/`miniMonthDays` are pure Jalalian date-math with **zero DB queries** — there is no baseline query cost to lazy-load away.

**(j) Grid pill shows the end time, is clickable to edit, and carries a full tooltip** (`event-block.blade.php`). The pill originally showed start time only, even though `duration_minutes` was already available for geometry — `$showEndTime = $event['duration_minutes'] > 30` gates a computed end time into `$rangeLabel = "start–end"` (vs. bare `start` at ≤30 min, avoiding overflow on short/default-duration blocks). The end time is `$cappedDuration = min($event['duration_minutes'], 1440 - $event['start_minutes'])` then `$endMinutes = $event['start_minutes'] + $cappedDuration` — a plain `% 1440` wrap was tried first and rejected: `CalendarLayout::pack()` and `CalendarPresenter::withGeometry()` already clamp the pill's pack-end/height to the day boundary (`SaveEventAction`/`EventForm` have no cross-field cap between `time` and `durationMinutes`, unlike `ResizeEventAction` which enforces `duration <= DAY_END_MINUTES - start`), so a `%1440`-wrapped label for an over-midnight event (e.g. 20:00 start, 480min/8h duration) would read `"20:00–04:00"` while the rendered box visibly stops at midnight — text and shape disagreeing. The `min(...)`-capped version always agrees with what's rendered (worst case shows `"20:00–24:00"`). The outer `<div>` also gets `title="{title} — {rangeLabel}"` (full text for truncated titles) and `@click="!justDragged && $wire.editEvent({{ id }})"` reusing the existing `editEvent()` guard (already `Event::where('user_id', Auth::id())->find()`-scoped, so it's safe to call unconditionally — locked/reservation events get the existing "manage via Reservation tab" toast, non-owned events silently no-op) — previously only the sidebar/mobile list could open the edit modal; the Day/Week grid pill had no click handler at all. The resize handle gets `@click.stop` so a plain click on the 6px handle doesn't also bubble into `editEvent`.

**`justDragged` click-after-drag guard** (`calendarDrag()` in `calendar.js`) — a genuine pointer-drag (mousedown→mousemove past `DEAD_ZONE_PX`→mouseup on the same element) still fires a native `click` afterward in every browser (custom pointer-based drags, unlike the HTML5 DnD API, don't suppress it). `handleUp` sets `activeInstance.justDragged = true` when `dragStarted` was true, reset via `setTimeout(..., 0)` (deferred a macrotask so it clears only after the synchronous click has already been checked). The blade's `@click` checks `!justDragged` — do NOT try to solve this via `stopPropagation()` in a capture-phase listener added inside `handleUp`; DOM ordering on the SAME target element is registration order, not capture-before-bubble, so a listener added after Livewire's own `wire:click`-equivalent binding is not guaranteed to run first.

**Day/Week grid scrollbar — hidden until hover** (`resources/css/core/dashboard.css` `.scrollbar-hover-reveal`, applied to the two `overflow-y-auto` grid wrappers in `calendar.blade.php`). Deliberate exception to `userStylesPattern.md`'s "always-visible thin tinted scrollbar" convention, requested for this module specifically. Webkit: track/thumb stay reserved at 6px but the thumb is `background:transparent` until `:hover` (no layout shift). Firefox: `scrollbar-width:none` → `thin` on `:hover` (Firefox has no thumb-only-hidden primitive, so this does shift width slightly on hover — accepted tradeoff, same one virtually every hide-until-hover scrollbar implementation makes).

**Hover-reveal edit affordance** — the pill's outer `<div>` gained `group` in its `@class` list; a small `edit` (pencil) icon sits in the header row (`opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-70 transition-opacity`), gated `@if($draggable)`. `$draggable` (`is_owner && !locked && !isReservation`) is algebraically identical to `!locked` here (`locked = isReservation || !is_owner`, so `!locked` already implies `is_owner && !isReservation`) — both expressions gate the exact same set of pills, i.e. the ones where clicking will actually open the edit modal (not silently no-op on a non-owned pill, not just toast on a reservation-linked one). The icon is a pure teaching affordance (no click handler of its own — the whole pill is already clickable); it does not change the resize handle's own existing `cursor-ns-resize` + hover-bg + static `title` affordance, which already communicates "drag to resize" distinctly.

**Sidebar day-detail list also shows the end time** (`calendar/events.blade.php`, fed by `CalendarPresenter::selectedDayEvents()` — a separate query/shape from `rangeEvents()`, used only by the Month view's left-pane list). `selectedDayEvents()`'s per-event map gained `start_minutes`/`duration_minutes` (same `$event->duration_minutes ?: Event::DEFAULT_DURATION_MINUTES` fallback as `rangeEvents()`); the blade computes the same `> 30` gate + day-boundary-capped range label inline (mirroring event-block.blade.php's `@php` block, not extracted to the presenter, to match the precedent already set there). Holiday/birthday/anniversary entries in the same collection have no `duration_minutes` key at all (they're fixed `'time' => '00:00'` all-day markers) — the blade's `@php` guards on `($event['type'] ?? '') === 'event'` before reading `duration_minutes`, so it never touches those shapes.

## Links module — dead fields wired up, reusable filter component, collapsible recent strip

The `Links` module (`app/Livewire/Dashboard/Tab/Links.php`, `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/tab/links*`) was brought up to feature-parity with sibling `Tab/*` modules (Faqs/Reports/Status) — all changes are additive UI/accessor work on data that already existed, zero migrations.

**(a) Two admin-required fields were fully dead in the user panel — now wired up.** `url_description` (Textarea, searched by the global search index) never rendered anywhere; it's now `title="{{ $link->url_description }}"` on the card `<a>`, gated `@if($link->url_description)` — a native browser hover tooltip, which is *exactly* what the Filament admin guide (`resources/views/filament/resources/link/guide/overview.blade.php`) already claimed as the field's behavior, so no guide copy needed changing there. `image_description` (alt text for the banner image) was collected but discarded — the card `<img alt="">` now reads `$link->image_description ?: $link->url_title`.

**(b) `icon_description` was being misused as a Material Symbols glyph name — first fixed to plain alt-text, then formalized into a real 4-tier fallback with a curated icon picker.** The lang string was unambiguous: `'icon_description' => 'متن جایگزین (alt) آیکون برای دسترسی‌پذیری'` ("alt text for the icon, for accessibility") — yet the old blade rendered `{{ $link->icon_description ?: 'link' }}` **inside** `<span class="material-symbols-rounded">`, i.e. free admin-typed text interpreted as an icon ligature name. Any real description sentence would have rendered as broken/garbled text where an icon glyph was expected (same ligature-must-be-a-real-name trap as the admin-vs-user icon-convention rules elsewhere in this codebase, and the exact same failure mode later found — independently, via a different data path — in the Department `ticket_options` Heroicon/Material-Symbols mismatch). Rather than stop at "just alt text," `icon_description` was repurposed as a proper **icon picker** (`App\Enums\LinkIcon`, a closed Material Symbols ligature enum with Persian `getLabel()`s, e.g. `hub`→"هاب / مرکز") consumed by `LinkFormPresenter::iconDescription()` as a `Select` with **live glyph previews** in each option (`->allowHtml()`, each option rendered as `<span class="material-symbols-rounded">{value}</span> {label}` — no SVG file I/O needed, unlike the Heroicon-based `FilamentIconOptions` trait, since Material Symbols is a webfont already loaded admin-side via `resources/css/core/filament.css:6`'s `@import "material-symbols/rounded.css"`). The column keeps its original name (no migration) but its meaning changed from "free alt text" to "a picked icon key"; `getOptionLabelUsing()` + a synthetic options-array entry (mirroring `SkillFormPresenter::icon()`'s `->when(filled($record?->icon) && !SkillIcon::tryFrom(...), ...)` defensive pattern) keep any pre-existing legacy value visible/editable in the admin form even if it doesn't match a current enum case.

Runtime fallback is now 4 tiers per card:
```blade
@if($link->image)          <img src="{{ $link->image_url }}" alt="{{ $link->image_description ?: $link->url_title }}">
@elseif($link->icon)       <img src="{{ $link->icon_url }}" alt="{{ $link->icon_description ?: $link->url_title }}">
@elseif($pickedIcon)       <span class="material-symbols-rounded ..." title="{{ $pickedIcon->getLabel() }}">{{ $pickedIcon->value }}</span>
@else                       <span class="material-symbols-rounded ...">link</span> {{-- or open_in_new for external --}}
@endif
```
where `$pickedIcon = \App\Enums\LinkIcon::tryFrom((string) $link->icon_description)`. This `tryFrom()` guard is the safety net the picker needs: a legacy row whose `icon_description` is old free-typed text (or simply doesn't match any current enum case, e.g. after a case is renamed/removed) silently resolves to `null` and falls straight through to the hardcoded tier-4 glyph — never re-introducing the garbled-text bug for old data. No alt text is ever hand-typed for this tier — the enum's own `getLabel()` is used automatically as the `title` tooltip, which is the whole point of a picker over free text: the label is derived from what was *selected*, not separately retyped. `Link::iconUrl()` is a 1:1 mirror of the existing `imageUrl()` accessor (`Attribute::make(get: ..., )->shouldCache()` off `resolvePublicAssetUrl($attributes['icon'])`). The admin guide's field-hint copy for `image`/`icon`/`icon_description` (and a previously entirely-undocumented `image_description`) was corrected/added to match — it previously *documented the bug itself* as intended behavior, which is why it went unnoticed; a stale/wrong admin guide is worse than no guide.

**Why not reuse `FilamentIconOptions`/`ResourceTypeIcon`** (the existing Heroicon-based curated picker used by `DepartmentResource`'s `ticket_options.icon` and `ResourceResource`'s `display_icon`)? Because this codebase enforces **admin panel → Heroicons, user panel → Material Symbols** as a hard convention (see the admin-vs-user icon-convention rule elsewhere), and `Link`'s fallback tier renders in the **user panel** as `<span class="material-symbols-rounded">`. Reusing the Heroicon enum here would require translating a Heroicon identifier to a Material Symbols ligature at read time — exactly the translation step `ResourceResource` does correctly (`TypeProvisioner::class` derives `display_material_icon` via `ResourceTypeIcon::from(...)->getMaterialIcon()`) and `Department`'s `ticket_options` repeater does **not** do, which is precisely why that repeater's Material Symbols read path (`HasTicketOptions::getCustomMaterialIconForArea()`) prints a raw Heroicon string as literal garbled text in `ths/create.blade.php`/`ths/ticket-table.blade.php` — a live, still-open bug, unrelated to Link, found while auditing this exact "hand-typed/mismatched icon" failure class. `LinkIcon` sidesteps the whole problem by being Material-Symbols-native end to end: the value picked in the admin Select is *the same string* rendered in the user panel, no translation step to forget.

**(c) Copy-to-clipboard button** — the existing generic `<x-ui.buttons.copy :text="..." message="..."/>` component (already used elsewhere, fully self-contained: `navigator.clipboard.writeText` + 2s check-mark swap) dropped into a second floating icon slot (top-right, mirroring the existing top-left `arrow_outward` hint) on each card. The card was restructured so the **copy button is a sibling of the card's `<a>`, not nested inside it** — `<button>`-inside-`<a>` is invalid per the HTML5 content model (interactive content nested in interactive content) and was the original shape; the fix wraps both in an outer `<div class="relative ... group/card">` (the Tailwind named group moved up one level so both the `<a>`'s internal hover states and the sibling copy-button's `group-hover/card:opacity-100` still react to the same hover — CSS `:hover` on a descendant naturally puts every ancestor into its hovered-branch state, so this needed no JS), with the copy button's wrapper `<div @click.stop.prevent>` still required to stop its click from bubbling into anything (bare `@click.stop.prevent` modifier-only Alpine syntax, no expression needed, same precedent as `status/grid.blade.php`). `wire:key` moved from the old `<a>` to the new outer `<div>` — it must sit on the outermost per-iteration element for Livewire's morph to diff correctly.

**(d) Search + type filter via the shared `<x-ui.forms.filters>` component** — `Links.php` gained `public string $search = ''` and `public string $activeFilter = 'all'` (`all|internal|external`), following the exact `Faqs.php`/`Reports.php` shape (`updatedSearch()` clears `$open` focus-mode, `resetFilters()` resets both, `setFilter()` whitelists the three values). Unlike Faqs/DMS (which filter via the query builder), Link's two source lists are `#[Computed(seconds: 7200, cache: true)]` — small, already fully loaded — so filtering happens **in-memory on the cached `Collection`** (`filterBySearch()`, a plain `Collection::filter` over `url_title`/`url_description`), not pushed into SQL; this keeps the 2-hour cache fully intact (search/filter never busts or bypasses it). `activeFilter` doesn't need its own query scope either — `externalLinks()`/`internalLinks()` just short-circuit to `collect()` when the other type is selected. `<x-ui.forms.filters>`'s slot holds three type-chip buttons (all/internal/external, styled like `authority/filters.blade.php`'s department chips) rather than being left empty — every other usage of this component in the codebase always has real chip content in the slot, so an empty-panel usage would have been a first, oddly-shaped exception. `activeCondition`/`hasActiveFilters` (the red dot on the docked filter FAB) is scoped to `activeFilter` only, NOT `search` — matches every existing usage (Faqs/Reports/Feeds/Ads/Authority/Status all exclude search from that badge; search has its own inline clear-X).

**Empty-state disambiguation** — with search/filter now able to legitimately zero out a section, `@else` in both `internal.blade.php`/`external.blade.php` branches on `$search !== '' || $activeFilter !== 'all'`: shows "نتیجه‌ای یافت نشد" (no results, adjust search/filter) in that case, vs. the original "هیچ سامانه‌ای تعریف نشده" (admin hasn't defined any) only when genuinely no filter/search is active. Without this split, a real zero-match search would have shown the misleading "not configured by admin" message.

**(e) Collapsible "recent" strip** — `links/smart.blade.php` was ported to the exact same collapsible shape as `dms/recent-docs.blade.php`: `x-data="{ open: false }"` on the `<section>`, a `<button @click="open = !open">` header wrapping the icon+title+rotating `expand_more` chevron, `<button @click.stop="clearRecent()">` for the destructive action (the `.stop` keeps it from also toggling the collapse), and the item list wrapped in `x-show="open"` with the same enter/leave `x-transition` pair. Also added `:title="item.title"` on each recent item's truncated label (`line-clamp-1`), matching DMS's `:title="doc.title"` — previously a long link title had no way to be read except opening it. **This collapsible-strip shape is now the house convention for any "recent X" list** — see the cross-module note below.

### House convention: collapsible "recent items" strip

Any module-level "recently viewed/opened" horizontal strip (localStorage-backed or otherwise) should use this exact shape, not an always-expanded list:
- Outer `<section x-data="{ open: false }" x-show="items.length" x-cloak>`
- A `<button @click="open = !open">` wrapping icon + title + `<span class="material-symbols-rounded ... transition-transform" :class="open ? 'rotate-180' : ''">expand_more</span>`
- Any destructive/secondary action (e.g. "clear") as a sibling `<button @click.stop="...">` — `.stop` is required so it doesn't also toggle the collapse
- The scrollable item list wrapped in `<div x-show="open" x-transition:enter="transition ease-out duration-200" x-transition:enter-start="opacity-0 -translate-y-2" x-transition:enter-end="opacity-100 translate-y-0" x-transition:leave="transition ease-in duration-150" x-transition:leave-start="opacity-100 translate-y-0" x-transition:leave-end="opacity-0 -translate-y-2">`
- Each item's truncated label gets a `:title="item.title"` (or server-side `title="{{ }}"`) tooltip

Reference implementations: `dms/recent-docs.blade.php` (original), `links/smart.blade.php` (ported). Any other always-expanded "recent" strip found elsewhere should be ported to this same shape for consistency.

### Cardinal rule — Material Symbols is the user-panel icon system; never render a raw stored value that wasn't validated for it

Every icon glyph rendered in the user panel is `<span class="material-symbols-rounded">{ligature-name}</span>` — a literal font ligature, not an arbitrary string. If that ligature name comes from anywhere other than a hardcoded literal or a closed PHP `enum`/`match()`, it is one typo or one cross-system mixup away from printing broken/garbled text where an icon should be. **Before writing `{{ $value }}` inside a `material-symbols-rounded` span, trace `$value` back to its source and confirm it's either (a) a literal in the blade/PHP, (b) an enum case's own method (`SomeEnum::from($x)->getMaterialIcon()`), or (c) a value that was validated with `Enum::tryFrom()` and has a safe fallback for the null case** — never an unguarded raw DB column or unguarded admin-typed free text.

### Client-side array editing on a Form object — `$wire.set('form.x', value, false)` for add/remove/toggle without a round trip per click

TaskBoard's task-edit modal manages three small Form-object arrays (`labels`, `checklist`, `existingAttachments`) that used to require a `wire:click` server round trip for every add/remove/toggle. These were moved to pure client-side Alpine state (`taskboard.js`: `labels`/`checklist`/`existingAttachments` + a `syncFormArrays()` seeder called once from the modal's existing open-effect) that mutates a local array/object in place (`.push`/`.splice`/`item.done = …`) for instant reactive UI (chip lists, checklist progress ring, tab badges), then pushes the new value with `this.$wire.set('form.x', value, false)`. The `false` third argument is Livewire's documented non-live set: it calls `dataSet(component.reactive, …)` unconditionally but skips `queueUpdate`/the network round trip that `live=true` (the default) would trigger. Because `component.reactive` is `Alpine.reactive(component.ephemeral)` — a Proxy wrapping the *same* underlying object, not a copy — the mutation is already sitting in `ephemeral` by the time any subsequent real request fires; `getUpdates()` diffs `canonical` vs `ephemeral` on every request regardless of how `ephemeral` got mutated, so the value is picked up automatically on whatever real Livewire request comes next (the final `createTask`/`updateTask`/`duplicateTask` submit, or an earlier unrelated `.live` field triggering its own round trip). Server-side `#[Validate]` rules on the Form (`labels` max 10 items / max 30 chars each, `checklist.*.text` max 255) still run as the real enforcement — the client mirrors the same caps (e.g. a `MAX_LABELS = 10` guard + a `Set`-backed strict-equality dedupe matching `in_array($text, …, true)`) purely for instant UI feedback, never as the source of truth. **Rule: reach for `$wire.set(path, value, false)` only for a Form-object array/collection that (a) has its own client-side Alpine-reactive mirror seeded once per modal-open via a `syncFormArrays()`-style method, and (b) is still covered by server-side `#[Validate]`/Action-level validation on the eventual real submit** — never use the deferred set to skip validation, and never introduce it for a value with no genuine per-keystroke/per-click UI feedback to gain (a plain `wire:model` deferred binding already covers that case with less code). Reference: `Dashboard\TaskBoard\Main`/`Forms\TaskForm`, `resources/js/components/alpine/data/taskboard.js`, `taskboard/form.blade.php` + `taskboard/detail-fields.blade.php`.

Full rule + the live bug this was written after (Department's `ticket_options.icon` reusing a Heroicon value as a Material Symbols name in the Ths/Ticket module) is documented in `app/Filament/filamentPattern.md` Rule 61 — that's the canonical version; this is a pointer, not a duplicate. The two reference *Material-Symbols-native* enums for user-panel-only icon pickers are `App\Enums\LinkIcon` and `App\Enums\SkillIcon` — reach for one of these (or a similar small closed enum) whenever a new feature needs an admin-picked icon that only ever renders in the user panel; reach for `App\Enums\ResourceTypeIcon` + its `getMaterialIcon()` translation only when the *same* picked value also needs to render as a Heroicon somewhere in the admin panel.

### Cardinal rule — every attachment write AND deletion in the user panel goes through `StoresAttachedFiles` + `CleansAttachedFiles`, never raw `Storage` calls

Any Livewire class and any of its Action/Validator collaborators that persists or removes an uploaded/stored file **must compose both `App\Traits\StoresAttachedFiles` and `App\Traits\CleansAttachedFiles`** (the pairing is a deliberate composition requirement — see `traitPattern.md` §2.3) and route every operation through them:

- **Write** → `static::storeAttachment($file, $directory, $fileName)` (or the batch `storeAttachments()`), taking `['path']` for a path-only column or the full `{path, name, mime, size}` meta for the canonical shape. Never `$file->store()`/`->storeAs()` inline — the raw calls return `false` on failure instead of throwing, so a failed store silently poisons the column, and there is no rollback.
- **Delete** → `static::deleteStoredFiles($value)` / `deleteStoredDirectory()`, never `Storage::disk('public')->delete()`. The trait versions carry the path-traversal guard, log failures instead of dying silently, and accept a string, array, `{path}`-object array, or Collection interchangeably (path-only and meta shapes both work).

Do not re-litigate the `public` disk either — both traits hardcode disk `public` internally, so the explicit `->store(..., 'public')` argument disappears along with the raw call.

Swept 2026-08-30, zero bypasses left. The five fixed sites, for archaeology: `Ths\SubmitTicketAction` (path-only objects → canonical `{file, name, mime, size}` kept inside `requester_files` JSON — readers key on `file`, extra keys ignored, legacy rows read unchanged), `Suggestion\CreateSuggestionAction`, `Profile\UploadStandardDocumentAction` + `UploadCustomDocumentAction` (write+replaced-file cleanup; `{key, path, category}` shape and `doc_*_{timestamp}.ext` filenames preserved byte-identically), `Profile\SaveProfileAction` (image write + rollback/old-image deletes), and `Profile\DeleteProfileImageAction` (deletion-only, the audit's 5th find). A model's own `deleting` hook using the same trait methods (`Ticket::booted()`, `Profile::booted()`) is complementary, not duplication — it deletes what's currently stored in the columns at row-deletion time, while Actions delete the replaced/no-longer-referenced files; the two path sets are disjoint, and a duplicate delete is a harmless no-op anyway.

### Cardinal rule — any file-attachment feature gets a real image preview, both before and after save, never a plain link/icon chip

Any user-panel module that lets a user attach files — comment, reply, task, ticket, leave/support request, whatever — must render those attachments the same way everywhere, at both stages:

- **Post-save (already persisted)**: an image attachment renders as a real thumbnail (`<img>`, object-cover, fixed square tile) wrapped in a Fancybox-lightbox anchor (`data-fancybox="<group>-{id}"`, `data-caption="{name}"`); a non-image attachment renders as an icon+name(+size) chip that opens/downloads directly. Never a bare "attach_file icon + filename" link for something that could be an image — that's the exact regression this rule exists to prevent (it's what Project's activity feed, the shared `ths/reply-bubble.blade.php`, and `release-request.blade.php`'s history list all did before being fixed).
- **Pre-save (staged, not yet submitted)**: while a file is a `TemporaryUploadedFile` sitting in a `WithFileUploads`-backed array, an image shows a small live thumbnail (`<img src="{{ $file->temporaryUrl() }}">`) instead of a generic upload icon, right in the staged-chip row next to its remove button. A composer that accepts file uploads but shows **nothing** about what's staged (TaskBoard's reply composer and Ths's ticket-reply composer both did this before being fixed) is the same class of bug as the post-save one — fix it by adding the staged-chip list, not just the thumbnail.

**How to detect "is this an image" depends on the data shape already available** — don't invent a new shape, match what's already there:
- Canonical `{path, name, mime, size}` array (the shape produced by `App\Traits\StoresAttachedFiles`/`saveUploadedFileUsing`, used by `Reply`, `TaskDetail`, `ReleaseRequest`, etc.): use `App\Traits\BuildsChatBubbles::attachments()` if the consuming class already has that trait (it maps `mime` → `is_image`, and computes `url`/`size_label` in the same pass — see `ProjectPresenter::attachments()` usage in `Project\Main::activityFeed()`), otherwise inline `str_starts_with($attachment['mime'] ?? '', 'image/')` (used directly in `release-request.blade.php` and `ths/reply-bubble.blade.php`, which have no presenter to route through).
- That same canonical shape already sitting in **client-side Alpine state** (e.g. TaskBoard's `existingAttachments` array, seeded once per modal-open — see the `$wire.set(path, value, false)` rule above): branch in the template with `<template x-if="(attachment.mime || '').startsWith('image/')">` / the negated twin, not a PHP `@if` — the `mime` field is already there in the JS object, no controller round trip needed to check it.
- A single plain path string column with no stored `mime` (e.g. `Suggestion::$attachment`): sniff the file extension instead. Don't inline the extension list ad hoc — add a small presenter method that returns the bool (see `SuggestionPresenter::attachmentIsImage()`, which mirrors the same `['png','jpg','jpeg','webp']` list `suggestion/create.blade.php`'s pre-save block already uses) so the check has one home instead of drifting between the create-form and the detail-view copies of it. There is no `size_label` to show for this shape, don't fabricate one.
- A raw legacy array with its own ad hoc `file`/`file_url` keys and no `mime` at all (e.g. Ths's `$selectedTicket['requester_files']`): extension-sniff the `file` key directly in the blade (`Str::contains($file['file'], ['jpg','jpeg','png','gif','webp'])`) — don't force-fit it into the canonical shape just for this one check.
- A live `TemporaryUploadedFile` object (pre-save): `str_starts_with($file->getMimeType() ?? '', 'image/')` + `$file->temporaryUrl()`.

**Fancybox is wired up exactly once, globally** — `resources/js/components/alpine/data/menu.js` (`menu()`, the `x-data` wrapping the always-present top navbar in `layouts/app.blade.php` via `<x-dashboard.header/>`) spreads `fancyboxMixin()` and calls `this.initFancybox()` in its own `init()`. Since `Fancybox.bind("[data-fancybox]", …)` is a `document`-level delegated bind guarded by a module-level `initialized` flag, this single call covers every `data-fancybox` element on every dashboard page for the lifetime of that page load. **Do not** re-import `fancyboxMixin`/re-call `initFancybox()` in a page-specific Alpine component — `project.js`, `taskboard.js`, and `ths.js` never had it re-added, and the pre-existing (now redundant) copies in `channel.js`/`contact.js`/`gallery.js` were removed once this rule was written, so **zero** page-specific Alpine factory in this codebase should import `../mixins/fancybox.js` going forward; just add the `data-fancybox` attribute to the markup and it works. Give every `data-fancybox` group a value scoped to its parent record (`data-fancybox="<entity>-{id}-<list-name>"`, e.g. `"ticket-{$selectedTicket['id']}-requester-files"`) so the lightbox's slideshow/paging only cycles through that one record's own images, never every image on the page. The only reason to touch `menu.js` again here is if the navbar itself is ever made conditional (`View::hasSection('minimal_layout')`) for a page that also needs attachment previews — none currently are.

Reference implementations to copy from: `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/messaging/message-attachments.blade.php` (the original canonical-shape post-save pattern, shared by Channel/Contact/Project-team-chat), `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/project/activity.blade.php` + `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/ths/reply-bubble.blade.php` + `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/release-request.blade.php`'s history list + `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/ths/modal.blade.php`'s `requester_files` gallery (post-save, canonical-shape-adjacent variants), `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/taskboard/detail-fields.blade.php`'s `existingAttachments` (post-save, Alpine-state variant) and `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/suggestion/detail.blade.php` + `SuggestionPresenter::attachmentIsImage()` (post-save, single-path-string variant), `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/taskboard/form.blade.php`'s reply-tab staged list + `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/ths/workspace.blade.php`'s reply composer + `release-request.blade.php`'s submit-form attachments (pre-save; the first two are staged-chip-from-scratch, paired with a new `removeReplyAttachment(int $index)` method on the owning component).

### Sharing a whole modal/feature across independently-rendered Livewire components — PHP trait + a dedicated shared blade folder, never a nested child component

Project's "برد وظایف" (Kanban) tab needed the exact same rich task-edit modal TaskBoard already has (create/edit/view, checklist, labels, attachments, reply thread) — but `TaskBoard\Main` and `Project\Main` are two independent top-level Livewire components, not parent/child. The established way to share this much behavior between independent components in this codebase is a **PHP trait with an explicit method-name contract** (the same shape as `App\Traits\ChatComposer`, shared by Channel/Contact) — never a nested/embedded child Livewire component (that would add re-render/wire:model-forwarding complexity this app doesn't otherwise use).

- **`App\Traits\ManagesTaskModal`** carries every modal-only property/method (`$form`, `$taskReplyForm`, `$editingTaskId`, `$isModalOpen`/`$isEditMode`/`$isReadOnly`, `$staffMembers`/`$departmentOptions`, `editTask`/`viewTask`/`createTask`/`updateTask`/`duplicateTask`/`postTaskReply`/`removeAttachment`/`removeReplyAttachment`, the `availableSections`/`availableUnits`/`selectedDepartment`/`projectOptions`/`canReplyToTask`/`editingTask` `#[Computed]`s, and the protected `populateFormFromTask()`). Board-management concerns (pagination, column scoping, bulk actions, archive/assign/delete/undo) stay OUT of the trait and OUT of Project entirely — Project's Kanban is intentionally the minimal-vs-full-featured pairing already established by Team Chat vs. the real Channel (see below).
- **Two hook seams, not hard-coded calls**, let each host customize behavior without touching the trait: `abstract protected function refreshAfterTaskSave(): void` (TaskBoard's implementation calls its own `loadTasks()`; Project's calls `loadKanbanBoard()`) and `protected function afterOpenCreateModal(): void` (empty default in the trait; Project overrides it to default `$this->form->projectId = $this->activeProjectId` — a class's own method declaration always wins over a same-named trait method in PHP, no conflict-resolution syntax needed for this one-directional override).
- Livewire genuinely calls both hooks: `SupportLifecycleHooks::mount()` runs the host's own `mount()` first, then `callTraitHook('mount', …)`, which calls `mount{TraitBasename}()` — i.e. `mountManagesTaskModal()` — for every trait on the class. That's what seeds `$staffMembers`/`$departmentOptions` on both hosts without duplicating the population logic.
- **The Blade views are reused directly, not copied** — `@include('livewire.dashboard.taskboard.form', ['presenter' => new TaskBoardPresenter()])` is included from `project.blade.php` exactly as-is (explicitly re-passing `$presenter` as a `TaskBoardPresenter`, since Project's own `render()` already binds `$presenter` to a `ProjectPresenter` — Blade's `@include` data array only shadows that key for the included template, the outer scope is untouched).
- **The card is shared via the same blade, gated by `$isPersonalBoard`** — `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/tasking/` holds only `board-column.blade.php`, which `@include`s the existing `taskboard/card.blade.php` with `['isPersonalBoard' => false]` (parallel to `messaging/` for chat partials). There is no separate `tasking/board-card.blade.php`; the one `taskboard/card.blade.php` renders both boards, and every TaskBoard-only row action (archive/unarchive/assign/delete/undo-assignment/bulk-select/label-filter) is wrapped in `@if($isPersonalBoard && …)` so it stays hidden on Project's minimal board, where the only action is `wire:click="openProjectTask(...)"` (editable if `$task['can_change_status']`, read-only otherwise — never blocked outright like TaskBoard's own `editTask`/`viewTask` would be for a task you don't own). Forcing byte-identical reuse without the flag would have silently over-scoped what Project's board can do; the flag keeps the visual richness (priority chip, labels, checklist progress ring, deadline, assignee, reply/attachment counts) and drag-and-drop identical on both.
- **The drag-and-drop and staged-checklist/label JS also moved to shared mixins**, for the identical reason the PHP did: `resources/js/components/alpine/mixins/kanbanDrag.js` (`dragTask`, `col()`, `handleDragStart`/`handleDragEnd`/`handleDragOver`/`handleDrop`/`handleCardDrop` — calls `$wire.updateTaskStatus`/`$wire.reorderTask` by fixed name, so both `TaskBoard\Main` and `Project\Main` must expose methods with those exact names) and `resources/js/components/alpine/mixins/taskForm.js` (`formReady`/`formTab`/`syncFormArrays()`/the checklist-and-label staging state from the `$wire.set(path, value, false)` rule above). **This is not optional polish** — the very first version of this feature shipped `taskboard/form.blade.php` inside `project.blade.php` without also moving these Alpine properties/methods into `project.js`, and the modal rendered nothing but its Save/Cancel buttons, because `syncFormArrays()` didn't exist on `project()`'s Alpine scope and the `x-effect` that sets `formReady`/`formTab` threw and silently aborted. **Whenever a Blade partial that depends on specific Alpine state/methods gets `@include`d into a second host component, grep that partial for every bare (non-`$wire`) Alpine reference first and confirm the host's own `x-data` factory provides all of them — reusing the PHP side alone is not sufficient.**
- Reference: `app/Traits/ManagesTaskModal.php`, `App\Livewire\Dashboard\TaskBoard\Main` / `App\Livewire\Dashboard\Project\Main`, `resources/views/livewire/dashboard/tasking/`, `resources/js/components/alpine/mixins/{kanbanDrag,taskForm}.js`.

### Cardinal rule — object spread (`{ ...factory() }` or `{ a, ...mixin() }`) evaluates getters immediately; never spread anything that has one

This is a single root cause that broke three separate features this session, in two different-looking ways, before the pattern was recognized as one thing:

1. **A spread-merged factory call inside an `x-data` literal.** `x-data="{ value: @entangle(...), _names: @js(...), ...projectComposer() }"` — `projectComposer()` returns an object with `get filtered()`/`get candidates()`/`get willNotify()`/`get counterTone()`. Spreading it into the surrounding `{ }` literal reads each getter's CURRENT value via `[[Get]]` (with `this` bound to the freshly-constructed `projectComposer()` object, at a moment when `_names`/`value` are still empty) and writes that snapshot in as a plain, dead data property on the final object. Project's activity-tab @mention dropdown was permanently empty because of this — `filtered` was frozen at `[]` forever. **Fix used:** stop spreading; call the factory directly as the element's own `x-data`, passing `value`/`names` in as constructor arguments instead of merging them via an outer literal (`x-data="projectComposer(@entangle(...).live, @js(...))"`). Direct `x-data="fn(...)"` never spreads — Alpine uses the returned object as-is, so its getters stay live.
2. **A spread-merged shared mixin with getters, inside a large host object literal.** `{ ...maximizeMixin(), ...kanbanDragMixin(), ...taskFormMixin(), someOtherProp: … }` inside both `taskboard()` and `project()` — `taskFormMixin()` has six getters (`labelsCount`, `checklistBadge`, `actionBadge`, …), and `get actionBadge()` reads `this.$wire.form.attachments`. Spread evaluates that getter immediately with `this` bound to the bare mixin object — which has no `$wire` — so it **threw a `TypeError` synchronously inside the `taskboard()`/`project()` factory function itself**, before Alpine ever got a working component back. That doesn't just freeze one badge — it kills the ENTIRE component's `x-data` initialization, so every directive on the page goes dead: clicking anything does nothing, and any `x-show` with no other visibility rule (like the drag-drop placeholder, `x-show="dragTask"` with no `x-cloak`/inline `display:none`) renders permanently instead of conditionally. This is strictly worse than case 1 (which degrades one feature) — a getter that touches `this.$wire`/`this.$el`/anything Alpine-injected, spread into a plain literal, **crashes the whole page**, not just the feature it belongs to. **Fix used:** when the mixin must merge into a big literal (unlike case 1, direct-call isn't an option because other properties need to sit alongside it), don't spread it — copy its property descriptors instead, which preserves accessors as live getters: `return Object.defineProperties(host, Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptors(taskFormMixin()));` (build `host` as the literal first, `...` the getter-free mixins into it normally, then apply this as the final step for the ONE mixin that has getters).
3. **Net rule, going forward:** before writing `...anything()` inside ANY object literal (an `x-data="{ ... }"` attribute OR a factory function's own `return { ...mixin() }` body), open that `anything`'s source and grep for `get `. Zero getters → spread is completely safe. Any getter → do NOT spread it: either (a) make it the component's sole `x-data` value called directly with needed inputs as constructor args (case 1's fix — cleanest when nothing else needs to share that literal), or (b) merge it via `Object.defineProperties(target, Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptors(mixinCall()))` as the last step after building the rest of the object normally (case 2's fix — needed when other properties/mixins must sit in the same literal). This is not a one-off gotcha to patch reactively — grep every existing and future Alpine mixin/composer for `get ` before it is ever spread anywhere, because a spread-getter crash reads, at the surface, like a completely unrelated feature being "randomly" broken (a sidebar click doing nothing has no obvious connection to a checklist-badge getter three files away).

### Two sibling `#[Computed]`s over the same source rows — extract a shared `protected #[Computed]`, never let each issue its own query

Whenever two *public* `#[Computed]` methods need overlapping rows shaped differently for two different consumers, the fix is the same one used three times in this codebase now (Channel's `mentionMemberMap`/`mentionMemberPresence`, then Project's copy of the same pair, then Project's `projectMembers`/`mentionCandidates`): pull the shared row-fetch into its own `protected #[Computed]` that neither view nor JS ever references directly, and have every public consumer derive from that cached collection in PHP instead of re-querying.

- **`channelMembersForMentions()`** (`Channel\Main` and `Project\Main`, identical shape) — one `memberUsers()->select('users.id','users.name','users.presence')->get()`, consumed by both `mentionMemberMap()` (name → ids) and `mentionMemberPresence()` (name → presence label/class). Before this, `mentionMemberPresence()` was written as its own `#[Computed]` re-running the exact same query `mentionMemberMap()` already ran — caught by a direct "don't hit the DB twice for the same rows" instruction, not by a test.
- **`projectMemberIds()` / `projectMemberUsers()`** (`Project\Main`) — `projectMembers()` (header roster: id/name/avatar/presence/occasion/org_title) and `mentionCandidates()` (activity/chat @mention pool: filtered to `status = 'active'`) used to each run their own `User::whereIn('id', $ids)->...->get()` against the same member-ID set. `projectMemberUsers()` now runs that query ONCE (unrestricted columns, `with('profile')`, matching the pickier consumer's needs), and `mentionCandidates()` filters the already-fetched collection down to active users in PHP (`->where('status', 'active')`) instead of adding an `active()` scope to a second query — same filtering semantics, zero extra queries. Both `projectMembers()` (header, every tab) and `mentionCandidates()` (activity tab's mention picker) render on most page loads, so this is a real per-request query, not a rare-path one.
- **Why a `protected` computed, not a private method or a plain property assignment:** `#[Computed]` methods are per-request memoized by Livewire regardless of visibility, and marking the shared one `protected` (rather than `public`) signals "internal wiring, not a view/JS API" — it can never be called from Blade (`$this->channelMembersForMentions` in a template would throw, same as calling a protected method directly from outside the class) or entangled into Alpine, which is exactly right since it exists purely to be reused by sibling public computeds.
- **Invalidation:** every new shared/derived computed must be added to whatever `unset(...)` call already resets its siblings when the underlying scope changes (project switch, channel switch) — e.g. `Project\Main::selectProject()`'s `unset($this->projectMembers, $this->projectMemberIds, $this->projectMemberUsers, $this->mentionCandidates, ...)`. Missing one here doesn't crash anything — it just risks a stale-from-the-previous-project value surviving if something touches it before the unset runs, so add it defensively even if nothing currently would exploit the gap.
- **Verifying the dedup actually happened:** a test that calls both public methods back-to-back on the same component instance and checks the query log usually asserts nothing, because Livewire's own full-page `render()` (which every `->call()` in `Livewire::test()` performs) already evaluates every `#[Computed]` the Blade view touches — by the time you manually call the methods afterward, they're already cached from that render and cost zero queries regardless of whether the dedup fix is even in place. Flush and enable the query log **before** the `->call()` that triggers the render, then assert on the query count produced by that one render — not on two manual method calls made afterward.

### Per-feed regex precompute — when a stateless service builds an expensive derivation inside a per-row loop, lift it into a one-shot context object

`Project\Main::activityFeed()` renders each comment row through `MentionResolver`, which builds a `preg` pattern from the participant name set (`preg_quote` + length-desc sort + implode) for `mentionedUsers()` **and** a second escaped-name pattern for `render()`. Called per row, that is O(N×P) pattern construction on a feed re-derived on every poll that sees a heartbeat version change (`refreshActivity()` → `unset($this->activityFeed)`). The participant set is identical for every row in one feed build, so building it N times is pure waste.

- **Fix shape — `App\Services\ProjectTask\MentionContext`:** a small value-object the caller constructs ONCE per feed via `(new MentionResolver())->context($participants)`, which precompiles `$byName`, the raw-name `detectPattern` (for `mentionedUsers` on the raw body), and the escaped-name `highlightPattern` (for `render` on the `e()`-escaped body). `MentionContext::mentionedUsers($body)` and `render($body, $mentioned)` reuse those patterns with zero per-call construction. `render` keeps the empty-`$mentioned` short-circuit (returns `e($body)` without a regex scan) so mention-free rows stay cheap.
- **`MentionResolver` keeps its old `mentionedUsers($body, $participants)` / `highlight($body, $participants)` / `render($body, $mentioned)` signatures as thin delegating wrappers** (each builds a one-shot `MentionContext` and forwards) so existing callers and `MentionResolverTest` keep passing unchanged — the hot path (`activityFeed`) is the only caller switched to hold a single `$mentionContext` across the `->map()`. A parity test (`MentionContextTest`) asserts `context()->highlight($body)` === `resolver->highlight($body, $participants)` on a malicious-name body, locking the refactor behavior-equivalent.
- **General rule:** when a stateless service's method builds a derivation from an input that is fixed across a loop's iterations, and that method is called inside the loop, don't duplicate the work N times — either precompute outside the loop and pass it in, or expose a context/factory object that holds the precomputed state. Same spirit as the `#[Computed]` dedup above: identify the constant input, compute it once. The lever is one new small class + one call-site change, not a per-consumer rewrite.
- **Kanban query collapse on the same poll hot path:** `Project\Main::loadKanbanBoard()` ran 6 queries (1 per-status count + 4 per-column selects + 1 relations load) on every kanban-tab refresh; since no column select carries a `take()`/limit, the 4 selects merge into one `whereIn('status', KANBAN_COLUMNS)` + PHP `groupBy` (preserving the rank-then-created order within each column), and `kanbanTotalCount` is derived from `count()` of each PHP group — killing the separate count query too. 6 → 2 queries. Always verify there's no per-column truncation before dropping a count query — `board-column.blade.php` renders all `$columnTasks` and badges `$totalCount[$column]`, so here count === visible and the count query was redundant.

### Project "تحلیل‌ها" (Analytics) tab — 3 thematic insight tabs, project-wide totals (2026-08-28)

A sixth Project tab, positioned after "گزارش" (Report), scoped strictly to **the active project's own tasks** — no cross-project/global rollup. A 3-way Project/Owner/Collaborator perspective toggle was tried then dropped as confusing, so every chart now shows **project-wide totals only, nothing per-user**. A user-panel Livewire tab (stat tiles + Chart.js), not a Filament admin widget.

**Three tabs replaced the old 2×2 grid.** The original status/priority/department/scheme 4-chart grid was superseded by **Flow** (جریان کار), **Risk** (ریسک و مهلت), **People** (بار افراد), driven by `ProjectAnalyticsPresenter` (`tabs()`/`chartConfig()`/`chartData()`/`kpis()`/`verdicts()`/`matrix()`). The Risk tab carries an HTML priority×status matrix with red "fire" cells (urgent + todo/in-progress) above its charts. Full design, gating thresholds, and empty-state rules live in `projectPattern.md`'s "Project Analytics" section — not duplicated here.

**Server source is `ReportingService::analyticsInsights(int $projectId): array`** — a single project-id arg, no `scope`/`viewerId`, returning `{flow, risk, people, meta}`; `meta.populated` flags which enrichment dims have data. Cycle time / throughput / deadline-adherence derive from **status-change→done `Reply` events** (`JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(payload,'$.to'))='done'`, deduped via `joinSub` on `MAX(created_at)`), not `updated_at`. Cached via `Cache::flexible` keyed on a `ModelCacheVersion` embedding Task+TaskDetail+Reply versions (project-wide, no `viewerId`). The four old `statusBreakdown`/`priorityBreakdown`/`departmentBreakdown`/`schemeCompletion` methods were **dead code with no callers and no tests** after the redesign and have been removed (see `servicePattern.md`).

**JS is decoupled from the global `/analytics` module.** The project tab uses its own `resources/js/components/alpine/data/projectAnalytics.js` Alpine factory (`x-data="projectAnalytics(...)"`, tab mode), NOT the global `analytics` factory — so the `/analytics` HR module can be turned off without breaking the project tab. Both factories import `chartBuilderMixin` (`mixins/chartBuilder.js`) for shared `getCssVar`/`buildOptions`/`buildData`/`resolveColor`; `projectAnalytics` calls `this.buildData(data)` (its datasets use `var(--…)` MD3 tokens), the global `analytics` passes `data` raw (`HrAnalyticsService` emits literal hex). The global factory was renamed `analyticsChart.js` → `analytics.js` to match the `channel`/`contact`/`feed` module-named-factory convention.

Docs: `project/legend.blade.php` (analytics tab rows). Tests: `AnalyticsTest` (flow/risk/people shape, cycle-time, gates, empty state, locked `activeProjectId`, placeholder). Reference: `ReportingService::analyticsInsights`, `ProjectAnalyticsPresenter`, `Project\Analytics::analyticsChartData`, `project/analytics.blade.php`, `projectAnalytics.js`.

## An anonymous Blade component that drops `$attributes` silently eats every caller-passed attribute, including `x-show`

`resources/views/components/ui/decor/chat-pattern.blade.php`'s root `<div>` had a hardcoded `class="absolute inset-0 z-0 pointer-events-none"` and never echoed `{{ $attributes }}` anywhere. Every call site (`project/team-chat.blade.php`, `channel.blade.php`, `contact.blade.php`) passed `<x-ui.decor.chat-pattern x-show="backgroundPattern === 'on'"/>` expecting the background-wallpaper toggle to show/hide the pattern — but `x-show` (and anything else passed on the tag) was silently discarded by Blade's component renderer, since a component's `$attributes` bag is only merged into the output if the template explicitly echoes it. The overlay ended up unconditionally in the DOM with no Alpine binding at all, so the toggle button's own styling reacted correctly (that binding lives directly on the `<button>` in each header) while the actual wallpaper never appeared or disappeared — reading as "the toggle doesn't work" with zero console errors and zero Livewire/Alpine warnings, because nothing ever threw; the attribute just never made it into the rendered HTML. Confirmed via `Blade::render('<x-ui.decor.chat-pattern x-show="..."/>')` in tinker — the compiled output had no `x-show` attribute at all. This is the same class of bug as the two `@entangle`/`@island` component gotchas elsewhere in this file, but simpler and easier to miss: it isn't island-specific, doesn't require any special rendering context, and silently breaks in EVERY context the component is used in, not just a reduced-scope one.

**Rule: every anonymous Blade component (`resources/views/components/**`) whose root element can reasonably receive extra attributes from a caller — `x-show`/`x-cloak`/`class` overrides/`wire:*`/anything — MUST echo `{{ $attributes }}` (or `{{ $attributes->merge([...]) }}` if it also has its own base classes) on that root element.** A component with a genuinely fixed, non-configurable root (rare — check first) can skip it, but the default assumption for any new or existing decorative/wrapper component should be "attributes must pass through," and any component missing it should be treated as a latent bug the moment a caller is seen passing extra attributes into it. Grep for `resources/views/components/**/*.blade.php` files with a single root element and no `$attributes` reference anywhere in the file to find more of these proactively — three call sites (Project/Channel/Contact) were all silently broken by this ONE component, which is exactly the blast radius pattern to watch for.

## `x-ui.hover-popover` — a deliberate exception to the click-toggle convention, plus TaskBoard card popovers + Project report attachments aggregate

**Why a new primitive, not `x-ui.modals.popover`.** Every existing dropdown/popover in this codebase (`x-ui.modals.popover`, DMS's four table popovers, the report-filters dropdowns, the card's own "محول کردن" menu) is click-toggle: `x-data="{ open: false }"`, `@click="open = !open"` on the trigger, `@click.away="open = false"` to dismiss. `x-ui.hover-popover` (`resources/views/components/ui/hover-popover.blade.php`) is the first true-hover primitive in the app — **an explicit, deliberate exception**, not a style drift to "fix" back to click-toggle later. The three use cases below are read-only info previews (attachment list, checklist preview, full chip text) sitting on top of a dense card, where forcing a click just to peek and a second click to dismiss is worse UX than instant hover, but a PWA has no hover on touch — so tap must also work, or a phone user could never open it at all.

**Props/slots:** `alignment` (positioning + margin + transform-origin utility classes bundled together, e.g. `'top-full right-0 mt-2 origin-top-right'` — unlike `x-ui.modals.popover`'s `alignment`, margin is included here rather than hardcoded, since call sites need both `top-full`/`mt-2` and `bottom-full`/`mb-2` depending on where the trigger sits on the card), `surface` (`'elevated'`/`'plain'`, same two-tier shadow/border treatment as `x-ui.modals.popover`), `width` (Tailwind width class, default `w-56`). Slots: `trigger`, `body` (named `body` rather than `x-ui.modals.popover`'s `content` — deliberate, to keep the two components visually distinguishable in a diff and because this one is never a menu of actions, always a passive info body).

**Interaction implementation, and why it isn't three separate handlers on three elements (original, pre-teleport design).** `x-data="{ open: false, closeTimer: null }"` plus `@mouseenter`/`@mouseleave`/`@click.away` all lived on the ONE outer wrapper `<div>` that contained both the trigger and the absolutely-positioned body — not spread across trigger and body separately — because `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` fire based on the pointer entering/leaving the element's full descendant subtree (including absolutely-positioned descendants, regardless of the visual displacement caused by `position: absolute`), so a single pair of handlers on the common ancestor already covered "moving from the trigger into the body." The `@click="open = !open"` toggle, however, is scoped to the **trigger's own wrapper div only**, not the outer element — putting it on the outer wrapper would double-fire on any click landing inside the body (the click bubbles from the body up to the outer wrapper's own toggle handler and flips `open` back open a frame after intending to just follow a link), which is exactly the DMS/`x-ui.modals.popover` menu-item pattern's `@click="open = false"` closing-on-select behavior working against a hover component instead of for it. The mouse-leave delay (`closeTimer = setTimeout(() => open = false, 180)`, cleared on re-entry) exists specifically so the small `mt-2`/`mb-2` gap between trigger and body doesn't cause a flicker-close when the pointer transits it in a straight line. `@click.away="open = false"` on the outer wrapper is the tap-to-close path for touch, where there's no `mouseleave` to fall back on.

**Teleport amendment — the body no longer sits in the DOM where any of the above assumed.** `position: absolute` only works relative to the nearest positioned ancestor, and TaskBoard's column body (`taskboard/column.blade.php`'s `overflow-y-auto` task-list container) clips any absolutely-positioned descendant that renders past its own box — a card near the scrolled bottom (vertical clip) or a narrow card near the column edge (horizontal clip) cut the popover off instead of letting it float past. Fix: the body slot now renders inside `<template x-teleport="body">`, escaping the clipping ancestor entirely, with `position: fixed` and `top`/`left`-or-`right` computed in JS from `$refs.trigger.getBoundingClientRect()` instead of the old `absolute {{ alignment }}` Tailwind classes (meaningless once the body is no longer a positioned descendant). The rect is spread into a plain object (`{ ...el.getBoundingClientRect() }`) before assignment — DOMRect's `top`/`left`/`right`/`bottom` are prototype getters, and Alpine's reactive Proxy calls them with the wrong `this` (the Proxy, not the real DOMRect) if the raw DOMRect is stored directly, throwing `TypeError: Illegal invocation`; spreading reads the getters once against the real object first. `alignment`'s two Tailwind fragments are now handled separately: the `left-0`/`right-0` half is parsed server-side (`str_contains($alignment, 'left-0')`) into a `side` flag baked into `x-data`, translated at render time into `left: pos.left` or `right: innerWidth - pos.right`; the `top-full`/`mt-2` half collapses to a literal `+8` px added to `pos.bottom`. Recomputed once per open (mouseenter or click), matching `x-ui.forms.select`'s existing `pos = $el.getBoundingClientRect()`-on-`@focus` precedent — no scroll/resize tracking, since the popover is too short-lived to need it. Because the body is no longer a DOM descendant of the outer wrapper, the "single ancestor covers hover over both trigger and body" trick above no longer holds: the teleported body now carries its own mirrored `@mouseenter="clearTimeout(closeTimer)"` / `@mouseleave="closeTimer = setTimeout(...)"`, sharing the same `closeTimer`/`open` state because Alpine's `x-teleport` preserves the original component's data scope on the moved node. `@click.away="open = false"` stays on the outer wrapper only (not duplicated onto the teleported body) — same as `x-ui.forms.select`'s teleported dropdown, where clicking inside the teleported content also counts as "away" and closes it; harmless here since the body is always either a navigating `<a>` or inert read-only text, never something that needs to survive a click without closing.

**Root echoes `{{ $attributes->merge(['class' => 'relative inline-flex']) }}`** per the rule directly above this section, even though no current call site passes extra attributes — cheap insurance against the exact `chat-pattern.blade.php` class of bug.

**TaskBoard card wiring (`taskboard/card.blade.php`).** Three triggers: (1) the attachment-count badge in the card header — body lists `task.detail.attachments` (`{path,name,mime,size}`, the canonical writer shape) via the same doc-type-icon-or-image-thumbnail branching `detail-fields.blade.php` already uses, image entries carrying `data-fancybox="task-{id}-attachments"` so the card's gallery and the edit modal's gallery are the same Fancybox group (global delegated `Fancybox.bind("[data-fancybox]", …)` in `menu.js`'s `init()` means no extra JS wiring is needed for new DOM); (2) the checklist `<x-ui.decor.progress-ring>` — body lists `task.detail.checklist` items read-only (`text`/`done`, no editing affordance, matching the "read-only preview" requirement); (3) a `جوابگو` (responsible) chip AND a new `دپارتمان` (department) chip — both existed only partially before this batch (`responsibleUser` had a card chip already; `department` had only a board *filter*, dispatch `TaskBoard\Main::$departmentFilter`, and a *report-tab* label — never a card chip, despite `filament/resources/task/guide/user.blade.php` already documenting one). `$relationsToLoad`'s `detail:` column-list gained `department_id` (one more scalar column on an already-eager-loaded relation, zero new queries); the label is resolved from the pre-existing `$departmentOptions` public property (`ManagesTaskModal::mountManagesTaskModal()`, already reaching `card.blade.php` for free via `@include` scope inheritance, same as `$presenter`/`$staffMembers`). Both chips got `max-w-[100px] truncate` added to their name span (previously unbounded, relying solely on the native `title=` tooltip) with the popover body showing the untruncated string — the `title=` attributes on both chips were removed since the hover-popover now owns that role and a native tooltip stacking on top of a custom one is redundant.

**The header-row `truncate` clipping trap.** The attachment-badge popover's body is `position: absolute`; its containing header `<span class="... truncate">` (bundling `#id • created_at • replies • attachments` into one truncating line) has `overflow: hidden` via the `truncate` utility, which clips ANY absolutely-positioned descendant that visually extends past the span's own box — the popover would render but be invisible/clipped. Fixed by narrowing the `truncate` class to just the `#id • date` segment (wrapped in its own inner `<span class="truncate">`) inside a non-truncating `min-w-0` flex container, with the replies/attachments badges as `shrink-0` siblings — the same visual truncation behavior for the part actually prone to overflowing (a long relative-date string), none of the clipping risk for the popover-bearing badges. **Any new `x-ui.hover-popover` (or other absolute-positioned overlay) placed inside an existing `truncate`/`overflow-hidden` ancestor needs this same check first.**

**Project report-tab attachments aggregate (`project/report-attachments.blade.php`).** New `ReportingService::attachments(User, filters): array` reuses `query()` verbatim (same visibility/filter/search `when()` chain every other report method shares) with a narrowed `->with('detail:id,task_id,attachments')` override (Eloquent merges eager-loads by relation key, so this replaces just the `detail` column list, not the whole `with()` — the same idiom `schemeProgress()` already established for its own `detail:id,task_id,scheme` override), filters to tasks with a non-empty `attachments` array, and reshapes to `[{task_id, task_title, attachments}, …]` — one entry per task, not flattened per-attachment, so the Blade partial can label each attachment group with its originating task. Deliberately NOT capped by the report table's own `$reportLimit`/"بیشتر" pagination — the requirement is every attachment across the full filtered result, independent of how many rows are currently paged into the visible table. New `#[Computed] reportAttachments()` on `Project\Main` follows the exact `reportFilters()`-reuse + `unset()` wiring every other report computed already follows (added to `setReportStatusFilter`/`setReportFilter`/`clearReportFilters`/`refreshReport`/`selectProject`'s unset lists). The Blade partial is a single collapsible section (`x-data="{ open: false }"`, `x-collapse`, the same fold idiom `taskboard/checklist.blade.php` uses) rendering a flat wrapped grid of attachment cards, each with a doc-icon-or-thumbnail (reusing `DmsPresenter::extensionIcon()` — instantiated once in `report.blade.php` alongside the existing `TaskBoardPresenter` instantiation, not re-instantiated per attachment) and a small secondary link back to the owning task (`route('tasks', ['open' => $group['task_id']])`, mirroring the existing report-row title link), with the same `data-fancybox="task-{id}-attachments"` grouping as the card/modal so all three surfaces (card, edit modal, report tab) share one gallery identity per task.

**Report-filters two-row reflow (`project/report-filters.blade.php`).** Pure layout change, no new state: row 1 = the existing status-toggle buttons + the search input (moved to sit beside them, unchanged bindings); row 2 = the 7 filter dropdowns in their own full-width wrapping flex row. No Blade logic, filter data, or `wire:click`/`wire:model` binding changed — only which `<div>` each existing block sits inside.

Docs: `taskboard/legend.blade.php` (hover-to-preview note on chips/attachment-count/checklist-ring), `project/legend.blade.php` (new report-attachments row), `filament/resources/task/guide/user.blade.php` (hover-reveals-full-name on the chips panel, hover-preview on the checklist panel). Tests: `TaskBoardTest` (popover data — `attachments`/`checklist`/`department_id` — present in the hydrated task array), `ProjectTest` (`reportAttachments` respects active filters, reflowed filter markup still binds). Reference: `resources/views/components/ui/hover-popover.blade.php`, `TaskBoard\Main::$relationsToLoad`, `ReportingService::attachments`, `Project\Main::reportAttachments`, `project/report-attachments.blade.php`.

## Tab-content `#[Defer]` remount + hover cache-warm — reusable reference for any future multi-tab module

Two distinct, complementary techniques for a same-page multi-tab component (a `Main` with an `$activeTab` and several tab-scoped sibling components or `#[Computed]` sections), first shipped on `Project` (2026-08-28), also applied to `Energy\Chart`, both retrofittable to any future module with the same shape:

**1. `#[Defer]` + real remount-per-switch, for a tab whose sibling holds no state worth preserving.** Mount the sibling only inside a genuine `@if($activeTab === '<tab>') <livewire:...\<tab> wire:key="<tab>-<scope>" /> @endif` — never an always-rendered block hidden by a CSS/Alpine `x-show`/`hidden` class, which pays every tab's full query cost on every page-open regardless of which tab is active (a real, audited regression `Project`'s siblings had before this fix — see the "Final performance/security audit" and "Reopened, differently" sections of `app/Livewire/Dashboard/Project/projectPattern.md`). Add the class-level `#[Livewire\Attributes\Defer]` attribute (NOT plain `#[Lazy]` — that triggers via `x-intersect`, visibility/scroll-dependent, and tab content is always already in-viewport; `#[Defer]` triggers via `x-init`, immediately after paint, deterministic) and a `placeholder(): View` method returning a lightweight skeleton view matching the real content's rough shape (`animate-pulse` blocks, not a spinner — see any `*-placeholder.blade.php` under `project/` or `energy/` for the visual convention). `wire:key` must include both the tab AND whatever scope the module has (project id, or nothing if the module has none — `Energy\Chart` uses a bare `'energy-chart'` key since `Energy\Main` has no per-context scoping) so switching scope also forces a fresh mount, not just switching tabs. This is `Profile\Main`'s own original, already-shipped pattern (`profile.blade.php`'s `<livewire:dynamic-component :lazy="...">` swap) — not a new invention, just the second and third real adopters. **Before adopting this for a tab, verify it holds no per-switch client state a remount would destroy** (composer draft, in-progress filter selection, scroll/nav position) — if it does, use technique 2 instead, never force a remount onto stateful content to save a query.

**2. The `$activated`-flag hybrid, for the one tab (or few) that DOES hold state worth preserving across switches.** Keep the sibling always-mounted and hidden via CSS as before (no `#[Defer]`, no remount), but gate the expensive part of its Blade template — the part reading its real `#[Computed]` properties — behind a `public bool $activated = false;` property, seeded at `mount()` from a one-time `:initial-active="$activeTab === '<tab>'"` prop (so the module's default landing tab, and any direct deep-link via a `#[Url]`-bound `$activeTab`, still renders correctly with no extra round-trip), and flipped true by a `Livewire.dispatch('project-<tab>-activate')` browser event fired once from `Main::switchTab()` the first time that tab is switched to (mirroring the shape of whatever `-refresh` dispatch bridge the module already has, if any). `Project\TeamChat` is the reference implementation (excluded from technique 1 specifically because its composer holds an in-progress draft/attachments a remount would wipe).

**3.
